# After the demo — Ours (your decision or approval first, then Claude builds; or a live session together)

IDs `POST-B-NNN` are stable: never renumber; add new items at the next free number; delete an item in the same commit that closes it (then `python3 scripts/open_items_counts.py`, and remove the ID from any list in `README.md` and `decisions-and-strategy.md`). Read `README.md` first for the conventions (priority, effort, UNSURE, id namespaces).

## Demo and onboarding

- **POST-B-001** · `demo-and-onboarding` · normal · weeks · live session — **UAT catalogue and five-role sign-off have never been executed.** docs/PHASE_5_2_WS2_UAT_PLAN.md defines scenarios S1-S5, P1-P3, CS1-2, CO1-3, PA1-3, OP1-2, B1-2, R1-3 and PR1-2, isolation cases I1-I6 (a Firm-A Partner cannot touch Firm-B ids; an assigned-book Manager cannot see another's client; a portal contact is limited to its client), a per-role matrix and sign-off sheets for Partner, Manager, Executive, Reviewer and Portal client, to be run under USE_USER_JWT both false and true. Nothing in the repo records a run or a signature, and WS4's 'GO WITH CONDITIONS' made this a go-live condition. Blockers: it assumed a staging environment while render.yaml declares one service (practicesync-api), so run it on the production demo firm or create staging; it needs a second firm with a Partner (scripts/seed_uat.py was never written; apps/api/scripts/seed_demo_firm.py seeds one firm via the API); and the 2026-06-21 expectations must be re-derived from core/permissions.PERMISSIONS and the per-person grid (migration 403) first. Live cross-firm negative probes against the deployed API belong in the same run, and docs/BETA_OPERATIONS.md section 5 also asks for a scripted UAT. — _Sources:_ docs/PHASE_5_2_WS2_UAT_PLAN.md sections 3, 4, 7, 8, 10; docs/PHASE_5_2_WS4_RELEASE_READINESS.md section 3 conditions 1-2; docs/PHASE_5_1_SECURITY_REMEDIATION_REPORT.md section 4 last bullet — _Refs:_ WS2-UAT, WS4-condition-2
- **POST-B-002** · `demo-and-onboarding` · low · days — **Decide a public read-only sample-practice sandbox.** The demo fixture (Sharma and Associates: 315 invoices, 200 bills, 9 bank accounts, 12 employees, 24 payroll runs; FY 2025-26 by default) and scripts/seed_demo_firm.py exist, but no path loads sample data for a prospect (nothing in apps/web). DECISION: do you want a public or invite-link sandbox on its own firm with a nightly reset, linked from /demo as 'explore with sample data', plus 'load the sample practice' for new trial firms? Claude recommends yes, on a dedicated sandbox firm kept warm, which needs the always-on host (ops-01, the paid Render tier, owner-parked until after the demo). Seeding the demo firm for the live CA demo is the separate pre-demo step and is the prerequisite for any demo on seeded data. _When: The public self-serve sandbox is post-demo; it also depends on the owner-parked always-on host._ — _Sources:_ market_and_trust-15 Load the demo practice into a read-only sandbox a prospect can open without signing up — _Refs:_ rm/market_and_trust-15, D7, rm/ops-01
- **POST-B-003** · `demo-and-onboarding` · low · hours — **First-run checklist: should the practice's own fee invoices count?.** The first_invoice step ignores fee_invoices (the practice's own billing), so a pure-billing firm that never raises a client invoice sees that step stay open; this was deliberately not done, and sample data or demo-practice loading is also deliberately not offered by the card. DECISION: count fee invoices as a tick or keep the step about client invoices. Recommendation: leave as is until a pure-billing pilot firm exists. — _Sources:_ /home/user/caflow-ai/CLAUDE.md line 5445; apps/api/domain/onboarding/first_run.py — _Refs:_ rm/market_and_trust-16

## Security, access and RLS

- **POST-B-004** · `security-access-rls` · high · days — **Decide who may read payroll in the database; add role tiers.** The API requires Manager or above to read or write payroll and a Partner to finalise (core/permissions.py), but payroll_employees, payroll_runs and payroll_slips have only firm scope plus client assignment in RLS: no role tier and no rule stopping a change to a released run, and payroll_slips has no client_id or assignment scope at all. So an assigned Executive or Reviewer can read (and, per the matrix, write) employee PAN, UAN, bank account and salary straight over PostgREST (24 cells over 3 tables; a Reviewer's read and delete of payroll employees were reproduced; matrix class payroll-tier-not-in-rls). DECISION 4 (payroll read rule, shared with the two-factor rule): who may read salary, PAN and bank data. Claude recommends mirroring core/permissions (Manager and above read and write, Partner finalises): RESTRICTIVE per-command policies using my_permission('payroll', ..., 'Manager'), slips scoped through payroll_runs.client_id, keeping migration 262/323's 'caller's own record' branch so the employee portal still works, plus a trigger refusing changes to payroll_slips and payroll_runs once released; Claude also joins the tables to test_direct_write_tables_are_role_guarded.py, proves it with a real-Postgres test and deletes the DEVIATION_CLASSES entry in the same commit. The migration goes to production on merge. — _Sources:_ /home/user/caflow-ai/CLAUDE.md line 3250 (DEVIATION_CLASSES in apps/api/tests/test_rls_role_by_table_matrix_pg.py lines 870-975); payroll-11 Give the payroll tables a role tier and freeze released runs against direct writes (duplicate of security_privacy-02); security_privacy-02 Give payroll tables a role tier and give payroll_slips an assignment scope — _Refs:_ rm/security_privacy-34, payroll-tier-not-in-rls, rm/payroll-11, rm/security_privacy-02, rm/security_privacy-04
- **POST-B-005** · `security-access-rls` · high · weeks — **Stop Reviewer writes on 41 tables the API gives no write resource.** 41 tables (ai_insights, bank_accounts, bank_transactions, client_sales_invoices, purchase_bills, receipts, gstr1_returns, gstr3b_returns, itr_filings, vendors, year_end_*, ...) carry no write-tier policy, so a Reviewer assigned to the client inserts, updates and deletes rows although no API resource grants a Reviewer write on them (matrix class reviewer-writes-where-no-resource-allows-it, 123 cells; a Reviewer's vendor insert was reproduced). tests/test_direct_write_tables_are_role_guarded.py tracks only what screens write; the matrix is the first to try a write on tables screens only read. DECISION: apply the matrix's own rule R3 (a Reviewer writes only the activity log and their own name) table by table, since each fixing migration goes to production on merge. Claude recommends refusing Reviewer writes on all 41 with RESTRICTIVE per-command policies on my_permission for each table's resource (the migration 260/261/415 pattern). — _Sources:_ /home/user/caflow-ai/CLAUDE.md line 3250 (DEVIATION_CLASSES in apps/api/tests/test_rls_role_by_table_matrix_pg.py lines 870-975) — _Refs:_ rm/security_privacy-34, reviewer-writes-where-no-resource-allows-it
- **POST-B-006** · `security-access-rls` · normal · days — **Add a Manager write tier to vendors and knowledge_articles.** vendors and knowledge_articles have no role policy, so an assigned Executive writes rows whose API guard (client:write, knowledge:write) is Manager and above (matrix class executive-writes-manager-tier, 6 cells over 2 tables); a Reviewer's vendor insert was reproduced. DECISION: confirm the Manager tier on both (Claude recommends mirroring the API guards via my_permission). The knowledge_articles half interacts with the separate Manager-scope question for the knowledge base; then a migration and deletion of the DEVIATION_CLASSES entry. — _Sources:_ /home/user/caflow-ai/CLAUDE.md line 3250 (DEVIATION_CLASSES in apps/api/tests/test_rls_role_by_table_matrix_pg.py lines 870-975) — _Refs:_ rm/security_privacy-34, executive-writes-manager-tier
- **POST-B-007** · `security-access-rls` · normal · weeks — **Approve and commission an independent penetration test.** No VAPT or penetration-test evidence exists in the repo (only pip-audit and pnpm audit ratchets and pinned gitleaks), and DPDP Rule 6 names 'regular security vulnerability assessments' as a discrete obligation with no cadence or owner recorded; a firm's DPO or a corporate client will ask. DECISION 13: approve spend and timing, and the cadence, scope (API, authenticated PostgREST and Storage, RLS matrix, portals, cross-tenant and role escalation) and who performs it. The recalled cost is lakhs of rupees (low confidence), so get three quotes; CERT-In-empanelled auditors exist (recalled); expect 2-6 weeks. Claude recommends commissioning it after the open direct-database-path holes are settled (payroll tier, the eleven RLS classes, MFA scope) so the report is not a list of known holes, with a public summary, a dated fix log and a short attestation letter for buyers; it can also serve the information-systems due-diligence certificate the repo names for an ERI application (unconfirmed). Done when the named third party's report is on file, critical/high findings are closed in a public fix log and the trust page links the summary. — _Sources:_ docs/compliance/06-data-protection-dpdp.md §5 (Rule 6, bullet 4); market_and_trust-21 Commission an independent penetration test and publish the summary (duplicate of security_privacy-39); security_privacy-39 Commission an independent penetration test of the web app, API and database policies — _Refs:_ DPDP Rule 6, rm/market_and_trust-21, rm/security_privacy-39, rm/security_privacy-34
- **POST-B-008** · `security-access-rls` · normal · weeks — **Decide and build read-access logging of personal data (DPDP Rule 6).** audit_log is trigger-written on INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE only (migration 111; the public FAQ says 'it records changes, not views'), nothing records who opened an employee's PAN and salary, downloaded a bank statement or exported a client's documents, and no access-log writer exists in apps/api; the browser reads ~83 tables over PostgREST where no API log exists. DPDP Rule 6 wants access logs retained at least a year (commences 13-05-2027, [S]); the retention floor is met only by accident, since nothing purges audit_log and services/audit_service.py warns any tidy-up to keep a year. DECISION: (a) build an append-only access log written from the API for the sensitive endpoints (payroll employee and slip reads, bank statement and document downloads, exports), blocking UPDATE/DELETE like audit_log, retained 12 months and shown to Partners per client (a migration; complete only for tables moved behind the API), or (b) document reliance on the change log plus Supabase API logs, whose retention depends on a plan not recorded in the repo. Claude recommends (a) for payroll, PAN/UAN and bank data before 13-05-2027. — _Sources:_ docs/compliance/06-data-protection-dpdp.md §5 (Rule 6), §5a ('The one-year log floor is already met'); security_privacy-17 Log reads of personal data, not only writes (DPDP Rule 6 visibility) — _Refs:_ DPDP Rule 6, rm/security_privacy-17
- **POST-B-009** · `security-access-rls` · normal · weeks — **Decide and build the DPDP request, consent and breach pack.** No consents, data_rights_requests or breach_log tables and no per-row purpose tags exist; what exists is the retention position (domain/dpdp/retention.py, wired into employee, party and bank-account deletes via services/party_erasure.py and bank_erasure.py) and docs/compliance/06-data-protection-dpdp.md. Missing: a data-principal request register (access, correction, erasure) with deadlines, grievance-officer contact, client notice and consent templates, a breach register with the 72-hour clock, and processing-record export. DPDP Rules 3, 5-16, 22 and 23 commence 13-05-2027, Consent Manager registration opens 13-11-2026, and payroll data is the highest-exposure surface. DECISION: scope and timing, plus the owner facts only they hold (who the grievance officer is, notice wording; the marketing footer's Privacy link goes to /support because no Privacy Notice exists). Keep it prepare-only and never claim legal compliance. Verify: a logged request shows a due date, closes with the retention engine's refusal or erasure outcome and exports as a report; a test asserts the breach register computes the 72-hour deadline. — _Sources:_ docs/PRACTICESYNC_AUDIT_v1.md section 1 (DPDP Missing) and section 2 (TRD 10.2); see docs/compliance/06-data-protection-dpdp.md; security_privacy-38 Turn the DPDP retention engine into a compliance pack for CA firms — _Refs:_ DPDP, rm/security_privacy-38
- **POST-B-010** · `security-access-rls` · normal · hours — **Decide audit_log read access: drop whole-firm policy audit_log_own_firm?.** Production has two permissive SELECT policies on public.audit_log: audit_log_partner_read (authenticated, Partner only, migration 082) and the undeclared audit_log_own_firm (cmd r, roles PUBLIC) which migration 319's comment says 'lets the whole firm read the audit log'; permissive policies OR, so any firm member with a JWT reads every audit row over PostgREST. Nothing in apps/web reads audit_log from the browser and /api/audit is Partner-only (rbac('accounting','approve') is Partner-only in core/permissions.py) through the service role, so dropping it looks safe; the RLS role matrix runs on a migration-built database and never sees it. DECISION: who may read the log; Claude recommends Partner only (matches the API and migration 082's intent). Claude first reads pg_policies on production (the guards snapshot was captured 13-09-2026 at migration 381 and HEAD is 478), then drops audit_log_own_firm in a migration, refreshes the production-guard snapshot (docs/schema-drift.md) and adds a real-Postgres test: an Executive selects zero rows, a Partner reads them. Alternative: keep firm-wide read for the Companies (Accounts) Rules Rule 3(1) edit log. No demo path exposes it. — _Sources:_ docs/audits/2026-09-03-declaring-what-production-enforces.md 'What is deliberately NOT declared' (audit_log_own_firm); D2 'What remains'; migration 319 comment; security_privacy-18 Restrict who in the firm can read the audit log — _Refs:_ guard-drift audit_log_own_firm, rm/security_privacy-18
- **POST-B-011** · `security-access-rls` · normal · days — **Decide how a suspended or soft-deleted firm is refused at the database.** core/auth.py refuses every user of a suspended or soft-deleted firm, but the SQL helpers get_my_firm_id, get_my_role and get_my_user_id do not ask firms, so a member of a suspended firm still reads over PostgREST; this is the platform administrator's switch and a different decision from migration 468 (a suspended member). DECISION: extend the liveness check in those three helpers to the firm (a migration derived from migration 468 as the last definer) or leave the API as the only refusal. Claude recommends extending the helpers, since they are the one seam every policy already asks. Owner parked until after the demo. _When: owner parked until after the demo (suspended-firm reach)._ — _Sources:_ /home/user/caflow-ai/CLAUDE.md line 3245 — _Refs:_ rm/security_privacy-01, migration 468
- **POST-B-012** · `security-access-rls` · normal · days — **Decide how to scope attendance, leave and payroll-declaration tables by client.** attendance, leave_balances, payroll_it_declarations and payroll_it_declaration_items have no client_id, so migration 084's assignment scope never reached them: every member reads every client's attendance, leave and declarations (payroll:read is Manager+), a Manager writes them for unassigned clients, and attendance and leave_balances also let a Manager delete (matrix classes payroll-children-have-no-client-id, 24 cells over 4 tables, and payroll-children-manager-delete, 2 cells over 2 tables). DECISION: how to scope tables that carry no client_id, and the tier. Claude recommends scoping through the parent employee row in the policy (the way migration 016 joins journal_lines to its parent) rather than adding a column, with the Manager+ tier and no Manager delete, preserving the employee portal's own-record reads (migrations 262/296). Then a migration and deletion of the two DEVIATION_CLASSES entries. — _Sources:_ /home/user/caflow-ai/CLAUDE.md line 3250 (DEVIATION_CLASSES in apps/api/tests/test_rls_role_by_table_matrix_pg.py lines 870-975) — _Refs:_ rm/security_privacy-34, payroll-children-have-no-client-id, payroll-children-manager-delete
- **POST-B-013** · `security-access-rls` · normal · days — **Decide MFA scope: every staff role and the data routers.** mfa_guard is attached only to firms, assignments, identity, practice, billing, payroll and approve/reject (main.py:370-554, routers/approvals.py:58); accounting, banking, GST, TDS, documents, clients and the ITR module (individual assessees) are unguarded, so ledgers and bank data are readable at aal1, and MFA_REQUIRED_ROLES defaults to Partner,Manager (core/security_config.py:149) so Executives and Reviewers are never challenged and a user with no factor signs in at aal1. #642 shipped the enrolment prompt and GET /api/security/mfa-policy. DECISION 4 (the two-factor rule, also the answer to whether DPDP Rule 6's MFA duty is met by today's scope): all staff, or all staff who can see client data. Claude recommends all four staff roles that can read client data, enforced ONCE (a dependency on every staff router or at sign-in) so a new router cannot be forgotten, with a guard test derived from the app routes; docs/compliance/06 section 5c says the role list must be revisited in the same change. Prerequisites: lost-phone recovery (security_privacy-29) and the smoke principal change (security_privacy-28: the CI smoke user signs in by password grant and is always aal1). — _Sources:_ docs/compliance/06-data-protection-dpdp.md §5a, §5c ('The two decisions'), §2; security_privacy-04 Extend MFA from firm-admin and payroll routers to the data routers, and to every staff role at sign-in — _Refs:_ #128, DPDP Rule 6, rm/security_privacy-04
- **POST-B-014** · `security-access-rls` · normal · weeks — **Decide per table whether browser-written shadow tables get an API door.** Six tables are still written straight from the browser with RLS role tiers (migrations 260/261/346) as the only gate, no rbac() and no API audit: fixed_deposits (accounting/loans), tax_audits (income-tax/tax-audit; the backend reads only turnover for Form 3CD, and status/UDIN never syncs with compliance_records), tax_planning_records (lib/data/income-tax.ts), leave_balances (payroll/attendance and the employee portal; no payroll code reads it, payroll uses attendance), client_documents (client-portal, clients/[id]/documents, clients/documents, bypassing the documents table's dedup/audit/OCR) and shared_reports. DECISION per table: add an API door plus audit, or accept RLS-only. client_documents also appears in the document-delete and browser-direct-surface items. — _Sources:_ docs/audits/2026-07-02-executive-product-audit.md R3.9c items 4,6,7,8,9,11 (lines 4009-4046) — _Refs:_ R3.9c-4, R3.9c-6, R3.9c-7, R3.9c-8, R3.9c-9, R3.9c-11
- **POST-B-015** · `security-access-rls` · normal · weeks — **Decide role tiers for the eleven reported RLS deviation classes.** Umbrella for the security_privacy-34 residual: the role-by-table matrix (tests/test_rls_role_by_table_matrix_pg.py, tests/fixtures/rls_expected_access.json, #684) closed its first hole with migration 478 and REPORTS eleven DEVIATION_CLASSES (243 cells over 56 tables, a frozen equality in both directions) as role-tier decisions for the owner; each fixing migration applies to production on merge and deletes its entry in the same commit. The classes, each with its own item: Reviewer writes on 41 tables (123 cells); payroll tier on payroll_employees/runs/slips (24/3); attendance, leave and IT-declaration tables with no client_id (24/4) and Manager delete on two of them (2/2); year_end_notes and year_end_review_events (32/2); cross_client_matches (16/1); fee_engagements and fee_invoices read tier (6/2) and fee_receipts (4/1); client_portal_users contacts and invite_token (4/1); Executive writes on vendors and knowledge_articles (6/2); a suspended member's own users row (2/1); plus the undecided narrowing of the user_client_assignments SELECT. Claude recommends deciding by one rule, mirroring core/permissions per resource, then working the classes in the order payroll, Reviewer writes, portal contacts, the rest. — _Sources:_ security_privacy-34 Generate a role-by-table behavioural RLS test matrix (residual: eleven reported classes) — _Refs:_ rm/security_privacy-34
- **POST-B-016** · `security-access-rls` · normal · days — **Decide whether ~44 client_id tables get assignment-scope policies.** Migration 084's one-shot DO loop gave client_id tables a RESTRICTIVE <table>_assignment_scope policy and has never run again; migration 370 scoped the six browser-read tables (bank_accounts, debit_notes, purchase_credit_notes, sales_debit_notes, gstr2b_reconciliations, tds_lower_deduction_certificates) and about 44 more still lack one, deliberately not fixed because no screen reads them from the browser (the service-role API path relies on core.authz). The durable guard is tests/test_a_table_the_browser_reads_is_assignment_scoped_pg.py; PUR-28's premise was half false because 084 had already covered four Purchases tables. DECISION: leave them unscoped until a screen reads one directly (the stance of both sources), or scope them now. Claude recommends leaving them but settling the question inside the RLS-matrix extension item (a user's JWT reaches any table granted to authenticated, and 234 such tables are uncovered). Do NOT re-run 084's loop: migration 262 replaced the payroll policies with per-command ones so the employee portal can read a payslip, and a portal principal has no users row so can_access_client would deny them their own record. — _Sources:_ /home/user/caflow-ai/CLAUDE.md lines 3003-3017; docs/audits/2026-09-12-the-probe-pass/README.md section 3 PUR-28; CLAUDE.md 'The frontend's second data path' — _Refs:_ migration 084, migration 370, PUR-28
- **POST-B-017** · `security-access-rls` · normal · days — **Move browser document deletes to an API soft-delete with retention rule.** Migration 469 made Storage DELETE Partner-only, but five browser delete handlers still delete blobs and client_documents rows straight over PostgREST. Moving them behind the API needs an API door for client_documents (/api/documents is the separate documents table) and a retention decision beside it. DECISION: what retention rule a deleted client document follows (soft-delete window versus hard delete) and whether to build the door now; what migration 469 changed is only WHO may delete. It overlaps the browser-direct-surface and shadow-tables items, which list client_documents among their tables. — _Sources:_ /home/user/caflow-ai/CLAUDE.md line 3246 — _Refs:_ rm/security_privacy-03, migration 469
- **POST-B-018** · `security-access-rls` · normal · hours — **Publish a security contact and security.txt; rewrite stale SECURITY.md.** There is no root SECURITY.md, no /.well-known/security.txt on either site and no disclosure policy; apps/api/SECURITY.md (the only one) still says the USE_USER_JWT cutover is deferred and service_role is the production model, contradicted by render.yaml and migration 415, in what is a public repository. DECISION 6 (domain and sending domain): a disclosure address on a domain the owner controls and a response target (e.g. a security@ mailbox); the domain and mail-provider choices are owner-parked until after the demo. Claude can rewrite apps/api/SECURITY.md to the current model today with no input. Done when security.txt (Contact, Expires) resolves on web and marketing, a root SECURITY.md exists, and a test fails if SECURITY.md says USE_USER_JWT is deferred. _When: owner parked until after the demo (domain / mail-provider choice)._ — _Sources:_ security_privacy-10 Publish a security contact and rewrite the stale SECURITY.md — _Refs:_ rm/security_privacy-10
- **POST-B-019** · `security-access-rls` · normal · days — **Record encryption at rest; decide column tokenisation of identifiers.** DPDP Rule 6 requires encryption in transit and at rest (or obfuscation, masking, tokenisation). Supabase documents encryption at rest but nothing in the repo cites or checks it (the marketing hosting FAQ stopped saying 'encrypted' for that reason), and no migration encrypts or tokenises PAN, UAN, ESIC number or bank-account columns (migration 336 only redacts them inside audit_log snapshots). Human step: read Supabase's and Render's own documentation and record the citation in the compliance docs. DECISION after that: whether column-level protection of payroll and party identifiers is warranted; recommendation: record the citation first and decide tokenisation only if counsel or a firm's DPA asks for it. — _Sources:_ docs/compliance/06-data-protection-dpdp.md §5 (Rule 6, bullet 1), §5a — _Refs:_ DPDP Rule 6, rm/market_and_trust-09
- **POST-B-020** · `security-access-rls` · normal · days — **Record live Supabase Auth settings; add idle timeout and new-device alerts.** Password policy and leaked-password check, JWT expiry, refresh-token rotation, email confirmation, auth rate limits and network restrictions live in the Supabase dashboard and cannot be seen from the repo; the web app enforces a 10-character minimum client-side only (apps/web/app/settings/security/page.tsx:35), AuthContext has no idle logout (only the keep-awake ping) and login_events (repositories/login_events_repository.py) records admin actions only, not sign-in. OWNER input needed: read the dashboard Authentication settings and hand over an export or screenshots (Claude commits a dated record to docs and sets rotation and a short JWT expiry as the owner decides), and decide the idle-timeout length. Claude then adds the idle sign-out (stopping the keep-alive ping when idle) and a new-device e-mail on sign-in. Mail sending depends on the owner-parked mail-provider choice. — _Sources:_ security_privacy-30 Record the live Supabase Auth settings and add idle timeout and new-device alerts — _Refs:_ rm/security_privacy-30
- **POST-B-021** · `security-access-rls` · normal · days — **Replace plaintext engagement sign tokens with a hashed lookup.** engagements.sign_token is stored in the clear and read by equality in the public sign flow, so any staff member who can read engagements, or anyone with a database read, can read every live signing link. The fix: a sign_token_hash column, lookup by hash, backfill then NULL the plaintext, stop selecting sign_token in apps/web/app/engagements/page.tsx, and return the link only in the create/resend response. It changes behaviour for users: Resend must ROTATE the token (invalidating a link a CA may have pasted into WhatsApp) and 'Copy link' becomes shown once at creation. DECISION: timing, and acceptance of that behaviour change; docs/operations/incident-runbook.md section 5 (line 194) records it as known and not fixed and says that after any database read the owner decides whether to expire open letters. The engagement flow is one a CA is shown in a demo, so it waits for the owner's say-so. _When: owner parked until after the demo (security_privacy-24); the engagement-letter flow is shown to CAs._ — _Sources:_ /home/user/caflow-ai/CLAUDE.md line 3248; docs/operations/incident-runbook.md §5 last paragraph (and CLAUDE.md security_privacy-24) — _Refs:_ rm/security_privacy-24
- **POST-B-022** · `security-access-rls` · normal · hours — **Replace the production Partner login the smoke test uses.** The scheduled smoke workflow (.github/workflows/smoke.yml, every six hours, secrets SMOKE_EMAIL and SMOKE_PASSWORD) signs in to production by password grant as a real Partner (scripts/smoke_api.py lines 149-165), always aal1, so a leaked secret reads everything outside the MFA-guarded routers and it blocks enforcing aal2 in the database (security_privacy-15). OWNER: create a dedicated low-privilege principal in Supabase Auth plus a users row (for example a Reviewer assigned only to SMOKE_CLIENT_ID), replace the two GitHub secrets, then rotate the old Partner password; Claude adjusts the checks that need a higher role. Done when the smoke user cannot reach /api/payroll or accounting write routes, the old credential no longer signs in and the job stays green. — _Sources:_ security_privacy-28 Replace the production Partner login used by the smoke test with a dedicated low-privilege principal — _Refs:_ rm/security_privacy-28
- **POST-B-023** · `security-access-rls` · normal · days — **Require aal2 in the database for payroll and document tables.** MFA is enforced only in FastAPI; no RLS policy reads the JWT aal claim (only migration 089 for platform purge), so an aal1 password session (a phished password) can call PostgREST directly for payroll and document metadata. DECISION 4 follows the MFA-scope decision: add a RESTRICTIVE policy on the payroll tables and document metadata requiring (auth.jwt()->>'aal') = 'aal2' for staff roles in the MFA-required set, staged behind a per-firm or role check so a lock-out is recoverable. Depends on security_privacy-28 (the CI smoke user signs in by password grant and is always aal1, scripts/smoke_api.py ~151-160, so it would break) and on a lost-phone recovery path. Done when a real-Postgres test shows aal1 refused and aal2 allowed on payroll_employees and non-required roles unaffected. — _Sources:_ security_privacy-15 Require aal2 in the database for the most sensitive tables — _Refs:_ rm/security_privacy-15
- **POST-B-024** · `security-access-rls` · normal · days — **Restrict fee tables' reads to the Partner tier (billing:read).** fee_engagements and fee_invoices carry an assignment scope and no read tier, so an assigned Manager, Executive or Reviewer reads the practice's fee economics that core/permissions reserves to a Partner (6 cells over 2 tables); fee_receipts has no client_id and a firm-only select policy, so every member reads every client's fee receipts (4 cells over 1 table). Matrix classes billing-reads-are-partner-only and fee-receipts-have-no-client-id. DECISION: confirm Partner-only reads (Claude recommends it, via my_permission('billing','read','Partner')) and how to scope fee_receipts, which has no client_id; then a migration and deletion of the two DEVIATION_CLASSES entries. — _Sources:_ /home/user/caflow-ai/CLAUDE.md line 3250 (DEVIATION_CLASSES in apps/api/tests/test_rls_role_by_table_matrix_pg.py lines 870-975) — _Refs:_ rm/security_privacy-34, billing-reads-are-partner-only, fee-receipts-have-no-client-id
- **POST-B-025** · `security-access-rls` · normal · days — **Scope cross_client_matches by the caller's clients.** cross_client_matches names two clients and PAN, GSTIN or email values in match_value, and its one policy is firm_id = get_my_firm_id(), so every member reads and writes matches of clients they cannot see although the API's relationship view narrows to the caller's clients (matrix class cross-client-matches-have-no-client-scope, 16 cells over 1 table). DECISION: which matches a member may see (both clients assigned, either, or Partner only) and the write tier. Claude recommends matching the API's narrowing through can_access_client; then a migration and deletion of the DEVIATION_CLASSES entry. — _Sources:_ /home/user/caflow-ai/CLAUDE.md line 3250 (DEVIATION_CLASSES in apps/api/tests/test_rls_role_by_table_matrix_pg.py lines 870-975) — _Refs:_ rm/security_privacy-34, cross-client-matches-have-no-client-scope
- **POST-B-026** · `security-access-rls` · normal · days — **Scope year_end_notes and year_end_review_events to the engagement's client.** Both tables have no client_id and a firm-only policy, so every member reads and writes the notes and review trail of every client's engagement, a Reviewer and an unassigned Executive included (matrix class year-end-children-have-no-client-id, 32 cells over 2 tables). DECISION: how to scope them (they carry no client_id) and the write tier. Claude recommends a policy joining to the parent engagement's client through can_access_client, with the year-end write tier of core/permissions; then a migration and deletion of the DEVIATION_CLASSES entry in the same commit. — _Sources:_ /home/user/caflow-ai/CLAUDE.md line 3250 (DEVIATION_CLASSES in apps/api/tests/test_rls_role_by_table_matrix_pg.py lines 870-975) — _Refs:_ rm/security_privacy-34, year-end-children-have-no-client-id
- **POST-B-027** · `security-access-rls` · normal · days — **Stop staff reading every client's portal contacts and invite_token.** client_portal_users is left out of the assignment scope for the portal principal's sake and nothing scopes the staff side, so every member reads every client's portal contacts, email and invite_token (matrix class portal-contacts-are-not-scoped-for-staff, 4 cells over 1 table); the invite_token read was reproduced on an unmodified schema. DECISION: which staff may read portal contacts and invite tokens (Claude recommends assignment-scoped staff only, and the token column Partner-only or not readable from the browser). The fix must keep the portal principal working while adding a staff-side scope; then a migration and deletion of the DEVIATION_CLASSES entry. — _Sources:_ /home/user/caflow-ai/CLAUDE.md line 3250 (DEVIATION_CLASSES in apps/api/tests/test_rls_role_by_table_matrix_pg.py lines 870-975) — _Refs:_ rm/security_privacy-34, portal-contacts-are-not-scoped-for-staff
- **POST-B-028** · `security-access-rls` · low · hours — **Decide if the 30 s API auth-cache window after suspend is acceptable.** core/auth._get_user_and_firm caches the users+firms lookup per process for 30 s (R3.5e); only user_permission_service pops it, so suspend_user, force_logout and a suspended firm can take up to 30 s per worker to bite on the API path (the database path was closed by migration 468). The July audit asked QA or the owner to confirm the window is acceptable and nothing records that confirmation. DECISION: accept and document, shorten the window, or pop the cache on suspend and force-logout (same-process only, so it only helps a single worker). Claude recommends accepting and documenting while there is one worker. — _Sources:_ docs/audits/2026-07-02-executive-product-audit.md 'What manual QA should focus on' bullet 4 (lines 4591-4595), R3.5e (lines 4339-4349) — _Refs:_ R3.5e
- **POST-B-029** · `security-access-rls` · low · days — **Decide Manager access to unassigned clients' knowledge-base articles.** Batch 6 validated 'Manager = firm-wide' for the knowledge base (2026-06-14); module 9.0/M3 later made Manager assignment-scoped everywhere else (core/authz._FIRMWIDE_ROLES = {Role.PARTNER}), yet services/knowledge_service._FIRMWIDE_ROLES still includes 'manager', migration 079's RLS lets get_my_role() IN ('Partner','Manager') through, migration 084 skipped these two tables, and tests/test_rls_role_by_table_matrix_pg.py:1005 records them as FIRM_WIDE_BY_DESIGN. Client-path endpoints under /api/clients/{id}/... are still assignment-gated; the wider view is GET /api/knowledge/articles/{id} and direct PostgREST. DECISION: keep the knowledge base firm-wide for Managers (document it as an exception to M3) or bring it under the assigned book (change knowledge_service, a migration, the matrix fixture and FIRM_WIDE_BY_DESIGN). — _Sources:_ docs/BATCH_6_COMPLETION_REPORT.md 'Validated decisions implemented'; docs/BATCH_6_DESIGN_REVIEW.md section 6 matrix; verified services/knowledge_service.py:30,49-52, migrations/079_knowledge_rls.sql, core/authz.py:36-42 — _Refs:_ Batch6-manager-scope, M3
- **POST-B-030** · `security-access-rls` · low — **Decide on antivirus scanning and a per-firm storage quota for uploads.** Deliberately not done under SECURITY-PRIVACY-20: a magic-byte check says what a file is shaped like, not that it is harmless, so there is no antivirus or content scanning, and there is no per-firm storage quota. DECISION: reopen either or neither. Options: add a scanning service (a vendor and a recurring cost), add a quota counted from client_documents and the buckets, or leave both until pilots show a need. Claude recommends deferring both until a pilot firm uploads at volume. — _Sources:_ /home/user/caflow-ai/CLAUDE.md line 3241 — _Refs:_ SECURITY-PRIVACY-20
- **POST-B-031** · `security-access-rls` · low — **Decide on eSign/ESP integration; guard against storing DSC private keys.** MCA forms, GST returns for companies/LLPs and TDS statements all end in a signature, and eSign is provided by licensed ESPs under CCA (partner-based; commercials unknown). The external research and docs/compliance/08 agree on one rule: never store users' DSC private keys centrally and support a compliant signing architecture instead. The Track F6 never-do tests (tests/test_the_never_do_list.py) cover portal passwords, PIN, OTP columns, credential storage and government-host transmission but do not name DSC private keys. DECISION: whether to explore an ESP (a commercial question; Claude recommends deferring until filing registrations progress); Claude can add the DSC-private-key guard to the never-do list alone. — _Sources:_ docs/compliance/08-government-api-access-the-verified-position.md B.3 — _Refs:_ F6
- **POST-B-032** · `security-access-rls` · low · days — **Decide rate-limit alert, API-wide rule and CSP-report follow-ups.** Three things were deliberately not done in ops-30 / security_privacy-05. (1) No alert on a public-route limiter refusal: only a log line exists (logger caflow.public_rate_limit: the first ten then every hundredth, naming the bucket and never a path or address); an alert could be added once a monitor or pager exists. (2) No API-wide rate rule: an office sends hundreds of requests a minute, and a figure not measured from the caflow.access lines would lock a practice out on a month-end morning. (3) No CSP violation report endpoint: a report carries the page URL, which holds a client id, and the error tracker's scrubber does not run on a browser's own report, so report-only mode is for a person with DevTools open. DECISION: reopen any of the three. Claude recommends none until real traffic can be measured from the access log (and, for the alert, until a monitor or pager exists). — _Sources:_ docs/operations/edge-protection.md §3.4; /home/user/caflow-ai/CLAUDE.md line 4470; docs/operations/edge-protection.md §3.4, §1.1 — _Refs:_ rm/ops-30, rm/security_privacy-05
- **POST-B-033** · `security-access-rls` · low · months — **Decide timing for SOC 2 Type II or ISO 27001.** PracticeSync holds no certification and claims none (apps/marketing home page comment); Zoho and ClearTax advertise theirs (recalled). It separates a 7 from an 8 against incumbents and is not needed to reach 7: policies, risk register, access reviews, vendor management, change-management evidence and an audit window. DECISION: approve spend and timing; no cost figure exists in the repo or the plan. Claude recommends deferring until after the pilots and the penetration test, then starting readiness after the security items. — _Sources:_ security_privacy-40 Work towards SOC 2 Type II or ISO 27001 certification — _Refs:_ rm/security_privacy-40
- **POST-B-034** · `security-access-rls` · low · hours — **Decide when to raise HSTS beyond 30 days.** HSTS is max-age 30 days with no includeSubDomains and no preload, because a browser told HSTS cannot be told otherwise until it expires. Raising it is one constant in each of three places (the product site's and the marketing site's header generators and the API's security-headers middleware), held equal by tests/test_the_two_sites_send_the_same_security_posture.py. DECISION: when to raise it; Claude recommends waiting until the custom domain is chosen and the console walk is clean. — _Sources:_ docs/operations/edge-protection.md §1.1 — _Refs:_ rm/security_privacy-05
- **POST-B-035** · `security-access-rls` · low · days — **Decide whether leads are assignment-scoped.** leads has firm_id and no client_id, and any firm member can read every lead and lead-stage engagement and the lifecycle dashboard's lead tallies, although migration 059 gave leads an assigned_to column; tests/test_router_client_scope.py EXEMPT still records it as 'an open product question', i.e. an accident of having no owner column rather than a decision. DECISION: should an Executive or Reviewer see only leads assigned to or created by them, or is a shared pipeline intended? Claude recommends assigned-or-created for non-Partners, via assigned_to. — _Sources:_ docs/audits/2026-08-02-bank-module-quickbooks-gap-audit.md 'Follow-up this surfaced: leads have no assignment scope' (~line 482) — _Refs:_ leads scope
- **POST-B-036** · `security-access-rls` · low · days — **Decide whether rate limiters need a shared cross-process store.** The unsigned-request limiter on the payment webhook (core/rate_window.SlidingWindowLimiter: 100 per address per 60 s, 4096 keys) and the AI, public and probe limiters are all per process and in memory: right for the one worker, N times looser for several, forgotten on restart, and nothing stops a flood from many addresses. The protection that matters (an unsigned webhook request writes no audit_log row) holds regardless; a shared store would be a new dependency for abuse damping. DECISION: add a shared (cross-process) store, or not. Running more than one instance depends on the owner-parked paid-host decision. Claude recommends deferring until the API runs more than one instance, and verifying TRUSTED_PROXY_HOPS against the real proxy count first (render.yaml declares it). — _Sources:_ /home/user/caflow-ai/CLAUDE.md line 3239; /home/user/caflow-ai/CLAUDE.md line 4470 — _Refs:_ SECURITY-PRIVACY-23, rm/security_privacy-05, rm/ops-30, rm/security_privacy-25
- **POST-B-037** · `security-access-rls` · low — **Decide whether suspending a member also bans them in Supabase Auth.** Migration 468 made a suspended or force-logged-out member nobody to the database (staff_session_is_live), but Supabase Auth's own ban / sign-out on suspend is not done: the refresh token still mints JWTs (which the database now ignores). A ban is a second system to keep in step, and an unban that fails strands a reactivated member; force_logout still lets the member sign in again, which is what it means. DECISION: keep database-side revocation only, or also call the Supabase admin ban and sign-out. Claude recommends leaving it as is. — _Sources:_ /home/user/caflow-ai/CLAUDE.md line 3245 — _Refs:_ rm/security_privacy-01, migration 468
- **POST-B-038** · `security-access-rls` · low · days — **Decide whether the shared entities register is assignment-scoped.** entities, entity_relationships and entity_to_entity_relationships have no client column by design (sharing powers cross-client match detection), so an Executive assigned to one client can read name, PAN, date of birth and address of every director and beneficial owner the firm records; only the roles list on GET /entities/{id} and cross_client_matches are narrowed, and routers/relationships.py treats it as firm-level. DECISION: narrow the register to entities with a role at an assigned client (breaks cross-client detection unless a firm-wide Partner job does it) or accept it as firm-level data. — _Sources:_ docs/audits/2026-08-02-bank-module-quickbooks-gap-audit.md 'relationships (19 endpoints)' (~line 936) — _Refs:_ entities register scope
- **POST-B-039** · `security-access-rls` · low — **Decide whether to add a CAPTCHA to Supabase Auth sign-in and sign-up.** Sign-in, sign-up and password reset go browser to Supabase, so neither the in-process limiters nor a Cloudflare rule covers them. DECISION: after the limits have been read (separate item), add a CAPTCHA or not. Options: none (rely on Supabase's own limits), or a CAPTCHA, which is a third-party script the owner has already ruled out for the demo form and which would also require widening the CSP. Claude recommends deciding only after reading the limits and seeing whether there is abuse. — _Sources:_ docs/operations/edge-protection.md §3.3 — _Refs:_ rm/ops-30
- **POST-B-040** · `security-access-rls` · low · weeks — **Decide whether to encrypt PAN, bank account and UAN columns.** No field-level encryption exists (no pgcrypto, pgsodium, Fernet or AES in migrations or apps/api), so PAN, bank account numbers, UAN and salary are plaintext columns protected by provider disk encryption (recalled) and RLS; DPDP Rule 6 lists encryption and masking among safeguards. DECISION: whether to build it (weeks: an envelope key in the API environment or a KMS, a blind index for uniqueness and lookup, masked by default in the browser, redesign of reports and search over those columns). Claude recommends deferring until after pilots and first closing the payroll browser read path. Done when a raw SELECT of payroll_employees.bank_account_no shows ciphertext, the API returns plaintext only to authorised roles and duplicate detection and payslip printing still work. — _Sources:_ security_privacy-33 Encrypt the most sensitive columns at the application layer — _Refs:_ rm/security_privacy-33
- **POST-B-041** · `security-access-rls` · low · hours — **Decide whether to narrow the user_client_assignments SELECT to own rows.** Migration 478 restricted writes to a Partner via my_permission('assignment','write','Partner'), but the SELECT policy stays firm-wide because core.authz.assigned_client_ids reads the caller's own rows under their JWT (listed as READABLE_BY_EVERY_MEMBER); no existing row was touched or backfilled. DECISION: keep it firm-wide, or narrow it to the caller's own rows (plus all rows for a Partner). Claude recommends narrowing once every other reader of the table is confirmed to run as the service role or a Partner, so an Executive cannot list who is assigned to which client. — _Sources:_ /home/user/caflow-ai/CLAUDE.md line 3250 — _Refs:_ rm/security_privacy-34, migration 478
- **POST-B-042** · `security-access-rls` · low · weeks — **Decide whether to offer SSO with enforced MFA and IP allowlist.** No SSO code exists and there is no firm-level sign-in policy table; the false pricing claim was already taken off the page in PR #677. Supabase Auth supports SAML on paid plans (recalled, low-medium confidence; confirm plan and price). DECISION: approve spend for the plan that carries SAML and whether to build before any firm asks; Claude recommends deferring until a pilot asks. If built: a tenant-level policy (require SSO or MFA, allowed IP ranges), an admin screen, enforcement in get_current_user; verify with a test firm through its IdP, password login refused and an out-of-range IP getting 403. — _Sources:_ security_privacy-37 Offer SSO (SAML or OIDC) with enforced MFA and an IP allowlist for larger firms — _Refs:_ rm/security_privacy-37
- **POST-B-043** · `security-access-rls` · low · months · UNSURE — **Decide which Practice OS Module 9.2-9.6 designs the owner still wants.** docs/PracticeSync_Master_Reference.docx Part IV (and section 21, the Module 9 delivery roadmap) designs: teams (User -> Team -> Client, team-level assignment), bounded Manager delegation, temporary or expiring assignments (valid_from/valid_until), an access-request -> approval flow, View-As (server-side, time-boxed, read-only, MFA plus reason plus banner), tamper-evident audit, and SaaS seat and subscription management. Code search found no teams table, no View-As and no assignment expiry; per-person permissions (migration 403), maker-checker approvals, session revocation and MFA ARE built. DECISION: which of the remainder the owner still wants and in what order; Claude recommends expiring assignments first as the smallest. — _Sources:_ docs/PracticeSync_Master_Reference.docx Part IV sections 17-21 (text dumped to scratchpad inventory/ref.txt)
- **POST-B-044** · `security-access-rls` · low · months — **Reopen option: remove roles entirely (the 61-policy RLS rewrite)?.** The owner asked in September 2026 to 'remove the role wise access and only keep person wise'. Built instead: the per-person user_permissions grid (migrations 403/415) is the authority and a row wins; the role is kept for three jobs: public.get_my_role() at 61 sites across 32 migrations (the RLS policies guarding the ~83 tables the browser reads over PostgREST, where rbac() never runs), core.authz._FIRMWIDE_ROLES (scope: every client versus assigned book) and the template a new hire's grid is pre-filled from. Rewriting those 61 policies per person touches ~50 tables and its failure mode is a silent cross-client read. docs/audits/questions-for-the-owner.md says 'Say the word if you still want roles gone entirely ... the part I would want to do slowly'; not requested again since. DECISION: reopen or leave closed; Claude recommends leaving closed. — _Sources:_ docs/audits/questions-for-the-owner.md section 2 'Per-person module access' — _Refs:_ migration-403, migration-415
- **POST-B-045** · `security-access-rls` · low · hours — **Reopen stricter audit-log redaction if the position tightens.** Migration 336's audit_redact is a single array constant of identifier column names and deliberately keeps gstin/supplier_gstin, ifsc, bank_account_id and esic_employer_code, and also keeps names, emails and phone numbers (personal data, but how a human reads an audit row at all). DECISION, if counsel or the owner wants a stricter position: names, emails and phones are 'the line to move first' and it is a one-line migration affecting only rows written afterwards (existing rows are immutable). Also standing: services/audit_service.log_event has no redactor because none of its rows carried an identifier; passing a whole row to old_data/new_data would need redaction at the call site or a shared rule with a parity test. — _Sources:_ docs/compliance/06-data-protection-dpdp.md §5a ('DECIDED AND BUILT - migration 336') — _Refs:_ #127, migration 336
- **POST-B-046** · `security-access-rls` · low · days — **Scope task, lifecycle and risk dashboard counts to the caller's clients.** GET /api/tasks/summary/dashboard (domain/task_service.get_dashboard_summary reads every client, open task and compliance record of the firm), GET /api/lifecycle/dashboard (leads, proposals, renewals) and GET /api/risks/stats and /api/risks/firm/score (domain/risk_engine.get_risk_dashboard_stats) return firm totals to an Executive assigned to two clients. They name no client (a cardinality signal and arguably wrong product behaviour); narrowing needs scope parameters threaded through task_service, risk_engine and compliance_record_service (the AI executive dashboard was narrowed under ai-08, these were not). DECISION: scope the counts per caller (Claude recommends it) or accept firm-wide aggregates. — _Sources:_ docs/audits/2026-08-02-bank-module-quickbooks-gap-audit.md 'Recorded, not fixed - the dashboard counts are firm-wide for everyone' (~line 721, also ~2711) — _Refs:_ dashboard counts firm-wide

## Platform, operations, CI and deploy

- **POST-B-047** · `platform-ops-ci-deploy` · high · hours — **Open statutory sources to Claude: allowlist .gov.in hosts or hand-fetch.** Every government host and most publishers are refused at this environment's proxy (re-tested 02-10-2026: curl to incometax.gov.in, tutorial.gst.gov.in and www.gst.gov.in returns CONNECT 403; it is the organisation's network policy, not a gov.in block), so no primary source has been read by any research pass and every statutory figure stays [S] with many domain modules at VERIFIED = False. DECISION: (1) an administrator changes the Claude Code environment's network policy to allowlist incometaxindia.gov.in, incometax.gov.in, egazette.gov.in, cbic.gov.in, indiabudget.gov.in and, for GST and payroll, gst.gov.in, tutorial.gst.gov.in, epfindia.gov.in, esic.gov.in, labour.gov.in, which upgrades every future pass; or (2) a human fetches Finance Acts 2025 and 2026 (First Schedule), Notification 22/2026 with G.S.R. 286(E), Notifications 70/2025 and 85/2026 and Notification 09/2019 and drops the text into docs/compliance/sources (the ITR-schema precedent); or (3) accept [S-gov] for planning and require [P] before shipping a figure into a computation. The research recommends the allowlist; Claude then re-reads the ranked re-verify lists and promotes [S] figures to [P]. Until one of these happens every statutory-reading item stays [S]. — _Sources:_ docs/audits/2026-09-07-market-research/income-tax-tds-primary.md section 11; docs/audits/2026-09-07-market-research/README.md; docs/audits/2026-09-07-market-research/gst-primary.md section 0; docs/audits/2026-09-07-where-we-are-against-the-one-platform-goal.md section 14 and 14.5 (lines 1142-1264) — _Refs:_ MR-META-1
- **POST-B-048** · `platform-ops-ci-deploy` · normal · days — **Alert when the daily sweep is missed; give scheduler status a consumer.** GET /api/scheduler/status (health, stale flag, run history) has no caller in apps/web, .github or scripts, and a missed 06:00 IST sweep (compliance reminders, escalations, recurring invoices) is visible only in logs; run_daily_jobs has no dead-man's-switch ping. DECISION: pick the alert recipients and monitor service (roadmap decision 1; Sentry cron monitors or similar); Claude then builds a ping at the end of run_daily_jobs to an external cron monitor that alerts if none arrives by a set time, and a Partner-visible status line in Settings backed by the endpoint. Related owner step: the external-trigger endpoint POST /api/internal/scheduler/run-pending (ops-15, routers/scheduler_trigger.py) needs SCHEDULER_TRIGGER_TOKEN set in the Render dashboard (openssl rand -hex 32, at least 32 characters) and an external 06:00 IST caller (Render cron job or pg_cron); nothing creates that job. Verify: with the scheduler disabled in staging for a day, an alert arrives by a fixed time and Settings shows 'stale' with the last success date. — _Sources:_ ops-13 Alert when the daily sweep is missed, and give the scheduler status a consumer — _Refs:_ rm/ops-13, rm/ops-15
- **POST-B-049** · `platform-ops-ci-deploy` · normal · days — **Approve a staging copy and rehearse migrations on it.** Merging to main applies migrations to the live Supabase project with no review step (docs/deploy-migrations.md), render.yaml declares one service, and backend-ci's migrations job is a fresh-empty-database apply, so data-dependent failures (NOT NULL or a unique index over existing rows) would surface first on client data; a runbook mentions a staging project only for the table-top exercise and none exists. DECISION (roadmap decision 12; B3): approve a staging copy, either a Supabase branch or a second project restored from a recent production copy with client data masked (doubles as the restore drill), plus a second Render service; cost unsure, current pricing not looked up. Claude recommends approving before the first paying firm and after backups; Claude then adds a staging job that runs pending migrations and the smoke check there before deploy-migrations proceeds, with duration and result posted on the PR. Migrations already apply one transaction per file (ops-16), which narrows but does not remove the case, because production has diverged from migrations before. Verify: a deliberate unique-index-over-duplicates migration fails in staging and never reaches production. — _Sources:_ roadmap engineering-12: Rehearse migrations on a prod-shaped copy before they reach production; ops-17 Stand up a staging environment that mirrors production and rehearse migrations there — _Refs:_ rm/engineering-12, rm/ops-17, rm/engineering-27
- **POST-B-050** · `platform-ops-ci-deploy` · normal · hours — **Choose a pager channel and attach an external uptime monitor to /ready.** DECISION: the monitor product (UptimeRobot, Better Stack or Sentry uptime/cron monitors; free-tier limits recalled at low confidence), the two named people to alert and the channel (email plus SMS or WhatsApp). Today the only alarm is the smoke workflow emailing on failure (cron '17 */6 * * *', measured 4.4 to 9.4 hours between runs), nothing in the runbook's section 7 alerts table wakes anyone at night, and no monitor is attached to /ready (built for this: distinguishes config, database and auth, a five-second cached answer, unauthenticated; ops-05). Claude then configures and documents a 1 to 5 minute check on GET /ready and on the static frontend, and the owner fills the section 7 blanks (who is paged by backend Sentry rules, who gets browser alerts, the nightly screen walk, live-redirects and Supabase alerts). It is also the missing instrument for an availability target and a status page. An every-5-minute ping also keeps a free Render instance awake (750 free instance-hours a month covers one always-on service; searched, medium), so it is the stop-gap for the cold start if the paid-tier decision stays deferred. Verify: stop the API in a test window and an alert reaches both people within 5 minutes, with a recovery message afterwards. _When: owner parked until after the demo (external-ping stop-gap, ops-07)._ — _Sources:_ docs/operations/incident-runbook.md §7; docs/operations/incident-runbook.md §7; docs/operations/service-levels.md §3; ops-07 Add an external uptime monitor with an alert that reaches a person — _Refs:_ rm/ops-29, rm/ops-10, rm/ops-05, rm/ops-12, rm/ops-07
- **POST-B-051** · `platform-ops-ci-deploy` · normal · days — **Complete the breach procedure with counsel: CERT-In and DPDP Rule 7.** DPDP Rule 7 (in force 13-05-2027, [S]) requires notifying the Board without delay on becoming aware, then within 72 hours with an updated description, remedial measures and cause, and notifying affected principals without delay in plain language; the clock starts on awareness and the penalty is up to Rs 200 crore. docs/operations/incident-runbook.md section 5 ('A data exposure') records the awareness time and containment steps, but 'whether and whom to notify' is [FILL IN: counsel], nothing in the file mentions CERT-In's six-hour reporting duty, and there is no CERT-In, Data Protection Board or affected-person notification template and no breach register. DECISION: who may authorise notification (the runbook says owner and counsel, not whoever is on call) and counsel's reading of whether CERT-In's six-hour and 180-day directions bind PracticeSync. Claude then adds the CERT-In step, the three templates, a breach register and a test that required contacts are filled; the table-top exercise is a separate item. — _Sources:_ security_privacy-11 Write and rehearse an incident-response plan with CERT-In and DPDP breach clocks; docs/compliance/06-data-protection-dpdp.md §5 (Rule 7); docs/operations/incident-runbook.md §5 — _Refs:_ rm/security_privacy-11, rm/ops-29, DPDP Rule 7
- **POST-B-052** · `platform-ops-ci-deploy` · normal · hours · UNSURE · live session — **Confirm backend Sentry is on and prove its alert tags.** curl https://practicesync-api.onrender.com/health must say "error_reporting": "on"; "off" means SENTRY_DSN (declared sync:false in render.yaml, so dashboard-only) is unset and every swallowed financial-posting failure reaches the log stream only. - **POST-B-053** · `platform-ops-ci-deploy` · normal · days — **Decide custom domain, brand and sending domain; stage the cutover.** DECISION (roadmap decision 6, market_and_trust-11): choose, buy or confirm a domain and a sending domain, and decide when to cut over; small cost, the owner does DNS and the mailbox. The product serves at caflow-ai.pages.dev (the Cloudflare project practicesync-ai keeps its original pages.dev name and cannot be renamed) and marketing at practicesync.pages.dev, so the URL a CA sees says 'caflow'; sitemap, canonical and OG all use pages.dev; apps/marketing/lib/site.ts holds hello@practicesync.com with nothing in the repo showing that domain or mailbox exists; and the domain/DNS owner is recorded nowhere. Path (apps/marketing/README 'Later'): put the domain on Cloudflare, site at the apex and app at app.<domain> (add the apex to the marketing project and app.<domain> to practicesync-ai), add a Render custom domain with a proxied DNS record for the API, then Claude changes NEXT_PUBLIC_APP_URL, NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_URL, FRONTEND_URL, MARKETING_URL, ALLOWED_ORIGINS, the Supabase auth redirect URLs, the redirect file and NEXT_PUBLIC_API_URL (apps/web/wrangler.toml and next.config.mjs, where scripts/api-url.test.ts fails if they disagree, plus apps/marketing's pair; the security-header builder reads the app, Supabase and error-tracker origins from the build so the CSP follows the variable), sets SPF/DKIM/DMARC for Resend and auth mail, and keeps the caflow naming in code (only the public hostname changes). If Cloudflare fronts the API, TRUSTED_PROXY_HOPS must become 2 in the same change. It is a staged cutover with CORS and auth side effects (sign-in redirects and emailed links change), interacts with D26 (Workers) and with the sending domain, whose mail half moved before the demo as PRE-B-016 on 8 Oct 2026 (buying the domain and verifying it for mail move no URL), and it unlocks the Cloudflare edge rate limits. _When: the hostname cutover stays after the demo; buying the domain and the mail path are PRE-B-016._ — _Sources:_ docs/operations/edge-protection.md §3.2 steps 1 and 3; docs/marketing/2026-09-16-website-redesign-plan.md section 3 context; apps/marketing/README.md 'Later - attaching a custom domain'; apps/marketing/wrangler.toml comments; CLAUDE.md Deployment section; roadmap market_and_trust-11: Put the app and the site on one custom domain under one brand — _Refs:_ rm/ops-30, D26, rm/market_and_trust-11, PRE-B-016
- **POST-B-054** · `platform-ops-ci-deploy` · normal · hours · UNSURE — **Decide custom SMTP for Supabase Auth sign-in and invite mail.** Portal invites, employee invites, sign-up confirmation, password reset and staff invites all use Supabase Auth's mailer: the built-in sender allows about 2 messages an hour and is not for production, custom SMTP starts at 30 an hour until raised (searched, high), and on 27-09-2026 production auth logs showed sign-up failing with over_email_send_rate_limit (429); the invite screen carries the sentence that email only goes to pre-approved addresses until a custom SMTP is set. Only the wording was fixed (PR 642, lib/auth/authErrorMessage.ts); nothing in the repo configures Supabase Auth SMTP and its state cannot be read from the repo, so check the dashboard first because it may already be done. DECISION (decision 1, linked to the domain decision 6): connect Supabase Auth to Resend (the account the app already uses, RESEND_API_KEY) or another provider with a branded sender, raise the limit and set SPF, DKIM and DMARC on a sending domain the practice controls, which is the parked domain decision. Parked, so for the demo avoid sending more than a couple of portal or employee invites in an hour. Verify: ten sign-ups or portal invites in an hour from different external addresses each arrive within a minute, not in spam, with no 429 or pre-approved-address error in the auth log. _When: owner parked until after the demo (SMTP/mail provider and sending domain); demo mitigation is to limit invites to a couple per hour._ — _Sources:_ roadmap market_and_trust-12: Give Supabase Auth a real mail server so any stranger can sign up; ops-20 Send sign-in and invite emails through a real mail service with a verified sender — _Refs:_ rm/market_and_trust-12, rm/ops-20
- **POST-B-055** · `platform-ops-ci-deploy` · normal · days — **Decide D26: move apps/web from Pages to a Worker.** DECISION: approve migrating apps/web from Cloudflare Pages to a Worker with static assets, doing the /clients/<id>/... _placeholder rewrite in about 15 lines of a fetch handler? Today public/_redirects has 138 rules (98 dynamic, first dynamic rule at position 41; the plan's grep -c '200$' metric reads 138 and misleads, use scripts/generate-redirects.test.ts cloudflareDynamicCount); Cloudflare Pages allows 100 dynamic rules and silently drops the rest (this once 404'd every hard reload into a client workspace page, fixed by emitting literal rules first, #640). One more page under a dynamic prefix costs 2 and a new splat group 3 and breaks it, so D10 forbids new dynamic routes under /clients/[id] (new sections must be query parameters); the 41 bare-path rules are load-bearing because a Pages preview does not resolve /clients/x/bank by directory index (owner, 24-09). Workers alone does not lift the cap (same 100-dynamic limit in _redirects); the exit is the rewrite in code, which deletes scripts/generate-redirects.js and D10 as a constraint, with no new subscription (Workers Free 100,000 requests a day, static assets free, not re-checked) and about a day of work. Claude recommends yes, before the next navigation work (THE-PLAN D26). The cutover is the owner's Cloudflare account (apps/web/wrangler.toml is Pages config today: project practicesync-ai serving caflow-ai.pages.dev; which of the two accounts attached to Claude owns it is not verified): verify every route shape, including a hard reload of /clients/<id>/accounting, on a preview URL first, then move the domain; a together-live cutover through the attached Cloudflare connector may be possible and preview hostnames are unreachable from the sandbox. Do not start unasked. _When: owner parked until after the demo (Cloudflare Workers, D26)._ — _Sources:_ /home/user/caflow-ai/CLAUDE.md lines 4403-4442; docs/plan/THE-PLAN.md D26; docs/audits/2026-09-08c-the-phase-plan.md Phase 1 ('routing constraint'); docs/plan/THE-PLAN.md D26; CLAUDE.md Deployment section; docs/audits/2026-09-07-a-plus-roadmap.md section 10 (lines 407-408); CLAUDE.md Deployment bullet; docs/plan/THE-PLAN.md D26 (+4 more) — _Refs:_ D26, D10, T6-a, rm/frontend_ux-27, rm/ops-26

- **POST-B-057** · `platform-ops-ci-deploy` · normal · days — **Decide on an independent off-site copy of database and uploaded documents.** Supabase database backups do not include Storage objects (only metadata rows come back on restore; searched, medium-high), yet about 25 non-test .storage call sites in apps/api keep uploaded documents, engagement PDFs and year-end exports there, and no dump or mirror job exists in .github/workflows. DECISION (roadmap decision 2): approve a scheduled job (GitHub Action or Render cron) that makes an encrypted logical dump and mirrors the Storage bucket to a separate provider account, with retention and a tested restore; the owner supplies the destination account or bucket and credentials as repository secrets; storage cost is unsure (likely small). - **POST-B-058** · `platform-ops-ci-deploy` · normal · hours · UNSURE — **Decide the paid always-on Render plan for the API.** DECISION: approve moving the API (service practicesync-api, Singapore, which must stay there) from Render's free instance to a paid always-on plan? Free instances spin down after 15 idle minutes and a full wake measured 56.55 s, longer than lib/api's 45 s abort that never retries; one gunicorn worker (Dockerfile:36) and render.yaml sets no plan key, so the live plan is visible only in the Render dashboard. Options: Starter (about $7/month, 512 MB) or Standard (about $25/month, about 2 GB recalled), both medium-high confidence and not re-checked, zero-downtime deploys are paid-only; Claude recommends Starter now (THE-PLAN also says yes) and then a load test (ops-23) to decide whether RAM headroom needs more. After a yes Claude deletes .github/workflows/wake-before-scheduler.yml (its header says to) and verifies GET /health after 30 idle minutes answers under 2 s for 7 consecutive days and the smoke workflow stops reporting OVER BUDGET. Fold in the keep-alive question: lib/api/keepAwake.ts keeps a free-tier instance running for as long as anybody is signed in and looking, which is instance-hours and the owner's call if the free tier stays (moot on a paid plan; no cap exists). While parked the stop-gaps are the 'Wake the API' item and the external 5-minute /health ping (ops-07). _When: owner parked until after the demo (Render paid tier)._ — _Sources:_ roadmap frontend_ux-06: Move the API to an always-on host so there is no cold start; ops-01 Move the API from the free Render instance to a paid always-on plan; /home/user/caflow-ai/CLAUDE.md line 4355 (frontend_ux-03/05) (+2 more) — _Refs:_ rm/frontend_ux-06, rm/ops-01, D25, rm/frontend_ux-03, rm/frontend_ux-05, Render-paid
- **POST-B-059** · `platform-ops-ci-deploy` · normal · days · UNSURE — **Decide to deploy code after migrations from the pipeline.** Code and database move on independent tracks: Render deploys the image on its own while the migration job runs about 19 minutes later after both required CI checks, so code needing a column can run before the column exists. Reported: NIC e-invoice production whitelists up to four static IPs (one source says 4-5 days) and ERI production reportedly allows at most four whitelisted Indian static IPs; whether the External-Agency rule actually binds ERIs and whether Indian-origin addresses are required at all is unconfirmed ([U]; asked in Email 1 Q5 and Email 4 Q4 of docs/compliance/08). Branch protection, required reviews, push protection and contributor 2FA cannot be seen from the repo. DECISION: whether every migration merge should wait for an approval click (recommended: GitHub environment 'production' with the owner as required reviewer and SUPABASE_DB_URL moved into it, CODEOWNERS for apps/api/migrations and .github/workflows, least-privilege permissions, SHA-pinned actions); this slows autonomous merges, hence the owner's call, and the GitHub settings are the owner's to create. A Dependabot config exists (engineering-05). — _Sources:_ security_privacy-27 Put change control in front of production migrations and harden CI — _Refs:_ rm/security_privacy-27
- **POST-B-064** · `platform-ops-ci-deploy` · normal · days · live session — **Rehearse a timed restore into a scratch project; write the DR runbook.** A backup nobody has restored is an assumption: docs/operations/incident-runbook.md section 6 says no restore has ever been rehearsed (the 'last restore into a scratch project' cell reads 'never' until a date is written), and DPDP Rule 6 lists business continuity and backup. Needs the owner's Supabase dashboard and likely a paid scratch project, so run it live with Claude after the backups item: restore the latest backup (a scratch project cannot be built from migrations alone, since ten baselined migrations fail on a fresh database, so it must be a restore or clone of production), run the migration ratchet and smoke script against it, record the elapsed time as the real RTO and compare row counts. The runbook must then say who restores, from what, how to re-point SUPABASE_URL and the anon and service keys in Render and Cloudflare, how to check schema_migrations matches, and how to roll back a bad migration (104 *_rollback.sql files exist and apply_migrations.py never runs them; state whether the answer is PITR to just before the migration); repeat quarterly. Read the consequences first: every write after the restore point is lost; an invoice number issued after it exists on a customer's PDF and will be reused (CGST Rule 46(b)); filings rows are lost and with them the period lock they set; restoring to before migration N makes the next push to main re-apply N. Roadmap rule: no real client data before a restore drill exists. — _Sources:_ docs/operations/incident-runbook.md §6; docs/deploy-migrations.md; ops-03 Run a timed restore drill and write the backup and disaster-recovery runbook; docs/compliance/06-data-protection-dpdp.md §5 (Rule 6, bullet 6); docs/operations/incident-runbook.md §6 — _Refs:_ rm/ops-29, rm/ops-03, DPDP Rule 6

- **POST-B-074** · `platform-ops-ci-deploy` · low · weeks — **Decide whether to wire or delete the 218 endpoints no screen calls.** The reachability ratchet (apps/api/tests/test_every_mounted_endpoint_has_a_way_in.py) has TOTAL_BUDGET = 218 of roughly 1,125 route decorators (about one in five; 221 when the roadmap was written; about 856 of 1,069 reached on 25-09) and may only go down. Largest remaining per-prefix budgets: /api/workflows 10, /api/year-end 10, /api/task-recurring 9, /api/tasks 9, /api/banking 8, /api/copilot 6, /api/billing 6, /api/compliance-records 6, /api/payroll 6, then 5 each for income-tax, itr, lifecycle, automation, gst-portal, relationships and sales-invoices. Each is a candidate for the built-correct-and-unreachable defect shape: wire a screen, delete a duplicate, or name a reason in NOT_REACHED_BY_A_SCREEN, lowering the budget in the same commit. DECISION: for each unwired engine wire it or delete it (THE-PLAN Track 1 section A lists them); deletion removes code that still needed security review and tests. Claude recommends deleting nothing before pilots show which engines matter, continuing to wire screens as part of feature work, and deleting only routes with no planned use together with their tests. — _Sources:_ docs/audits/WHERE-WE-STOPPED.md lines 684-704; 2026-09-15-the-unreachable-sweep.md; roadmap engineering-19: Retire or wire the dead API surface instead of only ratcheting it — _Refs:_ reachability-ratchet, rm/engineering-19
- **POST-B-075** · `platform-ops-ci-deploy` · low · hours · live session — **Flip OBJECT_DRIFT_FAILS_HEALTH after reading production's schema_objects.** core/schema_guard.OBJECT_DRIFT_FAILS_HEALTH is False: schema_objects (tables and functions the code calls that the live catalogue lacks) is reported on /health and logged CRITICAL but does not turn /health into a 503, because there is no production snapshot of functions and a 503 over something unmeasured would pull the service. DECISION: a person reads schema_objects on production /health and, finding it empty, flips the one constant (Claude can make that one-line change in the same session). It depends on migration 475's get_public_schema_functions() being live in production; a database without it reports 'functions not checked'. — _Sources:_ /home/user/caflow-ai/CLAUDE.md line 4390 (ops-19); docs/operations/incident-runbook.md §3 /health table; docs/operations/release-and-rollback.md §1 — _Refs:_ rm/ops-19
- **POST-B-076** · `platform-ops-ci-deploy` · low · hours — **Name code owners; add CODEOWNERS and a pull-request template.** No .github/CODEOWNERS and no pull-request template exist. DECISION: who is named code owner for the posting kernel (services/phase2_journal_service.py), apps/api/migrations/, the statutory domain/ modules and core/auth (the roadmap's commit count names dericklobo and gavinlobo730-code as the human committers; this shallow clone shows only dericklobo and Claude). Claude then writes CODEOWNERS and a PR template carrying the repo's commit-message expectations (what was wrong, why that shape, negative control) and the release-and-rollback section 2 pre-merge routine (read a migration as if it runs tonight, say how it would be undone, avoid deadline days), which that document says is not enforced; the PR template can be written without waiting for the owner decision. Verify: a PR touching the kernel auto-requests the owner. The required-check half is a separate item. — _Sources:_ roadmap engineering-26: Add CODEOWNERS, a PR template and make frontend-ci a required check; docs/operations/release-and-rollback.md §2 — _Refs:_ rm/engineering-26, rm/ops-29
- **POST-B-077** · `platform-ops-ci-deploy` · low · hours — **Ratify or change the proposed incident response goals.** DECISION: accept the severity table in docs/operations/incident-runbook.md section 2 as the internal goal: SEV1 15 minutes at any hour, SEV2 2 hours in working hours, SEV3 next working day, each raised one level on a deadline day (7th, 11th, 20th, advance-tax dates, 31 July). They are proposed, not promised to any customer, and the public 'SLA' claim is recorded as unproven (id sla-and-account-manager in apps/api/tests/_marketing_claims.py). Claude's recommendation: ratify as an internal goal only. — _Sources:_ docs/operations/incident-runbook.md §2 — _Refs:_ rm/ops-29
- **POST-B-078** · `platform-ops-ci-deploy` · low · hours — **Ratify the proposed p95 targets for the seven smoke endpoints.** DECISION: accept docs/operations/service-levels.md's proposed targets (Check.target_s in apps/api/scripts/smoke_api.py): health 1 s; identity/permissions and currencies/policy 2 s; clients/obligations, profit-loss, cash-flow and trial-balance 3 s. They are proposed, not ratified; 3 s for a report is only the audit's figure and no screen has been timed with a CA. Never move a target to meet a number. The 30-day timing history takes thirty days to exist (fewer than 20 answered samples is 'not enough samples'); no budget was tightened and no availability target exists because no instrument does. Unverified: the client behind SMOKE_CLIENT_ID may not hold a real ledger (separate item) and the GitHub runner's region is not ours. — _Sources:_ /home/user/caflow-ai/CLAUDE.md line 4466 (ops-12); docs/operations/service-levels.md header, §2, §5 — _Refs:_ rm/ops-12
- **POST-B-079** · `platform-ops-ci-deploy` · low · days — **Share AI rate-limit and budget counters across API workers.** middleware/rate_limit.ai_limit windows and domain/ai/budget_gate caches are in-process: right for one worker, N times looser for several, forgotten on restart. The database total (ai_usage_events) stays the authority for the budget, but bursts across workers are bounded only by the gate's refresh interval (TTL_S, one minute). A shared counter is a new dependency, so it waits on scaling beyond one worker; the other in-process limiters in the API share the same limit. _When: Owner parked until after the demo: only matters if the API is scaled past one worker, which is tied to the parked paid-host decision._ — _Sources:_ CLAUDE.md lines 93-98, 129 — _Refs:_ rm/ai-17, rm/ai-15

## AI

- **POST-B-080** · `ai` · normal · days — **Decide a per-firm and per-client AI off switch and per-call audit row.** No ai_enabled / ai_consent / allow_ai column or setting exists anywhere in apps/api or apps/web (re-checked). ai_usage_events (migration 465) records firm, user, feature, provider, model, tokens, latency and outcome per attempt with deliberately no client or document column, and ai-17 lets a Partner cap monthly use, but neither is an on/off switch nor a per-client, per-document audit_log row. Decision question (decision 5): may each firm and each client switch external AI off, and do new firms start with AI on or off. Claude's recommendation: a firm-level and per-client switch with a clear 403 sentence from extract-invoice and the statement scan, a test asserting it blocks both the Gemini and Groq paths, and an audit_service.log_event row per document call naming user, client, document, model and tokens, no text; days of work. The off switch also belongs in the processor terms (market_and_trust-03). — _Sources:_ roadmap market_and_trust-04: Move AI document extraction to a no-training commercial tier and give each firm an off switch; roadmap ai-14: Tell the CA what leaves the building at every upload, add an AI on/off switch, and log each AI call — _Refs:_ rm/ai-14, rm/market_and_trust-04, rm/market_and_trust-03
- **POST-B-081** · `ai` · normal · days — **Decide DPDP purpose and consent for AI processing and benchmarking.** docs/compliance/06 section 4: s.7(a) would not cover secondary uses at all (training AI on client documents, analytics, benchmarking), which need consent regardless, and the product sends documents and text to Groq and Gemini outside India. Redaction removes PAN/GSTIN shapes but not names typed into a question, and the analytics benchmark (client_period_metrics) exists. The provider data-use terms for the tiers in use are recorded nowhere in the repo.
