Decision: after a human reads Groq's and Google's terms for the tier in use (retention, training; shares its first step with the provider-terms item), the owner decides and documents whether extraction/narration is primary-purpose processing and whether benchmarks need consent, and the Privacy Notice states it. — _Sources:_ docs/compliance/06-data-protection-dpdp.md §4 (last blockquote), §5 (Rule 15 callout) — _Refs:_ DPDP s.6, DPDP s.7(a)
- **POST-B-082** · `ai` · normal · weeks — **Decide whether the assistant gets read-only tools over the books (ai-11).** Tool-calling is not started: no chat call passes tools, /api/assistant has a static statutory prompt plus the hub's counts, and the client-level copilot is a 410. The audit's central AI complaint is that the assistant cannot answer 'what is this client's ITC this month' or cite a voucher; the ai-16 safety design (a document is data, a model reply never triggers a write) is described as mattering more once tools exist. Of the roadmap's four gates, PAN/GSTIN redaction, untrusted-document handling and rate limits plus the monthly budget are built; the remaining gate is ai-13 (paid, no-training provider terms). Decision question (decision 5, 'ai-11 later'): build tool-calling now, later, or record it as out of scope. Claude's recommendation: only after ai-13 is settled, with 3 to 4 assignment-scoped read-only tools over existing services (GSTR-3B position, TDS by section, receivables/payables via outstanding_paise, overdue compliance), every rupee taken from a tool result, record ids for deep links, a numeric-grounding check that rejects a figure absent from tool outputs, redaction of tool results and the per-firm budget applied. It is the core gap against Xero JAX and Zia. _When: Owner parked until after the demo (part of 'which weeks-sized feature next')._ — _Sources:_ CLAUDE.md line 126 (Not done); docs/audits/2026-09-07-where-we-are-against-the-one-platform-goal.md section 7.4 (lines 664-677) and 12.4 (lines 920-929); docs/audits/2026-09-07-a-plus-roadmap.md section 10 (lines 399-402); roadmap ai-11: Give the assistant read-only tools so it answers from the client's books and engines — _Refs:_ rm/ai-11, rm/ai-16
- **POST-B-083** · `ai` · normal · weeks — **Decide whether to build batch invoice upload with a review queue (ai-21).** Extraction is reachable from one screen only (PurchaseBillEditor, one file at a time); POST /api/purchase-bills/from-document exists, always creates a DRAFT, and has no screen caller (re-checked: no web caller). Build: background extraction per file (the 30 s AI timeouts and Render cold starts rule out inline), drafts via from-document, a queue showing confidence, totals-check and near-duplicate warnings per row, a failed vendor match queued as 'pick a vendor' rather than a 422, nothing posted. Decision question: build it now (the roadmap cites decision 9, the portal-upload policy, as gating it, but a CA dropping files needs no portal upload). Claude's recommendation: after the demo, CA-side only first, and only after the provider-terms item (volume to a free-tier provider enlarges that issue). Verify: 20 invoices make 20 draft bills with per-row status, a bad total warns, a duplicate is flagged. _When: Owner parked until after the demo (part of 'which weeks-sized feature next')._ — _Sources:_ roadmap ai-21: Batch invoice upload with a review queue — _Refs:_ rm/ai-21
- **POST-B-084** · `ai` · normal · weeks — **Decide: pseudonymise names sent to AI, or document the residual risk.** domain/ai/redaction.py replaces only PAN- and GSTIN-shaped strings; it does NOT pseudonymise names, and the reversible 'Client A' / 'Vendor 3' layer that rehydrates the reply is unbuilt. A client's name that a CA types into an assistant question or conversation is therefore sent to the provider as typed. The assistant's client brief already carries no name (owner decision 02-10-2026) and the firm copilot sends counts only, so this typed-name path is the remaining leak. Decision question: build the pseudonym layer (a larger design), or accept the residual and state it in the Privacy Notice. Claude's suggested order: state the residual in the notice now, build the layer only if a pilot firm or CA asks. Related: the provider-terms and DPDP purpose items. — _Sources:_ CLAUDE.md lines 55-77 (A tax identifier never leaves for an AI provider); docs/audits/2026-09-07-where-we-are-against-the-one-platform-goal.md section 7.4 (lines 679-681); docs/audits/2026-09-07-a-plus-roadmap.md section 10 (lines 401-402); CLAUDE.md AI-providers section — _Refs:_ rm/ai-03, rm/ai-09, rm/ai-15, rm/security_privacy-12, rm/security_privacy-22
- **POST-B-085** · `ai` · normal · hours · live session — **Read reasoning_tokens evidence, tune AI token allowances, promote the hint grade.** The reasoning_effort: low and json_schema response-format hints are sent only to the model family Groq documents them for and are [S]-graded (egress is refused in the sandbox); a live call read off the rows promotes them. The response allowances 4096 / 2048 / 1200 / 2048 were chosen without knowing whether Groq counts a requested allowance toward its tokens-per-minute estimate, and reasoning_tokens on ai_usage_events rows is the stated evidence to tune them from. The probe sends no schema and no long input, so it does not prove a long invoice fits the response budget: this needs a real long-invoice extraction in production and a read of the rows, after the first probe has been pressed. _When: Follows the first live probe and some real extractions; not needed for a demo._ — _Sources:_ CLAUDE.md lines 122, 128 — _Refs:_ rm/ai-05, rm/ai-06
- **POST-B-086** · `ai` · low · days — **Decide near-limit alerts and finer limits for the AI monthly budget.** ai-17 ships a Partner-set monthly token/page cap per firm (ai_firm_budgets, migration 476) with deliberately no default allowance and no rupee figure. Not built: any alert or email when a limit is near, per-user or per-feature limits, and a cap on the probe itself (probe is exempt via budget.EXEMPT_FEATURES). Owner decides whether any of these are wanted; none has a suggested figure. — _Sources:_ CLAUDE.md line 129 (Deliberately NOT done) — _Refs:_ rm/ai-17
- **POST-B-087** · `ai` · low · weeks — **Decide on natural-language requests that create draft documents (ai-12).** On top of a tool layer (ai-11, not started), add write-intent tools that only ever produce DRAFTS through the existing create paths: a sales invoice from 'invoice Acme 50,000 for March retainer plus GST', a payment reminder, a task. Each returns a preview with the deterministic tax calculation (domain/sales/line_tax) and needs an explicit confirm click, never a batch; nothing posts. The old execute-action stub was already deleted (routers/ai_copilot_v2.py header), so the whole feature remains, plus a guard that no AI-created document has status posted; every call goes through the one gateway door with redaction and rate limits. Decision question: allow a model to initiate documents at all. Claude's recommendation: not before ai-11 and the provider-terms item are settled. Source classed it claude-alone; settled as owner-decides because it needs the ai-11 decision first. _When: Owner parked until after the demo (part of 'which weeks-sized feature next'); depends on ai-11._ — _Sources:_ ai-12 Natural-language requests that create drafts through the existing engines — _Refs:_ rm/ai-12
- **POST-B-088** · `ai` · low · days — **Decide on scheduled AI probes and alerts when a provider fails.** Nothing probes on a schedule (a probe spends tokens and a scheduled call is the product acting unprompted, an explicit owner decision) and nothing alerts when gateway.provider_status becomes 'failing' (the last attempt did not answer). The /health 'ai' word and the Partner screen show the state only to someone looking. Owner question: schedule a daily probe, and send an email or Sentry alert on 'failing', or leave both off. — _Sources:_ CLAUDE.md line 128 (Deliberately NOT done) — _Refs:_ rm/ai-06
- **POST-B-089** · `ai` · low · months — **Decide which differentiation features to build: law copilot, consent vault, more.** July audit Tier 4 and Innovation lists, none started or scheduled: a RAG tax-law copilot with citations (the assistant shows a 'Source:' line but nothing retrieves statute text; see the retrieval item), a DPDP consent vault as a selling feature (domain/dpdp holds retention only; see docs/compliance/06 for the duties and the 13 Nov 2026 Consent Manager date), natural-language practice query across firm data, AI-drafted notice responses with CA approval, narrated MIS on the year-end engine, firm-wide anomaly insights, and a native mobile companion beyond the installable PWA shell (public/manifest.json + sw.js). All need an owner priority call and, for anything sending text to a model, the redaction/rate-limit rules in CLAUDE.md. _When: Owner parked until after the demo (part of 'which weeks-sized feature next')._ — _Sources:_ docs/audits/2026-07-02-executive-product-audit.md Innovation opportunities (lines 161-165) and Tier 4 (line 284) — _Refs:_ Tier4, Phase8
- **POST-B-090** · `ai` · low · hours — **Decide: keep or delete the two memory year-end-report endpoints.** GET /api/memory/year-end-reports and /api/memory/year-end-reports/{client_id}/{financial_year} (routers/memory_intelligence.py, re-checked present) serve the copilot's own stored recollection of year-end summaries; the year-end pack is the live document, so there is no screen by design. They sit on the frozen unreached list with that reason. Either delete them (D21) or leave them named; low value either way. — _Sources:_ docs/plan/THE-PLAN.md - Track 1 A, 1A-2 (frozen list entry) — _Refs:_ 1A-2

## GST returns

- **POST-B-091** · `gst-returns` · high · months — **Attribute each invoice, bill and note to a GST registration.** A client may hold several GSTINs (GST-20, migration 390, client_gst_registrations) but no sales invoice, purchase bill, credit or debit note records which registration it belongs to and every fetch is by client. So a return built 'for' a second GSTIN contains all the client's documents (GAP_RETURN_CAVEAT on GSTR-1, registration_caveat on GSTR-3B, documents_not_split_caveat on every registration in the picker), a client with several registrations keeps ONE GSTR-2B reconciliation per month (a second registration's 2B matches against all bills and REPLACES the first's), and gst_amendment_service still calls gstr1_exceptions for the PRIMARY GSTIN only, so a second registration's amendments are not proposed. Durable fix: a registration column on the document tables (migration, no backfill decided), editor fields, per-registration return builders, 2B reconciliation and amendment reads; this is what retires every caveat. DECISION QUESTION: is a multi-registration client a launch need, and is the registration set explicitly per document or derived from the ship-from state? Recommendation: explicit nullable per-document column defaulting to the primary registration; derivation would be a guess. NOTE: CLAUDE.md calls this 'GST-16' (and one source 'gst-16'); docs/audits/findings-status.json's GST-16 is a different, closed finding. _When: Only affects multi-GSTIN clients, and the caveat is shown on every such return, so nothing is hidden in a demo._ — _Sources:_ CLAUDE.md lines 2048-2050 (gst-06 mentions it) and the GST-04/05 bullet elsewhere; /home/user/caflow-ai/CLAUDE.md lines 4186, 4189, 4197, 5419-5430 (GST-04/05/17, gst-09, gst-10); /home/user/caflow-ai/CLAUDE.md line 5430 (+3 more) — _Refs:_ GST-16, GST-20, GST-04, GST-05, GST-17, rm/gst-09, rm/gst-10, rm/gst-16
- **POST-B-092** · `gst-returns` · normal · weeks — **Build prepare-only IMS decision worksheet (accept, reject, pending per document).** GSTN's Invoice Management System has no table, screen or effect in the product (IMS appears only in the filing-demo GSTR-3B walk-through, which says 'PracticeSync does not act on IMS'); the 2B reconciliation already knows the answer per document (domain/gst/itc_matching.py statuses), and the IMS API is reachable only via a GSP ([S] in docs/compliance/02-gst.md). The reported rule: a supplier's invoice must be accepted, rejected or held before GSTR-2B is cut on the 14th, no action is deemed acceptance, and an action after the draft 2B reaches the return only if 2B is recomputed (operative deadline is the GSTR-3B filing); the start date of mandatory use is reported inconsistently (live 14-10-2024; 01-04-2026 in the platform audit; 01-10-2025 in a blog claim graded [U]), so state no date until read. DECISION QUESTION: build a record-only IMS worksheet beside the reconciliation before any GSP access exists? Design (roadmap gst-22): a prepare-only sheet mapping each reconciliation status to Accept (matched), Pending (missing or disputed bill) or Reject (wrong document) with a stated reason, sorted by supplier, feeding the s.16(2)(aa)/Rule 36(4) pass, never changing any credit claimed; optionally the portal's own layout, which needs the owner to confirm the IMS download/upload formats and supply a sample file committed with provenance. Recommendation: build the record-only sheet after the demo (weeks) and after the pre-demo IMS advisory readings; TallyPrime 5.0 already offers bulk Accept/Reject/Pending. _When: A practising CA will ask about IMS in a demo; it is a gap, not something that misleads._ — _Sources:_ docs/audits/2026-09-07-where-we-are-against-the-one-platform-goal.md section 7.1 (lines 608-616) and 14.3 item 4; docs/audits/2026-09-07-a-plus-roadmap.md section 3 (lines 153-156); docs/audits/2026-09-07-market-research/gst-primary.md section 1; docs/PRACTICESYNC_AUDIT_v1.md sections 1-2 (FR-C-07 'IMS accept/reject/pending missing'); roadmap gst-22: IMS decision sheet: recommended Accept, Pending or Reject per 2B document — _Refs:_ rm/gst-22
- **POST-B-093** · `gst-returns` · normal · weeks — **Build Rule 42 (inputs and input services) ITC reversal working.** Rule 43 (common capital goods) is built (domain/gst/rule_43.py, GET /api/gst-workspace/itc/rule-43, fixed_assets.rule_43_use migration 372) but Rule 42, its inputs-and-input-services twin (apportioning common credit by exempt turnover with the annual true-up), is absent: nothing produces a rule_42 figure although itc_register_service accepts the ground since migration 362, and GSTR-3B 4(B)(1) / GSTR-9 Table 7 cannot be filled for it. The exempt-turnover input already exists via gst_return_service.outward_turnover; statute text is [S]. DECISION QUESTION: build now? Recommendation: build only when a client making both taxable and exempt supplies needs it (scope rule: only when asked by name). — _Sources:_ /home/user/caflow-ai/CLAUDE.md lines 4126-4130; docs/audits/findings-status.json FA-19; docs/audits/2026-09-12-the-probe-pass/fixed-assets.md FA-19 ('Rule 42 is equally absent'); findings-status.json FA-19 — _Refs:_ FA-19
- **POST-B-094** · `gst-returns` · normal · weeks — **Mark supplies made through an e-commerce operator for GSTR-3B 3.1.1.** GSTR-3B Table 3.1.1(i)/(ii) (CGST s.9(5)) is emitted as literal zeros (eco_dtls eco_sup / eco_reg_sup, domain/gst/gstr3b_computer.py ~798-801, written for periods from July 2022; named to the CA in _undeclarable_rows) because nothing marks a supply as made through an operator, and GSTR-1 typ 'OE', Tables 14/15 and supeco are not produced. s.9(5) has two sides (the operator liable instead of the supplier, and the supplier's own turnover through an operator) and a builder must not collapse them. A restaurant or aggregator supplier selling via Swiggy, Zomato or Amazon therefore has a wrong 3.1.1. The GSTR-8 build (migration 421, operator side) does not feed this table; how 3.1.1 relates to GSTR-8 needs confirming first. DECISION QUESTION: does any target client need it, and is the marker per invoice or per customer? Recommendation: build only when a client needs it, as a per-invoice marker (migration) feeding 3.1.1 and GSTR-1; this is GST-25's one unbuilt original limb. — _Sources:_ CLAUDE.md lines 1706-1721 (GST-22 nil rows); docs/audits/findings-status.json GST-25; original finding in docs/audits/2026-09-07-findings/gst-gstr-1-builder-gstr-3b-computer.json; docs/audits/what-to-fetch-for-me.md §5 (GSTR-8, 'how 3B Table 3.1.1 relates'); docs/audits/findings-status.json GST-25 (+1 more) — _Refs:_ GST-22, GST-25
- **POST-B-095** · `gst-returns` · normal · days — **Track the three-year GST filing bar per client and return.** Because the bar rolls monthly from the July 2025 tax period, a practice cannot track it by hand across many clients (the platform audit called it the best argument for an exception engine). Nothing in apps/api/domain/gst or compliance_engine tracks it (itc_time_bar.py is the s.16(4) credit radar, a different thing); the only code is demo copy. Build a per-client, per-registration, per-return list of periods approaching or past the bar on the compliance calendar and hub, worded 'cannot be furnished on the portal' rather than 'time-barred', with the enforcement date as a named [S] constant (VERIFIED False) until the sources-reading item is done and the s.39(11) proviso is understood. DECISION QUESTION: build it? Recommendation: yes after the demo; it is cheap. — _Sources:_ docs/audits/2026-09-07-market-research/gst-primary.md section 5; docs/audits/2026-09-07-where-we-are-against-the-one-platform-goal.md section 14.3 item 2; docs/audits/2026-09-07-where-we-are-against-the-one-platform-goal.md section 14.3 item 2 (lines 1210-1214); docs/audits/2026-09-07-a-plus-roadmap.md section 3 (lines 175-177) — _Refs:_ MR-GST-7b
- **POST-B-096** · `gst-returns` · low · weeks — **Add a GSTR-2B variance-approval (CA sign-off) log.** Two July UX audits list a per-variance CA sign-off log for GSTR-2B reconciliation as a follow-up. Today gst_2b_reconciliation_service writes gstr2a_records and a timeline event for mismatches (log_discrepancies) but nothing records that a CA reviewed, accepted or annotated a missing-in-2B or amount-mismatch row. Needs a table and screen (who, when, decision, note). DECISION QUESTION: should an accepted variance change the Rule 36(4) withholding? Recommendation: no, the log is evidence only. — _Sources:_ docs/audits/2026-07-01-search-sort-filter-ux-audit.md section 4.3 and docs/audits/2026-07-01-search-sort-filter-migration-summary.md follow-up 6
- **POST-B-097** · `gst-returns` · low · weeks — **Build GSTR-1A prepare-only correction of a filed GSTR-1.** From the July 2025 tax period GSTR-3B Tables 3.1/3.2 are auto-populated from GSTR-1 and locked, so the only way to change them before GSTR-3B is filed is GSTR-1A (or IFF). The product has only the filing-demo walk-through; domain/gst/gstr1_3b_tie_out.py names GSTR-1A as 'not held' and cannot see whether one was filed; the form's schema was not obtainable here. DECISION QUESTION: is a prepare-only GSTR-1A builder wanted, or are the Amendments tab plus the tie-out enough? Recommendation: tie-out and Amendments tab are enough until advisory 606 and the GSTR-1A form are read. — _Sources:_ docs/audits/2026-09-07-where-we-are-against-the-one-platform-goal.md section 7.1 (lines 618-620); docs/audits/2026-09-07-a-plus-roadmap.md section 3 (line 160); docs/compliance/02-gst.md section 4
- **POST-B-098** · `gst-returns` · low · weeks — **Build GSTR-9 Tables 10-14, 15, 16 and 18.** gstr9_builder.NOT_BUILT names each unbuilt part and why: 10-14 (previous-FY transactions declared in this year's returns; amendments are tracked per period, not across a year boundary), 15 (demands and refunds, not recorded), 16 (supplies from composition taxpayers, s.143 deemed supply, goods sent on approval; none modelled) and 18 (HSN summary of inward supplies; the requirement moved by notification and could not be read). Table 19 is the separate item on recording the late fee paid. Each needs a data source or a primary document, and the portal auto-populates much of it. DECISION QUESTION: build any of these, given the data gaps? Recommendation: not before the GSTR-9 primary documents (separate item) are fetched; the GSTR-9 filing walk-through exists meanwhile. — _Sources:_ CLAUDE.md lines 1893-1901 (GST-10); docs/audits/findings-status.json GST-10; CLAUDE.md GST-10 paragraph — _Refs:_ GST-10, GST-25
- **POST-B-099** · `gst-returns` · low · days — **Compliance calendar lacks CMP-08, GSTR-4 and GSTR-9C due dates.** Research dates: CMP-08 on the 18th of the month after the quarter (Rule 62, [S-gov]); GSTR-4 annual on 30 June after the FY from FY 2024-25 (was 30 April; proviso to Rule 62 inserted by Notification 12/2024-CT, [S]); GSTR-9C on 31 December with GSTR-9 for aggregate turnover above Rs 5 crore (Rule 80(3), [S-gov]). services/compliance_obligation_service.py gst_obligations takes only filing frequency and state (no registration type) and emits GSTR-1, GSTR-3B, PMT-06 and GSTR-9 for every GST client; GSTR9C is in RETURN_OBLIGATION_TYPES but never generated, and there is no CMP-08 or GSTR-4 obligation although GST-25 built the CMP-08 and GSTR-4 Annual computations. DECISION QUESTION: do composition and 9C obligations belong in the calendar, and with what [S] caveat? Recommendation: yes, after the composition rates and GSTR-9C items are verified, with the registration type passed in. — _Sources:_ docs/audits/2026-09-07-market-research/gst-primary.md section 3a — _Refs:_ MR-GST-18, GST-25
- **POST-B-100** · `gst-returns` · low · days — **Decide how to learn whether a supplier filed GSTR-3B (Rule 37A).** Rule 37A (Notification 26/2022-CT: reverse by 30 November where the supplier has not filed GSTR-3B by 30 September following the availment year) cannot be run because whether the supplier filed is held nowhere: GSTR-2B proves only the GSTR-1, and gstr2a_records.supplier_filed_on is the GSTR-1 date and would report every supplier compliant; supplier_filed_gstr3b is typed null in the browser so a screen cannot fill it in. DECISION QUESTION: a manual per-supplier per-period flag the CA records, or a GSP/GSTN lookup (dependent on filing registrations, D17)? Recommendation: a manual, record-only flag now, and the lookup once a GSP exists. The s.50 interest side is the separate Rule 37A reading item. — _Sources:_ CLAUDE.md lines 2072-2105 (GST-28 second half) — _Refs:_ GST-28, D17
- **POST-B-101** · `gst-returns` · low · days — **Decide whether to build a one-click 'Post this reversal' for ITC reversals.** A one-click journal for a Rule 37, 37A, 42 or 43 ITC reversal is deliberately NOT built: itc_register_service records why and a guard asserts no such button appeared. The CA raises the journal and registers it with the statutory ground, because the amount (interest readings, Rule 37A's unknown supplier status, Rule 43's reading) is often not settled. DECISION QUESTION: reopen it? Recommendation: leave it unbuilt until the open statutory readings for Rules 37 and 37A are settled. — _Sources:_ CLAUDE.md lines 2069-2071 and 2102-2105 (GST-28) — _Refs:_ GST-28
- **POST-B-102** · `gst-returns` · low · months — **Decide whether to build ISD (GSTR-6, Rule 39) and GSTR-7 returns.** No Input Service Distributor invoice is modelled: GSTR-3B Table 4(A)(4) is the only remaining named 4(A) gap and is nil (named in table_4a_gaps, because a nil there says 'we cannot see it', not 'none'), GSTR-9 Table 6G / Rule 39 is refused and named, and domain/gst/registrations.OTHER_RETURN_FORMS refuses the ISD (GSTR-6) and s.51 TDS-deductor (GSTR-7) registration types with a sentence naming the form owed; neither return is built (GSTR-4, CMP-08 and GSTR-8 now are). Per the GST-24 verdict ISD registration became compulsory from 01-04-2025 (Finance Act 2024, Notification 16/2024-CT, [S]), so a client with common input services across registrations has a live obligation. Building it is a separate product: own registration type, return, Rule 39 distribution engine, ISD document type and migration. DECISION QUESTION: do any target clients need ISD or s.51 deduction? Recommendation: build only when a client needs it, ISD first. — _Sources:_ CLAUDE.md lines 1347-1352 (PUR-18); CLAUDE.md lines 1749-1754 (GST-24) and 1886 (GST-10); docs/audits/findings-status.json GST-24, GST-10, GST-20; apps/api/domain/gst/registrations.py (+1 more) — _Refs:_ GST-24, GST-10, GST-20, GST-25
- **POST-B-103** · `gst-returns` · low · days — **Decide whether to key GSTR-9 rows nobody can derive.** GSTR-9 rows that no ledger can derive render as a dash with a note: Table 6B's three-way split (inputs / capital goods / input services) is a judgement about USE (the total is derived, only the apportionment is not; no column records it); 6C against 6D is recorded on vendors.gst_registration_status but not carried by the monthly GSTR-3B payload being consolidated; TRAN-I/TRAN-II and 8C (a fact about the next year's returns) are refused and named. Building would need a use-classification field and carrying the vendor registration status through the 3B payload (Rule 39 / 6G is the ISD item). DECISION QUESTION: should the CA key these in-product? Recommendation: reopen only if the owner wants them; 6B/6C-6D are the only ones with a plausible build. — _Sources:_ CLAUDE.md lines 1880-1892 (GST-10); docs/audits/findings-status.json GST-10 — _Refs:_ GST-10
- **POST-B-104** · `gst-returns` · low · weeks — **Decide whether to model the electronic cash ledger and its balance.** gst-06 (migration 474) chains only the electronic CREDIT ledger opening and closing; the cash ledger and its balance are not modelled, nothing reads the portal (every figure is keyed by a person or carried from a return computed here), no existing return was back-filled (a client's first return after 474 opens with a keyed balance or says it assumed nil) and the opening is not split by registration documents. DECISION QUESTION: model the cash ledger? Recommendation: no until the portal can be read (GSP), since a keyed cash balance would drift. — _Sources:_ CLAUDE.md lines 2045-2051 (gst-06) — _Refs:_ rm/gst-06, GST-16
- **POST-B-105** · `gst-returns` · low · days — **Firm-level cross-client supplier reliability view.** The reconciliation lists unfiled suppliers per client (itc_matching.defaulters) but nothing aggregates across a firm; the same supplier GSTIN often sells to many of a firm's clients (roadmap gst-34). DECISION QUESTION: build a firm-level view from stored 2B rows (gstr2a_records, nothing new fetched) scoped to the caller's assigned clients? A cross-FIRM version would be a data moat but needs owner and DPDP terms review (GSTIN is a business identifier per migration 336's note). Recommendation: firm-level yes after the demo (days), cross-firm no for now. Verify: one supplier unfiled across three clients shows one row with three clients and the credit at risk; an Executive assigned to two clients sees only those two. — _Sources:_ roadmap gst-34: Cross-client supplier reliability scores for the whole firm — _Refs:_ rm/gst-34
- **POST-B-106** · `gst-returns` · low · days — **GSTR-2B probable matches: one-to-one assignment, re-run, reopen.** domain/gst/itc_probable.suggest offers graded pairs (strong or possible, with sentences) but persists nothing and is computed only after an upload. Deliberately not done: one-to-one assignment (picking a winner is the guess it avoids), re-running suggestions from stored rows, and suggestions on a reopened tab. DECISION QUESTION: reopen any of them? Recommendation: no; the CA corrects the bill and re-runs the reconciliation. — _Sources:_ /home/user/caflow-ai/CLAUDE.md line 4190 (gst-12) — _Refs:_ rm/gst-12
- **POST-B-107** · `gst-returns` · low · weeks — **Optional HSN and rate master with cited, effective-dated rates.** The product deliberately ships no shared HSN/rate master (routers/hsn.py header; migrations 179 and 181 'ship no seed data'; public.hsn_master from migration 036 is not read), so when the 22-09-2025 re-rating moved most 12% goods to 5% and most 28% to 18%, every firm had to edit every item by hand. Roadmap gst-25 reverses that recorded decision narrowly. DECISION QUESTION: add an optional effective-dated master where each row cites its notification, used only as a pre-fill hint and a stale-rate warning ('your library says 12%, the notification in force from 22-09-2025 says 5%') and never applied to tax math? A human must enter and check each row against the notifications. Recommendation: yes but narrow, after the demo: a check enforcing a notification reference on every master row, starting with the 22-09-2025 re-rating rows. Verify: an invoice dated after the change shows the warning while the stored rate is unchanged. — _Sources:_ roadmap gst-25: Optional HSN and rate master with notification-cited, dated rates — _Refs:_ rm/gst-25
- **POST-B-108** · `gst-returns` · low · days — **Optionally create the vendor when drafting a bill from a 2B document.** POST /api/purchase-bills/from-2b refuses when no vendor with the supplier GSTIN exists (or two live vendors have it); it creates no vendor and invents no HSN, unit, product or s.17(5) decision, and the receive path is untouched. DECISION QUESTION: should it create the vendor? Recommendation: leave as is; reopen only if a CA wants vendor auto-creation. — _Sources:_ /home/user/caflow-ai/CLAUDE.md line 4191 (gst-13) — _Refs:_ rm/gst-13
- **POST-B-109** · `gst-returns` · low · weeks — **RCM documents: month-end self-invoice, cancelled registration, UQC gaps.** domain/gst/rcm_documents.py (PUR-19, migration 388) records three refusals rather than guesses: no consolidated month-end self-invoice ([S], tied to the withdrawn Notification 8/2017-CT(R), so it needs a statutory reading first); a cancelled supplier registration is not modelled; and Rule 46(h)'s UQC is absent because purchase_bill_lines has no unit column. Whether s.9(4) applies stays the bill's own is_reverse_charge flag, the CA's answer. DECISION QUESTION: does any client need any of the three? Recommendation: reopen only with a client need. — _Sources:_ CLAUDE.md lines 1388-1395 (PUR-19); docs/audits/findings-status.json PUR-19 — _Refs:_ PUR-19
- **POST-B-110** · `gst-returns` · low · days — **Record late fee paid (challan) so GSTR-9 Table 19 can be built.** GSTR-9 Table 19 stays unbuilt: the s.47(2) annual late fee PAYABLE is computed (domain/gst/late_filing._annual_late_fee), but what was PAID is a fact about a challan this product does not record, and a return declaring the fee paid when it has not been is a false declaration. Needs a late-fee challan/payment record (a migration and entry screen); the same absence blocks GSTR-9C's Table 17 'Late Fee Payable and Paid'. DECISION QUESTION: record late-fee challans in-product? Recommendation: yes if GSTR-9/9C tables are to be built, otherwise leave Table 19 named. — _Sources:_ CLAUDE.md lines 1893-1901 and 2197-2201 — _Refs:_ GST-10
- **POST-B-111** · `gst-returns` · low · weeks — **Rule 43 gaps: classification-history table and excise exclusions.** Rule 43's working names but does not model (a) the (a)->(c) and (b)->(c) transitions, where the provisos reduce credit by five percentage points per quarter when a capital good moves between exclusively-taxable, exclusively-exempt and common use (fixed_assets.rule_43_use, migration 372, holds only the current class, so a HISTORY, i.e. a second table and migration, is needed), and (b) the Explanation to Rule 43(1)(g)'s excise-related exclusions from the exempt-turnover figure (a statutory reading plus a place to record excluded amounts). Every answer names both as gaps rather than approximating. DECISION QUESTION: build the history table? Recommendation: defer until a client has an asset that changes use; the Explanation needs a human reading of the Rule first (all [S]). — _Sources:_ /home/user/caflow-ai/CLAUDE.md lines 4126-4129; /home/user/caflow-ai/CLAUDE.md lines 4126-4130; docs/audits/findings-status.json FA-19 (+1 more) — _Refs:_ FA-19

## GST e-invoice and e-way bill

- **POST-B-112** · `gst-einvoice-eway` · normal · weeks — **Build live IRN generation against the NIC sandbox beside the record-only path.** IRN is one of only two outputs software can complete end to end (the IRP signs, no taxpayer signature). Build a NEW endpoint beside routers/einvoice.py POST /records/{id}/irn-generated (which only records an IRN a human obtained), never a repointing: sandbox (self-service, free, any GSTIN) -> test every API in pre-production with minimum success AND failure cases against the real application (not NIC's online tool) -> Test Summary Report -> email support.einv.api@gov.in -> up to four static IPs (one source: 4-5 days) -> production Client Id/Secret (no fee found for the ERP category). Repo prerequisites: no INV-01 payload builder exists (GST-32 refused to build JSON from remembered field meanings; domain/gst/irp_validations.py checks values only; the specs, validations, error codes and a signed sample are committed at docs/compliance/sources/e-invoice/); read the Ship-to GSTIN advisory and the 30-day reporting rule first; TLS 1.2 minimum; idempotency with the reference written before the call (never a blind retry); explicit CA confirmation click per invoice; write the record the period lock reads. Blocked on the route decision and sandbox registration items above, and production needs the NIC credentials item plus the India-hosted egress hop. _When: Starts only after the route decision and sandbox credentials (separate items)._ — _Sources:_ docs/compliance/07-getting-permission-to-file.md §3.2, §3.3, §4 wave 0 #2, §6; docs/compliance/08-government-api-access-the-verified-position.md sections 3.4, A.2; docs/audits/2026-09-07-where-we-are-against-the-one-platform-goal.md section 7.3; docs/audits/2026-09-07-a-plus-roadmap.md section 6 (line 285) and section 11 stage 6; docs/compliance/07 section 3.2-3.3 — _Refs:_ GST-32, D17, Track 5
- **POST-B-113** · `gst-einvoice-eway` · normal · weeks — **Build real e-way bill API integration with its own NIC onboarding.** Same NIC family as e-invoice but its own developer portal https://docs.ewaybillgst.gov.in/apidocs/ ([S-gov]): auth is client id/secret plus the taxpayer's GSTIN and API username/password; onboarding runs shortlisting -> pre-production credentials -> testing -> IP whitelisting -> production login. Constraint nobody discovers until onboarding: the taxpayer logs into the EWB system, chooses Registration -> For GSP and selects a GSP from a dropdown, so unless PracticeSync's name is in NIC's list the client cannot select it and in practice it runs under whichever GSP is contracted, whose name the client sees (same on the GST consent dashboard). Turnover tiers: >= Rs 500 crore both direct API and GSP routes, Rs 100-500 crore GSP only. The code seam routers/eway_bill.py is record-only; a real integration is a new endpoint beside it. Blocked on the registrations/GSP route decision; plan the client conversation first, never auto-submit. _When: Blocked on the route and GSP decision._ — _Sources:_ docs/compliance/08-government-api-access-the-verified-position.md section 3.4, A.3 — _Refs:_ Track 5
- **POST-B-114** · `gst-einvoice-eway` · normal · hours — **Decide route and timing for live e-invoice and e-way bill rails.** Decision question: build live IRN and e-way bill generation against the free NIC sandbox now, after the CA demo, or not at all, and for production go direct to NIC as an ERP (plausible per NIC's wording 'GSPs, ERPs and ECOs', eligibility unconfirmed) or through a GSP partner (ClearTax/Taxilla/other, with a GSP-agnostic provider layer as the competitive design's Phase 5 wanted)? Facts: einv-apisandbox.nic.in is self-service at any turnover and e-way bill API enablement is self-service on the EWB portal; the IRP signs, so IRN and EWB are the only two outputs software can complete end to end, but routers/einvoice.py and routers/eway_bill.py today only RECORD what a human obtained, with no bulk generation and no 24-hour IRN cancellation (TODO(compliance) markers in both). Production is direct API only above Rs 500 crore turnover, Rs 100-500 crore GSP-only, and needs an India static-IP egress hop; docs/compliance/08 recorded 'no registrations pursued at this time' but names the sandbox as the first thing to revisit. Recommendation: after the demo, register on the sandbox (separate human-step item) and build against it, which is free and forecloses nothing, and defer the production-route choice until the ERP-category and IRIS reads are done; never auto-submit, one explicit CA click per document. — _Sources:_ docs/compliance/02-gst.md §5, §6; docs/audits/2026-07-05-invoice-workspace-competitive-design.md Pillar 4 / Phase 5; docs/audits/2026-07-05-sales-invoice-ux-workflow-review.md Part 2 item 2; docs/compliance/07-getting-permission-to-file.md — _Refs:_ D17
- **POST-B-115** · `gst-einvoice-eway` · normal · weeks — **Decide whether to build prepare-only IRP INV-01 / e-way JSON.** DECISION (roadmap decision 7, gst-18): reverse GST-32's refusal and build prepare-only INV-01 / IRP bulk-upload JSON that the CA uploads on the IRP themselves (and the e-way bill JSON and IFF/GSTR payload envelopes that the same refusal covers: GST-32, SALES-28, GST-11), never auto-submit, one CA click per invoice, nothing transmitted? GST-32 refused because a wrong field NAME fails visibly at the portal but a misremembered field MEANING generates a real document with wrong figures, but the objection is weakened: domain/gst/irp_validations.py now checks VALUES in named fields against primary sources the owner hand-fetched on 18-09-2026 into docs/compliance/sources/e-invoice/ (notified-schema and eway-bill schema-to-API mappings, the 39 system validations, field regexes, IRP error codes, master codes, a signed sample invoice and QR), so the blocker may now be a decision rather than a missing document. Today domain/income_tax/einvoice_service.generate_irn returns nulls and no builder exists; the IRP's self-service Bulk Generation tool takes a JSON upload (about 2 MB per file, single source) and returns the signed result with no GSP needed. Five things remain named as not held per irp_validations.NOT_HELD (the HSN master behind error 2176, IsServc against the HSN class, the goods-only quantity rule, the payload field expressions, the arithmetic the IRP recomputes). Recommendation: first assess whether the committed sources suffice, then build prepare-only JSON after the demo, with golden-file tests that field meanings match the committed signed sample and pass irp_validations, plus a human step to upload one generated file to the NIC sandbox and confirm it returns IRNs; it pairs with the bulk-response import item, and real submission waits on NIC/GSP credentials (Track 5, a separate commercial step); IRN and e-way are the only two statutory outputs software can complete end to end. — _Sources:_ /home/user/caflow-ai/CLAUDE.md lines 3948-3951; docs/audits/findings-status.json GST-32 needs, SALES-28 user_visible_impact, GST-11 what_is_left; docs/plan/2026-09-24-overnight-run.md Batch 5.1 (lines 205-206); CLAUDE.md GST-32 bullets (+3 more) — _Refs:_ GST-32, SALES-28, GST-11, rm/gst-18, decision 7
- **POST-B-116** · `gst-einvoice-eway` · normal · weeks — **Prepare e-way bill bulk-generation JSON and Part B vehicle update file.** E-way bill is record-only: routers/eway_bill.py records an e-way bill a human generated; validity arithmetic (domain/gst/eway_validity.py) and the expiring-bill panel (eway_expiry.py) exist but generation happens on the NIC portal. Build: prepare the portal's bulk-generation JSON from sales invoices plus the recorded distance, vehicle and transport fields, with golden-file tests against the committed docs/compliance/sources/e-invoice/eway-bill-schema-to-api-mapping.txt and the same refusal to guess a field meaning as GST-32, plus the Part B vehicle update. Resolved as owner-decides because CLAUDE.md records that the IRP and EWB JSON payloads stay refused with GST-32, so this rides on the same decision 7 as the IRP bulk-JSON item (recommendation there: yes after the demo). The bulk file format itself was only 'searched, medium confidence', so a person must upload a generated file to the EWB portal bulk tool or sandbox to prove every row returns a bill number. Prepare-only; nothing is sent. _When: Final proof needs a human to upload to the EWB portal/sandbox._ — _Sources:_ gst-21 Export e-way bill bulk-generation JSON (and Part B vehicle update) — _Refs:_ rm/gst-21, decision 7
- **POST-B-117** · `gst-einvoice-eway` · low · days — **Decide bulk import of the IRP response file to record many IRNs.** Decision question (roadmap decision 7): build bulk IRN import even if the CA generates IRNs in another tool? Today IRNs are recorded one record at a time (POST /records/{id}/irn-generated, routers/einvoice.py ~line 255); no bulk route exists in einvoice.py or eway_bill.py. The bulk-response file's own layout is NOT among the committed sources (docs/compliance/sources/e-invoice has only the sample signed invoice and QR decodes), so whoever runs the sandbox upload of the bulk-JSON item must also supply a real response file. Recommendation: yes, small (days) and useful whichever tool generated the IRNs: match each row to an invoice by document number and date, record IRN, ack number, ack date and QR data, report unmatched rows, make re-import a no-op; verify with 5 invoices plus one unknown number. Note roadmap gst-19 is not the older GST-19 audit id. — _Sources:_ roadmap gst-19: Import the IRP's signed response file to record many IRNs at once — _Refs:_ rm/gst-19

## Income tax and ITR

- **POST-B-118** · `income-tax-itr` · normal · days — **Decide how to record facts that settle the ITR due date.** compliance_obligation_service.itr_due_date_for_client decides only three cases (Companies Act company 31 October; active audit engagement 31 October; s.92E report 30 November) and REFUSES LLP, Partnership, Trust, Proprietorship and Individual with decided=false and the earlier 31 July assumed, because s.44AB turnover, LLP Act s.34(4) with Rule 24(8) and s.12A(1)(b) facts are not held against a client; early costs nothing, late costs s.234A interest, the s.234F fee and the carry-forward. Decision question: record per-client turnover and audit applicability so the date can be decided, or keep the named gap? The Tax Audit tracker now holds turnover in tax_audits.turnover_paise, so a partial derivation for proprietors and partnerships may be possible. Recommendation: derive from tax_audits where a figure exists and keep refusing otherwise. — _Sources:_ CLAUDE.md lines 1682-1693; docs/audits/OPEN-QUESTIONS.md B20; CLAUDE.md 'Which ITR date applies' — _Refs:_ B20
- **POST-B-119** · `income-tax-itr` · normal · months — **Decide when the income-tax side forks for the Income-tax Act 2025.** The TDS vocabulary forks by period (domain/tds/vocabulary.py, act_for_date), but the income-tax computation and ITR side has no such fork: only domain/tds and domain/ai/statutory_brief use act_for_*, and it is still written in 1961-Act section numbers and AY terms. ITR-1..7 for AY 2026-27 were notified 30-03-2026 under the 1961 Act, but the 2025 Act reaches income-tax RETURNS in 2027 for tax year 2026-27 (AY and FY collapse into one 'tax year', s.139 maps to s.263 [S]) with new forms, JSON schemas and a section-correlation table whose publication date could not be confirmed. Decision question: start a period-aware vocabulary fork for return forms and section labels now, or wait for the Department to publish? Recommendation: wait; when the forms, schemas and correlation table appear the owner downloads them (the annual hand-off) and Claude builds a keep-both-permanently fork like vocabulary.py (2025-Act labels with the 1961 key retained) and loads the new schema into the keying sheet. No owner answer is recorded so far. — _Sources:_ docs/audits/2026-09-07-findings/income-tax-and-itr-computation-works.json open_questions_for_owner; docs/compliance/03-income-tax-and-tds.md; docs/compliance/03-income-tax-and-tds.md §0 'ITR forms are the exception'; tds_income_tax-36 Prepare the income-tax side for Tax Year 2026-27 under the Income-tax Act 2025 — _Refs:_ rm/tds_income_tax-36
- **POST-B-120** · `income-tax-itr` · normal · weeks — **Decide when to build ITR e-verify clock and consent-expiry model.** DECISION: when to build one consent-and-expiry model for the client-owned clocks, and whether the ITR 30-day clock goes first. Nothing in apps/api models an e-verification state, deadline or consent expiry (itr_workflow records the acknowledgement only; the filing demo just describes it; checked 02-10-2026). Three clocks share one shape [S, per docs/compliance/03, 07 and 08]: ITR e-verification within 30 days of filing or the return is treated as not filed (Notification 5/2022 DGIT-Systems, from 01-08-2022; no ERI or CA can verify for the taxpayer); ERI client validity/consent 7 days to 1 year, extendable by up to 6 months and never past the ERI's own registration, with every ERI service request verified by the taxpayer within 7 days of its Transaction ID or it lapses; and GST API access consent (6 hours to 30 days, announced by email/SMS naming the ASP since 29-09-2025, revocable by the taxpayer at any moment without telling us). docs/compliance/07 asks for one design (follow routers/engagement_sign_public.py and share the consent shape with the Account Aggregator) with a pending-verification queue and reminders that degrades gracefully on revocation, since an integration that errors on a revoked consent looks like a product fault. The two source items recommend differently: one says build the 30-day clock and a pending-verification queue with reminders now, because it helps today's manual path (the CA files on the portal and the client must still e-verify), and defer the 7-day and validity clocks and the consent design until an ERI route exists (D17); the other says wait and build the whole model in the same change as the first provider, since nothing can exercise it earlier (gated on a registration existing). Nothing may capture an Aadhaar OTP or portal password. _When: D17: owner starts registrations after a CA demo (owner parked until after the demo)._ — _Sources:_ docs/compliance/03-income-tax-and-tds.md §1 consent model, §2; docs/compliance/08-government-api-access-the-verified-position.md A.4; docs/compliance/07-getting-permission-to-file.md §3.1, §3.4 ('Three clocks'), §5, §6 item 4 — _Refs:_ D17
- **POST-B-121** · `income-tax-itr` · normal · weeks — **Decide whether earlier-year returns (FY 2024-25 and before) are in scope.** A FY 2024-25 return (AY 2025-26, including an ITR-U) computes at substituted 2025-26 rates and says so in words via fy_rate_gap ('computed at FY 2025-26 rates', rates_verified False) but cannot be computed correctly, and s.89 relief is refused for most real arrears. Adding 2024-25 to RATES_BY_FY alone is UNSOUND: the year straddles the 23-07-2024 capital-gains fork and one FYTaxRates cannot hold two rate sets, so it would stamp post-fork rates on the whole year under verified=True. Decision question: are belated, revised, ITR-U, notice and s.89 computations for earlier years in scope? Option A keeps the honest warning; option B builds pre/post buckets (separate item) and has a person read each Finance Act (separate item). Recommendation: A until a client has an earlier-year return, then B starting with FY 2024-25. — _Sources:_ docs/audits/2026-09-08b-what-is-left.md section 4 (capital-gains fork); docs/audits/2026-09-08-what-is-left.md section 2.8; apps/api/domain/income_tax/statutory_rates.py RATES_BY_FY; docs/audits/2026-09-12-the-probe-pass/income-tax.md IT-04 — _Refs:_ IT-29, IT-04
- **POST-B-122** · `income-tax-itr` · normal · months — **Decide whether to build a portal-acceptable ITR JSON (non-computed half).** itr_json.py computes a tax-figures payload and then refuses in three named steps (SchemaNotVerified, SoftwareProviderNotRegistered, ReturnIncomplete; about lines 738-790): ITRPayload carries about 14 totals and 20 head lines and no PersonalInfo, FilingStatus, bank accounts, Schedules S/HP/CG/OS/TDS/IT, Verification, CreationInfo.Digest (44 characters or '-'), per-transaction CG/HP/BP interiors, or (ITR-3/5/6) balance sheet and P&L schedules. Field placement is already checked against the seven committed Department schemas; today the output is itr_field_placements() and the IT-17 keying sheet that a CA keys into the offline utility, and the public site must keep saying no ITR JSON is produced. Decision question: start this build, and in what order? Recommendation: not until the SW id registration and the entity decisions (roadmap decisions 3 and 7) are done; then ITR-1 and ITR-4 first, then 2 and 3, validated with jsonschema on 10 fixtures per form, a human uploading 10 on a consenting taxpayer's portal account (upload and validate only) and the portal's tax equalling ours to the rupee. Prepare-and-upload only; the taxpayer still e-verifies. Not in scope until asked for by name. — _Sources:_ docs/compliance/01-what-exists-today.md §3 (ITR JSON row), §5; apps/api/domain/income_tax/itr_json.py ReturnIncomplete; docs/compliance/08-government-api-access-the-verified-position.md A.4 (Code seam); tds_income_tax-01 Write a complete, portal-acceptable ITR JSON (ITR-1, 2 and 4 first, then 3) — _Refs:_ D17, IT-17, rm/tds_income_tax-01
- **POST-B-123** · `income-tax-itr` · normal · weeks — **Decide: merge it_notices into government_notices or give it an API door.** app/income-tax/notices/page.tsx tracks income-tax notices in it_notices straight from the browser (role-guarded by migration 261), while government_notices (AI extraction with staged CA approval, health 'open_notices' dimension, client compliance tab) is the backend-managed system; no apps/api code references it_notices, so a CA-entered IT notice never reaches the health score or any deadline. Decision question: merge it_notices into government_notices (one notice register and approval flow) or keep it separate and add an API door? Recommendation: merge, to keep one register. — _Sources:_ docs/audits/2026-07-02-executive-product-audit.md R3.9c item 2 (lines 3999-4003) — _Refs:_ R3.9c-2
- **POST-B-124** · `income-tax-itr` · low · days — **Compute the s.43B(h) deduction for earlier-year disallowances on opening bills.** For opening (carried-over, is_opening) bills the engine names but does not compute s.43B(h)'s other direction: an earlier year's disallowance actually PAID this year comes back as a deduction. Nothing on a carried-over bill records whether it was disallowed in the prior year's return, so the answer lists them rather than showing a nil. Computing it needs a recorded 'disallowed in year X' fact per opening bill (a field on the opening document or a CA's statement). DECISION: whether to add that fact; low priority, matters only for clients migrated mid-year; recommendation: leave listed-not-computed until a migrated client needs it. — _Sources:_ /home/user/caflow-ai/CLAUDE.md lines 3407-3410; docs/audits/findings-status.json ACC-14 — _Refs:_ PUR-15, ACC-14
- **POST-B-125** · `income-tax-itr` · low · weeks — **Decide how Form 3CD clause 35 (stock quantitative details) gets its rows.** Clause 35 needs the form's own row shape: opening stock, purchases, consumption or production, sales, closing stock, shortage and yield per principal item or category. stock_position_as_at, stock_ageing_as_at and INV-01/INV-04 hold opening and closing stock but not that shape, and no manufacturing yield is held anywhere. Decision question: what does the practice key in per client against what the stock ledger can supply? Options: derive purchases, sales and closing from the ledger and take yield and shortage as typed inputs; or leave clause 35 manual. Recommendation: derive what the ledger holds and take yield as an input. Deferred 25-09-2026 as unfinished work, not a missing capability. — _Sources:_ docs/audits/findings-status.json IT-11 what_is_left; docs/audits/2026-09-12-the-probe-pass/income-tax.md IT-11 (+ findings-status.json IT-11 what_is_left) — _Refs:_ IT-11
- **POST-B-126** · `income-tax-itr` · low · hours — **Decide on foreign-company 35% rate; correct CLAUDE.md entity_rates claim.** Research [S+]: a foreign company is charged 35% (Finance Act 2024, from 01-04-2024) plus surcharge 2% (Rs 1-10 crore) and 5% above, and 4% cess; 50% survives for certain royalty/FTS under old agreements. apps/web and apps/api have no foreign-company client entity type and domain/income_tax/entity_rates.py holds only firm, domestic company, s.115BAA and s.115BAB (no foreign entry; s.195 has only a foreign-company payee class), yet CLAUDE.md's annual-sweep table says entity_rates holds 'firm / LLP / domestic and foreign company rates'. Decision question: is foreign-company ITR computation in scope for an SME practice? Recommendation: out of scope; either way correct the CLAUDE.md row. — _Sources:_ docs/audits/2026-09-07-market-research/income-tax-tds-primary.md section 2.3 and section 9 — _Refs:_ MR-IT-14
- **POST-B-127** · `income-tax-itr` · low · weeks — **Decide whether to build the named house-property and Schedule S gaps.** domain/income_tax/house_property.py and schedule_s.py do not handle arrears (s.25A/25B), letting with furniture, s.192(1C) start-up ESOP deferment, each s.10 exemption's own ceiling or the s.16(ii) allowance; all are named on every answer, and a third self-occupied house is refused because which two is the assessee's choice. Decision question: build any of these now or leave them as named gaps? Recommendation: leave them until a client needs one, then build the specific item. — _Sources:_ CLAUDE.md line 2661 (TDS-INCOME-TAX-14, -15) — _Refs:_ TDS-INCOME-TAX-14, TDS-INCOME-TAX-15
- **POST-B-128** · `income-tax-itr` · low · weeks — **Decide whether to map Schedule CG, HP, BP interiors on keying sheet.** The ITR keying sheet (GET /api/itr/filings/{id}/keying-sheet) and payload carry Part B-TI heads and Chapter VI-A sections (137 field paths, IT-17 closed) but the per-asset, per-property and per-business interiors of Schedule CG, HP and BP are deliberately not mapped: they need a row per asset, property and business, and an aggregate written into a row field is 'right value, wrong field'. Since then house_property.py and schedule_s.py (migration 455 worksheets) hold per-property data and the capital-gains register holds per-transaction rows, so HP and CG may now be mappable, while no per-business rows exist for BP. s.234A/B/C and s.140A stay nil and named on the sheet. Decision question: map HP and CG interiors now? Recommendation: HP and CG only, each new path added with a tests/test_itr_schema_paths.py case against the committed Department schema in the same commit (the TaxPayableOnDeemedTI mis-resolution is the precedent). — _Sources:_ docs/audits/findings-status.json IT-17; apps/api/domain/income_tax/keying_sheet.py and itr_json.py; docs/audits/2026-09-12-the-probe-pass/income-tax.md IT-17 (+ findings-status.json IT-17 what_is_left) — _Refs:_ IT-17
- **POST-B-129** · `income-tax-itr` · low · weeks — **Decide whether to model s.80JJAA (additional employee cost).** s.80JJAA is refused with its reasons rather than half-modelled: it needs 240 days of employment, Rs 25,000 a month, a recognised PF, non-cash payment, a s.44AB audit and a Form 10DA report, none of which these books hold. Decision question: model it or keep the refusal? Recommendation: keep the refusal and reopen only with a client need. — _Sources:_ docs/audits/findings-status.json IT-32 — _Refs:_ IT-32
- **POST-B-130** · `income-tax-itr` · low · days — **Decide whether to persist s.115BAC(6) election history and Form 10-IEA.** GET /api/income-tax/regime-election takes prior years as repeated query parameters (prior=FY:opted_out|withdrew) and form_10iea_filed_on as a typed date; nothing stores a client's election history or the 10-IEA acknowledgement, so clause (i)'s once-only withdrawal answers history_unknown unless the CA retypes it each time. Storing it is a migration, and the election must never be set from the payroll Circular 04/2023 withholding intimation (three separate things with the Rule 26C Form 12BB statement, kept apart in domain/payroll/declarations.py). Decision question: persist it? Recommendation: yes, as a small per-client table, when regime advice is used in earnest. — _Sources:_ docs/audits/2026-09-12-the-probe-pass/income-tax.md IT-27 — _Refs:_ IT-27
- **POST-B-131** · `income-tax-itr` · low · days — **Decide whether to test s.44AB clauses (c), (d) and (e).** tax_audit.py tests s.44AB(a) and (b) only; (c), (d) and (e) each compare DECLARED profit against a figure deemed under s.44AE/44BB/44BBB/44ADA/44AD(4), which no turnover box carries, so they are named on every answer including 'not required'. Decision question: should the product decide s.44AB applicability beyond turnover? Option A keeps the named gap; option B adds declared-versus-deemed profit inputs (new fields) and applies the clauses. Recommendation: keep the named gap until a client's facts need it, since a wrong 'not required' carries s.271B exposure. — _Sources:_ CLAUDE.md lines 1673-1676 — _Refs:_ IT-11

## TDS and TCS

- **POST-B-132** · `tds-tcs` · high · weeks — **Decide whether to build the TDS RPU/FVU statement file writer.** DECISION QUESTION: build a byte-exact caret-delimited writer (File Header, Batch Header, Challan Detail, Deductee Detail, plus Form 27A) for 24Q/26Q/27Q/27EQ (138/140/144/143 from FY 2026-27), or stay on domain/tds/keying_sheet.py (computed figures grouped in RPU order for a CA to key, NO_FVU_FILE_IS_PRODUCED on every sheet) and say so in positioning. Facts: recorded as not built on 25-09-2026 because a wrong field position rejects the whole statement and the field list is moving (RPU/FVU v1.1 vs v1.2); the writer needs schema the product does not hold (deductor GSTN, a structured responsible-person block, a migration) and a correction-statement model (separate item); it is a multi-week build that must be round-tripped through a real FVU run by a human (owner runs the FVU and reports errors) with a test pinning each field position to the spec version. Market context: the platform audit says a TDS product is buyable only if it produces a file the FVU accepts, and greytHR markets one-click 24Q with FVU validation; a low-confidence interim option is an Excel export shaped to Saral's import template (template not fetched). Blocked on the spec download item. Claude's recommendation: do not start until the specs are committed (it cannot be written correctly without them), then schedule it once pilot firms show how much TDS volume they carry; meanwhile keep the keying sheet and wording that says it is not an FVU file. _When: Owner parked until after the demo (which weeks-sized feature next); also blocked on the spec download item._ — _Sources:_ CLAUDE.md lines 300-330 (TDS-16); docs/compliance/03-income-tax-and-tds.md §4 (25-09-2026 research pass, Decision); docs/audits/findings-status.json TDS-16 (open); docs/audits/findings-status.json TDS-16; docs/compliance/03-income-tax-and-tds.md s.4; docs/plan/THE-PLAN.md (+4 more) — _Refs:_ TDS-16
- **POST-B-133** · `tds-tcs` · normal · days — **Decide facts to record to complete the s.393 payment-code table.** From 01-04-2026 TDS lines carry numeric payment codes. domain/tds/vocabulary.payment_code_for() answers a confirmed subset of 14 sections from Annexure 2 of Protean's RPU/FVU file-format spec for 138/140/144 (a [P] read): s.192 by a stated non-government default, 193, 194, 194B, 194C (by the rate applied in payment_code_for, by the PAN's fourth character on a deductee row), 194D, 194G, 194H, 194I(a)/(b), 194J(a), 194LA, 194Q and 194T; and every deductee row on a 24Q/26Q/27Q already carries its code or the reason none exists (domain/tds/deductee_payment_code.py, TDS-INCOME-TAX-31; so the old note 'not yet wired into per-line output' is stale). Still named gaps, each with its own reason: s.194A splits into three codes (1020/1021/1022) by payee age and payer kind, and no registry fact holds either; s.194J(b) shares Sl. No. 6(iii).D(b) with a director's remuneration (1027 vs 1028) and cannot tell a professional fee from a director's fee; a bare 194I/194J names no limb; s.192A; 194K, 206C and the refused sections (194-IA/IB/M, 194N, 194O, 194R, 194S) are not held; and the rest of the 1001-1067 table (payment_code_gap() still flags the whole table on every 2025-Act statement and the keying sheet lists rows with no code). The probe pass (TDS-17) flagged the missing code as a real gap on any FY 2026-27 statement. DECISION: which facts to record on the supplier or bill (payee age/kind, director vs professional fee, limb) so those sections can resolve, plus a human read of the remaining Annexure 2 rows in an ordinary browser; once the owner's spreadsheet is committed to docs/compliance/sources (separate item) Claude can read and add the remaining rows itself, then record the new per-payee facts and wire them in. A wrong code is accepted by the product and then wrong on the statement, so none is guessed or written from memory. _When: Statutory data per se; no demo-day exposure (gaps are named on every row)._ — _Sources:_ CLAUDE.md lines 2659 (TDS-INCOME-TAX-31); docs/compliance/03-income-tax-and-tds.md §0 ('payment code table'), §6; apps/api/domain/tds/vocabulary.py _PAYMENT_CODES_CONFIRMED and gap reasons; CLAUDE.md lines 298-299 (+1 more) — _Refs:_ TDS-INCOME-TAX-31, TDS-16, TDS-17
- **POST-B-134** · `tds-tcs` · normal · weeks — **Decide whether the product should collect TCS (s.206C) and file Form 27EQ/27D.** DECISION QUESTION: should PracticeSync collect tax on a client's sales at all? Today s.206C is registry reference data only; a vendor may never carry it (deduction_section_refusal, SALES-32), no path computes TCS on a sales invoice, no 27EQ builder (Form 143 under the 2025 Act), no Form 27D and no TCS ledger exist (domain/risk/register.py: 'deliberately absent'); TDS-10 is recorded not-a-defect-as-stated, and the competitive matrix scored the product 'TDS on receipts only' against Business Central and NetSuite (the GST TCS of GSTR-8 is a different tax and exists). s.206C(1H) ceased from 01-04-2025 (buyer deducts under s.194Q), removing the commonest case; (1F) motor vehicle over Rs 10 lakh, (1G) LRS and tour packages, scrap, timber and liquor remain. If yes, the build is a TCS rate and threshold registry by nature of goods, a sales-invoice TCS line and ledger, a tcs_collections register mirroring tds_deductions, collection on receipt, challan matching, a 27EQ statement builder or keying sheet, Form 27D and a migration; any excess-only base must credit tax already collected, as s.200 does for TDS. Options: keep refusing (current) or build a limited set of cases. Claude's recommendation: keep refusing until a pilot firm has a client who sells in-scope goods. The s.206C table and 27EQ layout reading is a separate item. _When: Owner parked until after the demo (which weeks-sized feature next)._ — _Sources:_ CLAUDE.md lines 787-813; docs/audits/findings-status.json TDS-10 (not_a_defect_as_stated, needs: owner decision); CLAUDE.md section on s.206C; docs/audits/2026-07-02-executive-product-audit.md R3.1 audit finding 6 and R3.1a TCS bullet (lines 2430-2432, 2474-2478) (+3 more) — _Refs:_ SALES-32, TDS-10, R3.1
- **POST-B-135** · `tds-tcs` · normal · weeks — **Decide whether to build statements for clients whose books are elsewhere.** Statements are built only from posted purchase bills, vendor advances and finalized payroll (services/tds_return_service.py, source 'posted_purchase_bills_and_advances'); deductions typed or imported on /tds go to tds_deductions and never reach a statement, although the deposit worksheet reads them, so the two views disagree. The caller-supplied compute endpoints were deleted on purpose (TDS-29) because the old screen invented a TAN and stamped deposited equal to deducted. DECISION: reopen it by building 26Q/27Q/24Q from the register through the same deductor resolver, challan mapping and refusals so a TDS-only practice with clients on Tally or Busy can use the statement builder. The shared FY aggregate (separate item) must be unified on PAN first. Done when a client with no bills and ten register deductions produces a 26Q whose rows, FY aggregate and s.200 credit match the register. Claude's view: worth doing after pilots show how many TDS-only clients exist. _When: Owner parked until after the demo (which weeks-sized feature next); depends on the aggregate-unification item._ — _Sources:_ tds_income_tax-06 Build 26Q, 27Q and 24Q for clients whose books are not in PracticeSync, and unify the FY aggregate — _Refs:_ rm/tds_income_tax-06, TDS-29
- **POST-B-136** · `tds-tcs` · normal · weeks — **Design TDS on a foreign-currency vendor advance (currently a named gap).** A vendor advance in INR withholds (migration 358, services/vendor_tds.py) but a payment in a FOREIGN currency does not: the vendor is credited in their own currency while tax is remitted in rupees, so the cash leg is not simply 'amount - tax'. purchase_payment_service reports foreign_advance_not_withheld (domain/tds/residency.GAP_FOREIGN_ADVANCE_NOT_WITHHELD) rather than a silent zero. Under-deduction under s.195 disallows the whole expenditure (s.40(a)(i)). DECISION/DESIGN: the realised-FX cash leg and the s.195 chargeability question need a design before building. — _Sources:_ docs/audits/2026-09-08c-the-phase-plan.md Phase 11a (PUR-10 'Two things it deliberately does not do'); apps/api/services/purchase_payment_service.py:611 — _Refs:_ PUR-10
- **POST-B-137** · `tds-tcs` · normal · weeks — **Model TDS correction statements: original PRN, correction type, Conso reader.** Nothing models a correction statement: no original_prn or correction type exists (grep finds only a test), routers/tds_workspace.py treats filed/revised as furnished (_TDS_RETURN_FURNISHED) and refuses a recompute with 'a correction is a correction statement on TRACES', and the return transition set (about line 1334) allows only pending/prepared/ca_approved/filed so 'revised' (allowed by migration 037's CHECK) is unreachable. So when TRACES raises a short-deduction default a month later a CA cannot prepare the correction here. SOURCES DISAGREE on timing: the 12-09-2026 probe pass says the data-model half (original_prn plus a correction type on tds_returns via a migration, widen the transition set) is code-only and can precede the file writer, and it is also the prerequisite for restating a filed quarter when a purchase note later moves a bill (TDS-32 is only a reported adjustment); the later status record says shape it once the writer decision is made. Second half (Conso reader, correction categories, regular-versus-correction flag, original token recorded) needs Protean's correction format (C1-C5 per the finding, C1-C9 per what-to-fetch) and an anonymised real Conso file from TRACES for a real deductor and quarter, both owner-supplied; correction statements are normally prepared in RPU from the Conso file (medium confidence). DECISION: build the data-model half now, or wait for the writer decision; recommendation is to wait for the writer decision and the Protean format, because a model nobody can output is premature and the category vocabulary cannot be settled before the format is read. Done when loading a Conso file, changing one deductee PAN and producing a correction file passes FVU with the original token recorded. _When: Depends on the RPU/FVU writer decision and the spec download._ — _Sources:_ docs/audits/findings-status.json TDS-16 needs ('a correction-statement model'); apps/api/routers/tds_workspace.py; docs/audits/2026-09-12-the-probe-pass/tds.md TDS-16 / TDS-32; tds_income_tax-05 Support TDS correction statements — _Refs:_ TDS-16, TDS-32, rm/tds_income_tax-05
- **POST-B-138** · `tds-tcs` · low · days — **Accept a space-separated or PDF-pasted Form 26AS in the parser.** read_26as_text (domain/income_tax/form26as_service.py, split on tab or pipe only at line 219) is a real limit: a 26AS pasted out of a PDF viewer is space-separated, every line is reported skipped, looks_unrecognised is true and the router 422s. Splitting on runs of spaces would cut deductor names in half, so a real fix needs a layout-aware PDF or Excel import. The parser also cannot tell Part A2 from A (PART\s+([A-Z]) keeps one letter), which is safe only because both are credits. DECISION: whether a layout-aware 26AS PDF/Excel import is worth building; it needs one anonymised real 26AS download in each format from the owner as the layout reference, after which Claude builds it. — _Sources:_ /home/user/caflow-ai/CLAUDE.md lines 4152-4182 (TDS-21) — _Refs:_ TDS-21
- **POST-B-139** · `tds-tcs` · low · days · UNSURE — **Carry a mid-year-migrated client's earlier s.194 aggregate for each vendor.** A s.194 FY aggregate cannot be carried over by opening documents (it is measured on what was CREDITED this FY, not on what is still owed) and the module says so rather than approximating; the same limit is recorded against ACC-14 in the findings status. resolve_tds takes fy_prior_taxable_paise and fy_prior_tds_paise from its caller, but services/vendor_tds.aggregate_so_far derives them only from bills, payments and advances held in the product (checked: no input door in that file), so a client migrated mid-year cannot state what was already credited to each vendor earlier in the FY. Not checked: whether the TDS workspace router (routers/tds_workspace.py, which also names the two parameters) offers a manual door. Build option: an opening-aggregate entry per vendor and section, folded into aggregate_so_far. — _Sources:_ /home/user/caflow-ai/CLAUDE.md lines 3411-3416 — _Refs:_ ACC-14
- **POST-B-140** · `tds-tcs` · low · days — **Decide whether to automate same-quarter TDS reduction after a purchase return.** PUR-23 and TDS-32: domain/tds/purchase_return.py states the divergence (s.194 charges the aggregate credited; s.199 gives credit for tax already paid over) and, since 17-09-2026, decides recompute-versus-stands from tds_deductions.challan_date via deposit_state() (unpaid: may be recomputed on the smaller aggregate under s.194J(1); paid: stands and the deductor carries an excess deposit under s.200 with s.199; 'deposited with no date' is its own answer), but the FIGURES are never adjusted, because a return can take the year's aggregate back below the threshold so the recompute figure is not one bill's to compute. DECISION: whether the same-quarter reduction should be automated at all; until then the quarter's statutory_gaps name every bill whose credit a note moved and the purchases screen renders it. — _Sources:_ docs/audits/findings-status.json PUR-23, TDS-32 (deliberately_not_done) — _Refs:_ PUR-23, TDS-32
- **POST-B-141** · `tds-tcs` · low · days — **Decide whether to hold a buyer-turnover fact for s.194Q.** The engine computes s.194Q (0.1% on the excess over Rs 50 lakh, carried as charge_on_excess_only) but does not decide whether it applies: the first proviso binds only a buyer whose own turnover exceeded Rs 10 crore in the preceding FY and no client turnover figure reaches it, so the CA marks the vendor. DECISION: add a per-client business-turnover input (the GST aggregate-turnover table, migration 401, is a different figure: s.2(6) PAN-level all-India turnover) or keep relying on the CA's vendor marking; recommendation is to keep the current behaviour until a client shows the need. — _Sources:_ CLAUDE.md lines 345-351
- **POST-B-142** · `tds-tcs` · low · weeks — **Decide whether to model s.194R, s.194O and s.194S.** DECISION QUESTION: add the new documents these sections need, or keep refusing? Each is refused on the base as much as the rate, with its own sentence in _SECTIONS_WITH_NO_FIGURE_AND_THE_REASON: s.194R's benefit is often in kind with no bill amount, s.194O's base is the participant's sale rather than any bill the operator receives, s.194S has no virtual-digital-asset document (and s.194S(2) wants tax paid before consideration in kind is released); s.194N is a bank charging on cash withdrawn and is never a purchase-bill deduction. Building any needs new document types, not just a rate, and the rates and thresholds first have to be read off the Act (separate reading item). Default if the owner does nothing: stay refused, which is the safe direction. — _Sources:_ CLAUDE.md lines 1407-1412 and TDS-23 bullet; docs/audits/2026-09-12-the-probe-pass/tds.md TDS-23 — _Refs:_ TDS-23
- **POST-B-143** · `tds-tcs` · low · days — **Decide whether to model the s.194J call-centre 2% proviso.** s.194J's proviso cuts the rate to 2% for a payee whose business is only operating a call centre. That is a fact about the payee's business, not a clause of the bill, and the registry carries no payee-level facts of that kind, so it is deliberately unmodelled and named (TDS-22); the bare section still withholds at the higher rate, the recoverable direction (an excess is the payee's to reclaim, an under-deduction disallows the expenditure). DECISION: add payee-level business facts to the vendor master (which also touches the payment-code gap for s.194J limbs) or leave the over-deduction in place; recommendation is to leave it until a client has such a payee. — _Sources:_ CLAUDE.md lines 1284-1287 (TDS-22); docs/audits/findings-status.json TDS-22; docs/audits/2026-09-12-the-probe-pass/tds.md TDS-22 (+ findings-status.json TDS-22) — _Refs:_ TDS-22
- **POST-B-144** · `tds-tcs` · low · days — **Decide whether to produce Forms 26QB, 26QC and 26QD.** DECISION QUESTION: should the product ever produce the challan-cum-statement forms for s.194-IA (property), s.194-IB (rent by individuals/HUF) and s.194M? They stay refused by deduction_section_refusal (a bill deducting under them gets its own sentence) because Forms 26QB/26QC/26QD are not 24Q/26Q/27Q/27EQ statements and residency.return_type_for routes on residency alone against migration 014's four-value return_type CHECK and tds_deductions.return_type: adding a registry row would turn a visible 422 into a silently mis-routed 26Q row (pinned by tests/test_a_section_the_engine_cannot_answer_for_is_refused.py). Options: stay refused; prepare-only worksheets (the roadmap proposal, days); or a full form path with its own challan and no-TAN deductor handling (a CHECK/migration change plus the forms' layouts). The probe's safe subset (s.194T) is already built. Reading the sections and form fields is a separate item. — _Sources:_ CLAUDE.md lines 783-786; CLAUDE.md lines 1397-1416 (TDS-23); docs/audits/2026-09-12-the-probe-pass/tds.md TDS-23 — _Refs:_ TDS-23

## Payroll and its statutory returns

- **POST-B-145** · `payroll-statutory` · normal · days — **Confirm the ESI Rs 25,000 ceiling for disabled employees and model it.** Research [S] (tallysolutions, equily; docs/compliance/04 repeats it): the ESI wage ceiling is Rs 21,000/month and Rs 25,000 for employees with disability, last revised July 2019. domain/payroll/statutory.py ESIRates carries one ceiling (wage_ceiling_paise 21,000) and no disability flag exists on the employee master or import, although services/filing_demo/esi.py already tells the CA 'Rs 25,000 for employees with disability' in prose. A disabled employee earning Rs 21,001-25,000 is therefore excluded from ESI with nothing deducted and no gap named. Decision question: confirm the reading (ESI Act s.2(9) proviso or the notification) and approve a per-employee disability flag (a migration) plus the second ceiling; Claude then builds it. — _Sources:_ docs/audits/2026-09-07-market-research/payroll-primary.md section 3a — _Refs:_ MR-PAY-11
- **POST-B-146** · `payroll-statutory` · normal · days — **Decide how ESI Rule 50 coverage treats earlier unreleased payroll runs.** PAY-04 made routers/payroll.py::_members_contributing_earlier_this_period read only RELEASED runs (status finalized/paid), so a member covered earlier in the same ESI contribution period whose earlier run is still unreleased drops out of Rule 50 continuation the month wages cross Rs 21,000: ESI is under-deducted and the employer is liable with interest. The 12 Sep probe pass judged the status filter sound for the TDS read but wrong for the ESI read and advised leaving ESI inclusive or deciding coverage from wage history; CLAUDE.md's PAY-04 bullet argues the opposite (a draft has deducted nothing). Verified 2 Oct 2026 that nothing stops a run being created while an earlier month is unreleased. Decision question on Rule 50's intent: keep the TDS read filtered but decide ESI coverage from wage history, or refuse computing a month before the previous one is released. Low frequency (needs an unreleased earlier month). — _Sources:_ docs/audits/2026-09-12-the-probe-pass/README.md section 9 PAY-04; verified at apps/api/routers/payroll.py:528-560; docs/audits/2026-09-12-the-probe-pass/payroll.md PAY-04 — _Refs:_ PAY-04
- **POST-B-147** · `payroll-statutory` · normal · weeks — **Settle with a CA how Form 16/130 reaches employees.** Annexure II (Q4 24Q salary detail, routers/payroll.py form_24q_annexure_ii) is built and TRACES turns it into Part B, but nothing delivers the finished certificate: the employee portal (routers/portal_employee.py) serves only the TDS projection and payslip PDF (verified 2 Oct 2026: no Form 16 route), and annexure2.py says there is no Form 16 generator by design. A documents screen has a FORM16 type for upload (models/document.py, routers/documents.py) but it is unchecked whether the client Documents screens serve it to employees; from FY 2026-27 the certificate is Form 130 (three parts) and 16A is Form 131 (quarterly) while document-type labels still say Form 16. Decision (roadmap decision 14), with a practising CA: the repo says CBDT Notification 09/2019 requires Part B to come from TRACES while Zoho's help describes generating Part B itself; settle which is safe before any self-generated Part B is considered. Claude recommends the TRACES route: a Partner uploads the TRACES bulk PDF ZIP of Part A/B files, matched to employees by PAN and stored under the existing attachments rule (document id, no expiring URL), shown on the employee portal through an employee-principal route, listed (never dropped) when unmatched, and marked issued against tds_certificates. TRACES has no API, so a human downloads each PDF; any Form 16 the product renders itself must be labelled a working document. Employees ask every June. — _Sources:_ docs/compliance/03-income-tax-and-tds.md §0 (certificates table), §5; payroll-06 Deliver Form 16 / Form 130 to employees from the portal — _Refs:_ rm/payroll-06
- **POST-B-148** · `payroll-statutory` · low · weeks — **Build overtime and shift hours-and-rate capture the payslip reads.** No overtime, shift or hourly modelling exists on the slip: nothing in payroll_slips or migrations holds overtime hours or rates (grep of migrations finds none; domain/payroll/wage_base.py lists overtime among PF exclusions). A build needs a migration for an hours-and-rate table, attendance integration and a change to _compute_slip; overtime must stay out of PF wages, enter ESI wages on the two-month interval test and be taxable in the s.192 estimate. Statutory overtime rules differ by state and Act, so the rate basis needs a human reading first. Decision question: do practice clients need this before it is scheduled? Acceptance: hours and rate recorded for a month appear as an overtime line, excluded from PF wages, following the ESI test, included in the s.192 estimate. — _Sources:_ docs/audits/findings-status.json PAY-27 what_is_left; docs/audits/findings-status.json PAY-27 what_is_left (via 2026-09-14-what-needs-both-of-us.md); docs/audits/2026-09-12-the-probe-pass/payroll.md PAY-27 (+ findings-status.json PAY-27 what_is_left) (+1 more) — _Refs:_ PAY-27, rm/payroll-21
