- **POST-B-149** · `payroll-statutory` · low · weeks — **Build reimbursement claims and flexible benefits with approval state.** The only reimbursement support is a 'reimbursement' kind of one-time earning that the CA types (domain/payroll/one_time_earnings.py OneTimeEarningsIn, migration 331; non-salary, not an s.17(1) item). There is no claim table with its own approval state, no employee submission from the portal, no proof document, no flexible-benefit plan, no limits, and nothing derivable from payroll_slips; PAY-27 and PAY-26 record it unbuilt. A real build needs a migration (claim table with approval state), the s.17 treatment per component and a read into the slip. Decision question: is this wanted for the first cohort, and when? Acceptance: an employee submits a claim with a proof document, a manager approves it, it lands in the next run as a non-taxable reimbursement, a rejected claim does not. — _Sources:_ docs/audits/findings-status.json PAY-27 what_is_left, PAY-26; docs/audits/findings-status.json PAY-27 what_is_left (via 2026-09-14-what-needs-both-of-us.md); docs/audits/2026-09-12-the-probe-pass/payroll.md PAY-27 (+ findings-status.json PAY-27 what_is_left) (+1 more) — _Refs:_ PAY-27, PAY-26, rm/payroll-23
- **POST-B-150** · `payroll-statutory` · low · days — **Decide an optional two-person release rule for payroll.** Decision question: offer a rule that the payroll approver differs from whoever prepared or recomputed the run, per firm? Finalise is rbac('payroll','finalize'), Partner by default, and since migration 403 a Partner can grant that one permission to a named person, so the Partner bottleneck is partly gone; but nothing records a preparer-approver pair and the maker-checker engine (services/approval_service.py) covers users, assignments and chart-of-accounts changes only. Ties to roadmap decision 11's maker-checker policy (the compliance-obligation item). Recommendation: a per-firm opt-in; with it on, finalising by the same user who recomputed the run is refused with a sentence, a different authorised user succeeds, and the transition log records both. Days of work. — _Sources:_ payroll-28 Two-person release for payroll (prepared by, approved by) — _Refs:_ rm/payroll-28, rm/practice_management-16, rm/practice_management-18
- **POST-B-151** · `payroll-statutory` · low · months — **Decide build order for per-state professional tax return/challan files.** Track F5: four states (MH, TN, KA, WB) are computed and NO challan or return is produced for any state; domain/payroll/handoff.py says so ('NO FILE... Track F, phase F5') and shows no PT due date. The slab half is built (migration 327 firm_pt_slabs, three endpoints, Settings screen) and remittances are recorded (migration 365); only the per-state RETURN/challan artefact remains, a 22-way fan in which each state has a different layout and needs a person to supply that state's actual return layout/format first. Decision question: which states to do first and whether to build any before a client needs one. Claude's recommendation: start with the states in the PT-slab priority list once their notifications are in hand, one complete layout at a time. — _Sources:_ docs/compliance/08-government-api-access-the-verified-position.md section 5.2 (F5), A.10; docs/audits/OPEN-QUESTIONS.md B13; docs/audits/WHERE-WE-STOPPED.md lines 720-740 (F5 re-read) — _Refs:_ F5, Track F, B13
- **POST-B-152** · `payroll-statutory` · low · days — **Decide how professional tax due dates reach the compliance calendar.** compliance_engine.payroll_deposit_due_dates emits EPF, ESI and TDS deposit dates but deliberately no professional tax date, because each state fixes its own, and the test suite pins PT as absent; so a client with PT deductions has no PT deposit reminder. Decision question: (a) add per-state dates for the four modelled states, (b) let a firm record its state's due date alongside its firm_pt_slabs rows (mirroring migration 327), or (c) keep it absent. The dates themselves need a person to read each state's notification (separate reading item); a wrong date is worse than none. — _Sources:_ docs/audits/2026-09-01-payroll-can-it-run-a-year.md finding 6 (PT absent); apps/api/services/compliance_engine.py payroll_deposit_due_dates
- **POST-B-153** · `payroll-statutory` · low · weeks — **Decide on pay-head formulas, bulk employee invites and portal Form 12BB.** docs/architecture/10-payroll.md 'Deferred with reasons' still stands (verified 2 Oct 2026: no formula code in salary_structure.py, no bulk-invite path in the payroll or portal routers): a formula engine for pay heads, bulk employee invites to the portal, and Form 12BB (declaration) in the employee portal, which serves only the TDS projection and payslip PDF. Leave management and Form 16 delivery are separate items. Whether to take any up after the 7 September scope change is the owner's call; no recommendation beyond not starting any unasked. — _Sources:_ docs/architecture/10-payroll.md 'Deferred with reasons', 'Deliberately not built'
- **POST-B-154** · `payroll-statutory` · low · weeks — **Decide the scope of leave management: full workflow or typed balances.** Attendance holds five day-count fields and one hand-typed leave_balances row per employee per year; nothing in apps/api reads leave_balances (migration 428 says so), the browser upserts it directly (apps/web/app/payroll/attendance/page.tsx), the employee portal's leave tab is read-only, there is no leave_request, policy, accrual, carry-forward or holiday calendar anywhere, and leave encashment on a settlement still comes from the request body. PAY-24 was closed by the honest option (an unrecorded entitlement is NULL and renders as an em dash, migration 428, never the invented 12/12/15). Decision question: take up a per-client leave policy with server-side accrual plus leave requests/approval in the portal (PAY-26 names these as PAY-27 residue), or leave leave to the CA? Claude's recommendation: do not build until a pilot firm asks, then only a minimum: leave types and policy per client, accrual and carry-forward, holiday calendar, employee request in the portal, manager approval, days-taken ledger, and unapproved or excess leave flowing to LOP in attendance; an unrecorded entitlement must still show 'Not recorded', not zero. Needs migrations. — _Sources:_ docs/audits/findings-status.json PAY-24, PAY-26; docs/audits/2026-09-12-the-probe-pass/payroll.md PAY-24 (+ findings-status.json PAY-24 why); docs/audits/2026-09-12-the-probe-pass/payroll.md PAY-26 (+ findings-status.json PAY-26 why) (+1 more) — _Refs:_ PAY-24, PAY-26, PAY-27, rm/payroll-07
- **POST-B-155** · `payroll-statutory` · low · weeks — **Decide where employer-side NPS is recorded in payroll.** Payroll cannot record an employer NPS contribution at all (routers/payroll.py: 'Payroll holds no employer-side NPS figure at all'; nothing passes employer_nps_paise), so s.80CCD(2) relief reaches an employee only if the EMPLOYEE declares it (_apply_declaration adds it to employer_nps_80ccd2_paise), which over-withholds (the safe direction) and is invisible on the payslip. Recorded in OPEN-QUESTIONS A1 as 'not yet a finding anywhere'. Decision question: where is the contribution recorded: a salary component, a ledger leg or a payslip line? Recommendation: a salary component feeding a ledger leg, decided before building. — _Sources:_ docs/audits/OPEN-QUESTIONS.md A1 (last paragraph) — _Refs:_ PAY-22, A1
- **POST-B-156** · `payroll-statutory` · low · days — **Decide whether earlier-year arrears count in the s.80CCD(2) base.** PAY-22 fixed the wrong-base half: routers/payroll.py passes salary_for_80ccd2_paise (recurring (basic+DA) times months, the figure s.10(13A) uses; 10% for a private employer, 14% only for Central/State Government). Still open: s.80CCD(2) caps on 'his salary in the previous year', and arrears received this year are salary of this year under s.15 where untaxed earlier, so an employer who contributed NPS on arrears has an argument they count. The smaller recurring base is deliberate so an arrears month cannot enlarge the cap and under-withhold; settling it the other way only ever increases the deduction. Not blocking; recommendation: keep the recurring base unless a client needs the arrears reading. — _Sources:_ docs/audits/OPEN-QUESTIONS.md A1 — _Refs:_ PAY-22, A1
- **POST-B-157** · `payroll-statutory` · low · weeks — **Decide whether payroll may feed an employee's own ITR (DPDP consent).** A CA who runs a company's payroll here often files the directors' and staff ITRs; the platform holds each employee's PAN, finalized slips, Form 12BB declarations and a 24Q Annexure II (routers/payroll.py around lines 6070-6100) but nothing links payroll_employees to an individual client, so Schedule S and Schedule TDS1 are retyped. Using the employee's data in a separate client context needs the employee's consent under DPDP. Decision question: allow the bridge, and on what consent? Recommendation: build only with a recorded employee consent captured through the employee portal, prefilling from RELEASED runs only (a draft contributes nothing). Done when a fixture employee linked by PAN with consent opens the client's computation with gross salary, exemptions and TDS from released runs. — _Sources:_ tds_income_tax-11 Bridge finalized payroll into the employee's own ITR (Schedule S and TDS) — _Refs:_ rm/tds_income_tax-11
- **POST-B-158** · `payroll-statutory` · low · days — **Decide whether payslips are emailed to employees on release.** PAY-27 / payroll-24 remainder, deliberately deferred: the employee portal door (GET /api/portal/employee/payslips/{slip_id}/pdf, payroll-01) is the delivery channel, and a mail to an employee carries a different consent posture from a mail to a client's customer. No send_payslip or salary-mail path exists in email_service, practice_mail_service or notification_service (verified 2 Oct 2026), so the employee must accept an invite and sign in. Decision question: email payslips or stay portal-only, opt-in or opt-out per employee, and is web push through the existing PWA (apps/web/public/manifest.json, sw.js) wanted too? Claude's recommendation: one mail per opted-in employee linking to the portal with no salary figures in the body, sent through the single practice_mail_service.deliver door (so the PRACTICE_MAIL_ENABLED firm-wide switch, off by default, governs it), a per-employee opt-out, and a failed send logged and retryable without duplicating. Mail-provider and sending-domain choice is owner-parked elsewhere. Days of work once decided. — _Sources:_ docs/audits/findings-status.json PAY-27 what_is_left; docs/audits/findings-status.json PAY-27 what_is_left (cited via 2026-09-14-what-needs-both-of-us.md PAY-27); docs/plan/THE-PLAN.md - Track 1 section C row PAY-27 ('three more payroll report shapes, if wanted'); findings-status.json (+2 more) — _Refs:_ PAY-27, rm/payroll-24, rm/payroll-01
- **POST-B-159** · `payroll-statutory` · low · weeks — **Decide whether to build the Labour Welfare Fund deduction mechanism.** domain/payroll/lwf.py lists 16 levying states but MODELLED_STATES is empty: LWF has never been deducted anywhere (no payroll_slips column, no journal leg, no payslip line) and every run reports LWF states as named gaps. Migration 327's header (and domain/payroll/firm_rates.py) deliberately held the firm-recorded LWF table back until it can arrive TOGETHER with a slip column (employee and employer LWF), an LWF Payable account and journal leg through the posting kernel, and a payslip line, because a screen recording figures nothing reads would look like the gap is closed. Decision question: build the full mechanism (firm-recorded amounts with notification provenance, like firm_pt_slabs; periodicity monthly/half-yearly/annual with deduction months) now, or keep the named gap? Claude's recommendation: LWF is small money and lower value than PT, so build it after the payroll-29 account resolver and when the first state's table is in hand; mind that payroll accounts are still found by name. Acceptance: a Maharashtra employee in a deduction month shows LWF on the slip, payslip PDF and statutory handoff and posts a balanced LWF Payable leg, nil in other months; unmodelled states still report a gap. — _Sources:_ docs/audits/OPEN-QUESTIONS.md B2; migration 327 header; docs/audits/2026-09-01-payroll-can-it-run-a-year.md statutory-data table (LWF amounts, 16 states); apps/api/migrations/327 header; payroll-05 Labour Welfare Fund: amounts, periodicity, a slip column and a ledger leg — _Refs:_ B2, rm/payroll-05, rm/payroll-29
- **POST-B-160** · `payroll-statutory` · low · days — **Decide whether to capture s.89 and lifetime-exemption facts from employees.** Three facts come off the employee's own return or history and the employer never held them, so the engines refuse rather than guess: an earlier year's total income for s.89 relief (domain/payroll/arrears.py), and the prior gratuity (s.10(10)) and leave-encashment (s.10(10)AA) exemption already used, which are LIFETIME limits across employers (gratuity.py, leave_encashment.py). They stay caller-supplied inputs today. Decision question: leave as inputs, or add a declaration capture (for example through the employee portal)? No action is needed if left as is; the opening-position item would carry lifetime exemptions used. — _Sources:_ docs/audits/2026-09-08-what-is-left.md section 5; CLAUDE.md section 3b
- **POST-B-161** · `payroll-statutory` · low · weeks — **Decide whether to model the PF-election cases left out.** payroll-22's pf_wage_election.NOT_MODELLED names four cases that are deliberately not built: the higher-pension option under EPS para 11(3) as the Supreme Court read it in 2022 (a different joint option exercised with EPFO; EPS wages stay at the Rs 15,000 ceiling for every member), contribution on an amount the employer picks between the ceiling and actual wages (the election is all-or-nothing), whether EPFO raises the administrative charge on the uncapped wage, and the joint request held as a document. Decision question: build any of them, or leave all four named? Claude's recommendation: build none until the legal-position item for PF on actual wages is settled and a client actually asks. Reopen only on the owner's say-so. — _Sources:_ CLAUDE.md line 647 (payroll-22); docs/compliance/04-mca-epfo-esic.md §2 item 4; apps/api/domain/payroll/pf_wage_election.py NOT_MODELLED — _Refs:_ rm/payroll-22
- **POST-B-162** · `payroll-statutory` · low · weeks — **Decide whether to produce Bonus Act Form C and Form D.** domain/payroll/bonus_register.py computes the annual bonus debt and posts nothing (the provision is a journal the CA raises); Form C (Rule 4(c) of the Payment of Bonus Rules 1975, computation of allocable surplus/bonus paid or payable) and Form D (Rule 5, annual return) are named but not produced, and the 12 Sep probe expected the register to feed a Form C. Their layouts would need to be read by a person. Decision question: build the forms or leave them named? Claude's recommendation: only after the minimum-wage figure is supplied (or the gap is rendered as blocking), since a form on Rs 7,000 would carry the understated base. — _Sources:_ CLAUDE.md lines 565-602 (PAY-23); docs/audits/2026-09-12-the-probe-pass/payroll.md PAY-23 (+ CLAUDE.md PAY-23 bullet) — _Refs:_ PAY-23
- **POST-B-163** · `payroll-statutory` · low · weeks — **Decide whether to provide for gratuity liability at year end.** /api/payroll/statutory-position returns each employee's gratuity liability but no journal, year-end adjustment or note books a provision (no gratuity provision code in services/phase2_journal_service, year-end services or domain/reporting); the 1 Sep payroll walkthrough listed it as unfixed. Decision question: offer a CA-raised provisioning journal and a Schedule III employee-benefit note line, and on what basis, given AS 15 normally wants an actuarial valuation rather than the Payment of Gratuity Act formula? Needs the owner (and a CA's view of the basis) before anything is built. — _Sources:_ docs/audits/2026-09-01-payroll-can-it-run-a-year.md 'Smaller things, unfixed' (gratuity not provided for)
- **POST-B-164** · `payroll-statutory` · low · weeks — **Extend the challan-is-money link to EPF and TDS challans.** F4 (statutory liability accounts never clear; year-end shows a liability the client has paid) is built for ESIC and professional tax only: statutory_remittances (migration 365), domain/payroll/remittance_match.py and services/statutory_remittance_service.link_payment, a LINK to the bank-line journal and never a second posting. EPF is absent on purpose (its own record is epfo_ecr_filings, migration 335, which carries no journal reference) and docs/compliance/08's table still shows ECR 'Lock + ledger: no' and TDS 'partial', so a paid EPF challan and an unpaid one look identical on the EPF Payable ledger, and the same for TDS challans. Decision question: give EPF (and TDS Payable) the candidate-entry matcher with reason sentences and no auto-link that ESIC/PT have? Claude's recommendation: yes, EPF first, reusing the same shape. — _Sources:_ docs/compliance/08-government-api-access-the-verified-position.md sections 5.1, 5.2 (F4); apps/api/services/statutory_remittance_service.py — _Refs:_ F4, Track F
- **POST-B-165** · `payroll-statutory` · low · days — **Let a CA declare ECR months run elsewhere, including nil months.** Outstanding-month detection in domain/payroll/ecr_sequence and epfo_ecr_filings (migration 335) is drawn only from months for which this system holds a FINALISED run (months_known_from names the window). A month run on paper or with a previous provider is invisible and will still block the portal upload, and the revamped ECR (from wage month September 2025) makes nil returns mandatory for months with no contributory members, but a zero-member month with no run is likewise invisible; no nil-return handling exists. The limit is stated in the sentence the CA reads (an empty list means 'nothing outstanding that we know about'). Decision question: add an explicit record by which a CA declares pre-product months (filed elsewhere or pending) and nil months? Claude's recommendation: a small CA-declared month record rendered as declared and never silently counted as filed. Unsure how often a CA would simply create a run for a nil month. — _Sources:_ docs/compliance/04-mca-epfo-esic.md §2 revamped ECR, 'The honest limit is stated in the sentence the CA reads'; docs/audits/2026-09-07-market-research/payroll-primary.md section 4 — _Refs:_ task #122, MR-PAY-14
- **POST-B-166** · `payroll-statutory` · low · weeks — **Model s.17(3) profits in lieu of salary in payroll.** domain/payroll/annexure2.py carries a profits_in_lieu_17_3_paise field that is always 0 and reports 'not modelled' as a gap on every Form 16 Annexure; settlement.py and one_time_earnings.py also place such payments by reasoning only. Items such as compensation on termination under s.17(3)(i) therefore cannot be recorded or taxed through payroll. A build needs an input model (type, amount, exemption, s.89 interaction) and statutory reading first. Decision question: build it, or keep the named gap? — _Sources:_ docs/audits/2026-09-01-payroll-can-it-run-a-year.md 'Smaller things, unfixed' (s.17(3))
- **POST-B-167** · `payroll-statutory` · low · hours — **Resolve payroll_employees.pf_applicable defaulting FALSE in the schema.** Migration 014's CREATE TABLE (pf_applicable DEFAULT false) won over migrations 054/093 (DEFAULT true) and the production snapshot agrees, while EmployeeIn defaults pf_applicable to True, so only the API's explicit value keeps PF on and a raw insert reads as 'no PF' (which migration 477's election CHECK then refuses beside an election). The demo seeder (apps/api/scripts/seed_demo_firm.py) drives the API and is not affected. Decision question: change the column default to true in a migration, or leave it and keep the API explicit? Claude's recommendation: leave it unless a path that inserts rows without going through EmployeeIn appears, and check docs/schema-drift.md before any default change because the schema is compared against production snapshots. — _Sources:_ CLAUDE.md line 647 (last warning) — _Refs:_ rm/payroll-22
- **POST-B-168** · `payroll-statutory` · low · hours · UNSURE — **Review stored leave balances written by the old 12/12/15 default.** The attendance screen used to default casual/sick/earned leave balances to 12/12/15 and upsert them, and leave_balances has no provenance column, so rows already stored by that default cannot be told from a typed policy; removing the default leaves them looking like recorded entitlements. Whether any such production rows exist could not be determined from the repo. Decision question: add a provenance column (a migration) or accept a one-time review? First step: a read-only production count of leave_balances rows holding exactly 12/12/15. — _Sources:_ docs/audits/2026-09-12-the-probe-pass/payroll.md PAY-24 — _Refs:_ PAY-24
- **POST-B-169** · `payroll-statutory` · low · days — **Value a car used wholly privately in the perquisite engine.** domain/payroll/perquisites.py Rule 3(2) values an employer-provided car from the monthly table but refuses a car used wholly for private purposes, because Sl. No. 1(b) needs the actual running expenditure plus 10% of the car's cost and payroll holds neither; the valuation is refused, not guessed (a gap). A build needs two new per-employee inputs (actual expenditure incurred, cost of car) on the perquisite declaration. Decision question: build it, or keep the named gap? — _Sources:_ docs/audits/2026-09-01-payroll-can-it-run-a-year.md 'Smaller things, unfixed' (car used wholly privately)

## Accounting, ledger and reporting

- **POST-B-170** · `accounting-gl-reporting` · normal — **Decide client-lock treatment for payments, return saves, TDS, reversals.** The rest of the client-lock NOT_YET list needs a decision, not a call. (a) Purchase payments (create_purchase_payment, _create_foreign_payment, create_payment_core, create_foreign_payment_core): a payment moves Bank/Creditors but can carry TDS that lands in a return, and the receipts argument 'has NOT been taken'. (b) save_gstr1, save_gstr3b, save_gstr9: should saving a draft return row for an already-filed period be refused? (c) tds_workspace.create_challan / create_return: not a GST period, the lock's filing types do not cover it. (d) Cancellations and reversals dated today (cancel_invoice, cancel_purchase_bill, reverse_journal_entry, reverse_run, reverse_receipt, reverse_payment, bank undo): deliberately allowed into the open period so the ledger never disagrees with a filed return; adding the lock would refuse legitimate cancellations. DECISION QUESTION for each group: refuse, warn, or keep as is? Recommendation: keep (d) as is; decide (a)-(c) with a CA's view of each. — _Sources:_ docs/audits/2026-09-08c-the-phase-plan.md Phase 12a; apps/api/tests/test_every_dated_posting_path_asserts_the_client_lock.py NOT_YET (comments) — _Refs:_ ACC-12
- **POST-B-171** · `accounting-gl-reporting` · normal · weeks — **Decide how far server-built report exports extend beyond four reports.** Today only the ledger, trial balance, cash flow and ageing have server PDF/XLSX; P&L and Balance Sheet keep their browser Excel plus the year-end pack, and MSME tracker, budget, Schedule III, trend, AIS and time still build their workbook in the browser (SheetJS CE cannot style fonts, fills or borders; services/time_export_service.py already builds a styled openpyxl workbook and is the precedent). Separately a ledger PDF is refused past 6,000 lines (ReportLab lays one out at 1.2-2.8 ms/line against a worker killed at 120 s) while the largest control account measured is 5,659 lines, and a spreadsheet past 50,000; raising either needs a background/async render or streaming. DECISION QUESTION: extend server exports to P&L and Balance Sheet (and later the other workbooks), and raise the PDF cap or not? Options: (a) server PDF and Excel for P&L and Balance Sheet from the same function the screen calls, (b) also move the remaining browser workbooks, (c) async/streamed render for very large ledgers, (d) stop at four. Recommendation: (a) first, because P&L and Balance Sheet are what a CA hands a banker; (b) only after the owner reviews a printed sample ('is this what you would have formatted by hand'); (c) only when a client approaches the cap. The July 2026 search/sort/filter audit also asked for the P&L/Balance Sheet server PDF (the account-search half is a separate item). — _Sources:_ CLAUDE.md line 281; docs/plan/2026-09-12-compliance-and-craft.md - Track 3b; docs/plan/2026-09-16-the-redesign-plan-revised.md - Track 3b — _Refs:_ rm/accounting-16, 3b
- **POST-B-172** · `accounting-gl-reporting` · normal · weeks — **Decide receivable and payable write-off and settlement discount.** STILL OPEN: grep finds no write-off, bad-debt or settlement-discount posting anywhere in apps/api (domain/banking/exceptions.py calls a short settlement a 'write-off decision' and posts nothing); ReceiptIn has tds_paise and no discount/write-off field, so today the only way to clear a bad debt is a credit note, which wrongly changes GST. DECISION QUESTION: build a books-only non-cash settlement now or later, with which ledger account names (Bad Debts, Discount Allowed), and whether a CGST s.34 credit note is offered alongside? Claude's recommendation: build it after the demo as a receipt/payment of kind 'adjustment' posting Dr Bad Debts or Discount Allowed / Cr Trade Receivables through the kernel and moving paid_paise via the existing atomic settlement RPC (migration 235), NOT touching migration 278's generated outstanding_paise column (84 Python modules read it) and NOT reusing credited_paise (the s.34 term that feeds returns); no GST return figure may change. The income-tax bad-debt deduction (s.36(1)(vii) with s.36(2), recalled) needs a CA to confirm. — _Sources:_ roadmap accounting-09: Receivable and payable write-off and settlement discount — _Refs:_ rm/accounting-09
- **POST-B-173** · `accounting-gl-reporting` · normal · weeks — **Decide statement formats for partnership, LLP and proprietorship clients.** STILL OPEN: nothing in domain/reporting, year_end_statements or year_end_financial_service branches on entity type (only year_end_notes.py reads it, for one note); there is no Trading account and no partners' capital/current/appropriation presentation, so every client gets the company Schedule III layout. The live book has 3 non-company clients of 7 (1 LLP, 1 partnership, 1 proprietorship). DECISION QUESTION: build entity-aware layouts at all, and which presentation conventions (practice-based, not one statutory form)? A CA who does partnership finals must sign off a sample pack. Claude's recommendation: build Trading and P&L plus partner capital/current accounts (interest on capital, remuneration, appropriation) and proprietor capital/drawings after the demo, since non-companies are the majority of small practices; leave Ind AS Division II out. — _Sources:_ roadmap accounting-13: Statement formats for partnership, LLP and proprietorship (Trading and P&L, partners' capital and current accounts) — _Refs:_ rm/accounting-13
- **POST-B-174** · `accounting-gl-reporting` · normal · weeks — **Extend account keys beyond payroll to receivables, payables, GST and bank.** chart_of_accounts.system_account_key (migration 092, cash-basis Decision B) has a name-match fallback for charts seeded before the key; in practice services/coa_seed_service.seed_firm_coa sets a key on NO account and migration 092 stamped only some (both TDS accounts with tds_payable, all GST Input rows). So AccountResolver (domain/reporting/resolver.py), the posting kernel's _find_account (key branch firm-wide and unscoped, then ILIKE with .limit(1)), bank_posting_service, reconciliation_service (the 'Trade Payables' account has a NULL key while an unused 'Accounts Payable' pair carries one) and payroll still find A/R, A/P, Bank, GST and payroll accounts by name; the seed has ONE 'GST Output Tax Payable' and one 'GST Input Tax Credit', so CGST/SGST/IGST all post to one ledger and _gl_gst_movements can only report a combined output head. The July audit's 'fragile account resolution' and the cash-basis design's risk R3 (custom CoA names misclassify) therefore still stand. Decision question: after the payroll-only resolver is built, generalise the same one-vocabulary, client-scoped resolver to the posting kernel and the readers, which needs a migration that applies to production on merge and touches every client's posting? Claude's recommendation: do payroll first, then generalise behind a guard. — _Sources:_ docs/audits/2026-07-02-executive-product-audit.md section 4 'Fragile account resolution' (line 104); docs/CASH_BASIS_REMEDIATION_DESIGN.md sections 2.6, 3 (Decision B) and risk R3; CLAUDE.md payroll-29 — _Refs:_ exec-audit-s4-account-resolution, rm/payroll-29
- **POST-B-175** · `accounting-gl-reporting` · normal · weeks — **Import Tally sales and purchase vouchers with GST detail.** STILL OPEN: the Tally importer writes only customer and vendor masters although the parser already reads VOUCHER elements. Unlike accounting-03's historic vouchers, these current-period vouchers must feed GSTR-1 and GSTR-3B, so the owner must also choose which side of the 'carried over, declares nothing' line they fall. DECISION (decision 8, the Tally import rules): map Tally sales and purchase vouchers (GST ledger lines, party GSTIN, HSN, place of supply) into invoices and bills through the same core creators the CSV bulk importers use (routers/sales_invoices.py and purchase_bills.py bulk routes), no second posting path, per-voucher named gaps. Recommendation: do it after accounting-01 to -03's rules are settled and a real Tally export with about 200 sales and 200 purchase vouchers is supplied by a person; verify GSTR-1/3B totals equal Tally's own GST reports to the paisa, unmapped vouchers listed by number, re-import idempotent. Reaches the biggest population: clients whose books are elsewhere. — _Sources:_ roadmap gst-26: Import Tally sales and purchase vouchers with GST detail into books — _Refs:_ rm/gst-26, decision-8
- **POST-B-176** · `accounting-gl-reporting` · normal · days — **Post input GST to separate Input CGST, SGST and IGST ledgers.** Purchase-side posting puts all three creditable heads on one GST Input account (services/phase2_journal_service.py lines ~440-460, 599, 673, 773-787; bank-charge GST the same via migration 092's gst_input key); the sales side already resolves per-head output accounts by gst_cgst/gst_sgst/gst_igst keys (migration 098) with fallback, but the seeded chart (services/coa_seed_service.py) has one 1301 GST Input Tax Credit and one 2002 GST Output Tax Payable. Plan (accounting-15): seed per-head accounts, post each head to its own, change the GL-to-return reconciler in services/gst_return_service.py (reads the single gst_input key) to sum the three; existing charts keep working through the fallback; no backfill. HELD with accounting-11/-14 for the post-demo design review. Recommendation: build as planned after the demo, in the same design review as accounting-11 and -14. _When: owner parked until after the demo (posting-structure design review accounting-11 / -14 / -15); touches the posting kernel, so not a casual pre-demo change._ — _Sources:_ accounting-15 Head-wise input GST ledgers (Input CGST, SGST, IGST) — _Refs:_ rm/accounting-15
- **POST-B-177** · `accounting-gl-reporting` · normal · days — **Post sales revenue to a ledger per line or item.** Every sales invoice credits ONE revenue account found by a '%Sales%' search with system_key 'revenue' (services/phase2_journal_service.py lines ~89, 341, 506); purchase lines already carry expense_account_id but no revenue_account_id/income_account_id/sales_account_id exists anywhere, and the seeded chart has one 4001 Sales Revenue. A Tally-style CA cannot see export vs service vs rate-wise sales separately in the P&L. Plan (accounting-14): nullable revenue_account_id on invoice lines plus a default on the catalogue item, group the credit legs by account in the sales journal, no backfill, GST returns untouched (built from documents). HELD with accounting-11/-15 for the post-demo posting-structure design review. Recommendation: build as planned after the demo, in the same design review as accounting-11 and -15. _When: owner parked until after the demo (posting-structure design review accounting-11 / -14 / -15); touches the posting kernel, so not a casual pre-demo change._ — _Sources:_ accounting-14 Sales ledger per line or per item (domestic, export, services, rate-wise) — _Refs:_ rm/accounting-14
- **POST-B-178** · `accounting-gl-reporting` · normal · days — **Sequential voucher numbers for journals, payments, receipts, contras, bank entries.** Manual journals get a random reference (MJ-<8 hex>, services/manual_journal_service.py:154) and bank-posted entries BANK-<txn id> (services/bank_posting_service.py:640), while receipts already use RCPT-<FY>-nnnn; no voucher_no column exists in any migration (verified), so a CA cannot cite 'journal 47' or show a gapless series to an auditor. Plan (accounting-11): nullable journal_entries.voucher_no stamped per (client, financial year, voucher type) from an ATOMIC counter (not services/numbering.next_sequence, which reads max+1 and can collide); editable and FY-aware with a gap warning in the style of invoice_series; no backfill of posted entries (migration 251 makes them immutable, same choice as line_order); reference_no stays the kernel's dedupe key and bank posting's idempotency key. The A+ roadmap lists the same Tally-parity item. HELD: part of the owner-parked posting-structure design review with accounting-14 and -15. Recommendation: build as planned after the demo, together with accounting-14 and -15 in one design review so the posting kernel is touched once. _When: owner parked until after the demo (posting-structure design review accounting-11 / -14 / -15); touches the posting kernel, so not a casual pre-demo change._ — _Sources:_ accounting-11 Sequential voucher numbers for journals, payments, receipts, contras and bank entries — _Refs:_ rm/accounting-11
- **POST-B-179** · `accounting-gl-reporting` · low · days — **Decide automatic RBI/ECB exchange-rate feeds or stay manual.** fx_rates is manual entry only (a Partner types a rate; the write policy was tightened by migration 470), including the gst_notified (CGST Rule 34) and customs rate types. RBIRateProvider and ECBRateProvider (domain/currency/providers/rbi.py, ecb.py) are abstraction-only and raise NotImplementedError, so a feed is implement-and-register through the RateProvider seam with no kernel or report change. Nothing schedules a rate fetch or the AS 11 revaluation, by design (the closing rate is a fact somebody records and the entry hits the P&L). DECISION QUESTION: build RBI reference-rate / ECB fetching (which source, who confirms the rate before use) or keep manual entry? Recommendation: stay manual until a pilot client with regular foreign documents asks; if built, a fetch proposes a rate that a Partner confirms and never applies one unprompted. — _Sources:_ docs/architecture/06a-multi-currency-phase1-implementation.md item 4; docs/architecture/06e-multi-currency-phase5-implementation.md; docs/releases/2026-07-01-multi-currency-phases-1-5.md ('realized + unrealized FX accounting ... FX reporting'); docs/architecture/06e-multi-currency-phase5-implementation.md 'Rate providers'
- **POST-B-180** · `accounting-gl-reporting` · low · weeks — **Decide bill-wise references on journal lines (Tally-style).** ACC-13 closed with cost centres (migration 418) and a derived party ledger (migration 419) but names bill-wise references ON journal_lines as not built; document-level settlement and ageing are answered by receipt_allocations / purchase_payment_allocations and ACC-14's opening documents. It only matters if a Tally-migrating client needs bill-wise tracking of manual-journal legs. Owner call; related to the party-named journals decision. Recommendation: do not build until a Tally-migrating client actually keeps manual-journal legs bill-wise; party-named journals (separate item) answer most of it. — _Sources:_ docs/audits/findings-status.json ACC-13 — _Refs:_ ACC-13
- **POST-B-181** · `accounting-gl-reporting` · low · weeks — **Decide branded monthly one-page summary for CA to send client.** Platform-goal section 12.4 point 5: a one-page monthly summary (GST filed, TDS deposited, top five expenses, receivables ageing, three things to fix) that makes the CA look good and keeps them paying. Not built: the year-end pack and a trial balance shareable to the portal (migration 412) exist, but no monthly client summary artifact (grep finds nothing). Every number already exists in the reporting engine; it needs a layout, the server-made PDF rule (pdf_style), CA-confirm-then-send (D27: nothing auto-sent) and a portal home. Owner decides whether to build it. Recommendation: build after the demo as a CA-confirmed PDF reusing the reporting engine, since every figure already exists. — _Sources:_ docs/audits/2026-09-07-where-we-are-against-the-one-platform-goal.md section 12.4 point 5 (lines 960-963)
- **POST-B-182** · `accounting-gl-reporting` · low · weeks — **Decide cost-centre extensions: balance sheet, hierarchy, sharing, budgets.** ACC-13 / D29 built cost centres as a nullable journal_lines.cost_centre_id (migration 418) with an allocation report in which the unallocated balance is its own row. Named and not built: a balance sheet by cost centre, a cost-centre hierarchy, shared-cost allocation across centres, and per-centre budgets. None is derivable from what exists; build only on client demand. Recommendation: build only on named client demand, starting with the per-centre budget since budgets already exist. — _Sources:_ docs/audits/findings-status.json ACC-13; migration 418 — _Refs:_ ACC-13
- **POST-B-183** · `accounting-gl-reporting` · low · weeks — **Decide cross-client supplier intelligence (same GSTIN treated differently).** Platform-goal section 12.4 point 3 ('the part only this architecture can do'). Built since: firm-wide benchmarking from client_period_metrics (migration 417, /practice/benchmark), fee concentration, and cross-client entity matching (cross_client_matches, /relationships/cross-client). NOT built: the same supplier GSTIN coded or withheld differently across two clients (TDS section, ITC treatment), and 'one supplier's late GSTR-1 blocking ITC for six clients' (not derivable: whether a supplier filed GSTR-3B is held nowhere). The deleted /api/ai-insights/cross-client stub (invented names) must not come back, and bill-coding history deliberately does not learn across clients or firms. Owner decides whether a cross-client view with named-fact evidence is wanted. Recommendation: build only the derivable half (the same supplier GSTIN coded or withheld differently across the firm's own clients, shown with the evidence rows); never name a client outside the caller's scope. — _Sources:_ docs/audits/2026-09-07-where-we-are-against-the-one-platform-goal.md section 12.4 point 3 (lines 949-953)
- **POST-B-184** · `accounting-gl-reporting` · low · weeks — **Decide foreign-currency credit and debit notes (correction documents INR-only).** Multi-currency Phase 3 (migration 148: txn_currency, frozen rate, foreign amounts) covers sales invoices, purchase bills, receipts and payments only. credit_notes, debit_notes, sales_debit_notes and purchase_credit_notes carry no currency columns (checked: no txn_currency on them in any migration), so a credit note against a foreign invoice is recorded in INR and does not reduce the foreign outstanding (documented as a future additive change in 06e known limitations). A fix needs a migration per table plus the realised-FX treatment on application. Owner decides whether foreign-currency correction documents are wanted; multi-currency is behind three gates so exposure is narrow. Recommendation: wait until a client with foreign-currency invoices needs credit notes against them, then do the four tables in one migration with the realised-FX treatment and a parity test. — _Sources:_ docs/releases/2026-07-01-multi-currency-phases-1-5.md (Phases 1-5); docs/architecture/06e-multi-currency-phase5-implementation.md 'Known limitations'; docs/architecture/06-multi-currency-phase0.md Roadmap 'Future'; docs/architecture/06e-multi-currency-phase5-implementation.md 'Known limitations'; docs/architecture/06-multi-currency-phase0.md Roadmap
- **POST-B-185** · `accounting-gl-reporting` · low · weeks — **Decide Ind AS (Division II) ageing variant of Schedule III receivables note.** Only the Division I (AS) ageing tables are built (domain/reporting/ageing.py and public.schedule_iii_ageing, migration 303). Division II (Ind AS) splits the doubtful receivables row into 'significant increase in credit risk' and 'credit impaired', which is not modelled. Build only if a client reports under Ind AS; the Division II text needs reading first (see the Schedule III reading item). Recommendation: do not build until a client reports under Ind AS. — _Sources:_ CLAUDE.md lines 2616-2631
- **POST-B-186** · `accounting-gl-reporting` · low · weeks — **Decide party-named journals (journal naming a customer or vendor).** STILL OPEN: JournalLineIn has no party field; a manual journal on Trade Receivables/Payables cannot name a party, and the read side (migration 419, party_ledger.py, PartyBreakdown) only labels such 'manual' entries as unattributed. The design derives the party from the source document and stores no column on journal_lines. DECISION QUESTION: add a new journal document kind that carries customer_id or vendor_id and posts through the kernel (like the nine existing party documents), widening journal_source.ALL_SOURCES and its guard tests? Claude's recommendation: yes, as a document kind (consistent with derive-not-store), but only once a pilot CA reports needing debtor/creditor adjustments or customer-also-vendor set-off; no pilot is running yet. Verify when built: party statement and ageing change, control total equals the sum of party sub-ledgers, the unattributed manual row in party_ledger_as_at does not grow, tests/test_party_ledger_parity_pg.py still passes. — _Sources:_ roadmap accounting-10: Journal entries that name a customer or vendor (party-named journals) — _Refs:_ rm/accounting-10
- **POST-B-187** · `accounting-gl-reporting` · low · days — **Decide per-client default and company warning for cash basis reports.** Cash/accrual is a per-report `basis` query parameter (default accrual; toggle on client Accounting reports; Schedule III is fixed to accrual). The roadmap's open decision (default per firm, per client or per report; recommended per client, overridable per report) was never taken, and its India guardrail was never built: surface a per-client default but WARN when cash basis is selected for a company or tax-audit client (Companies Act s.128 requires accrual for companies; IT Act s.145/44AA). The only text shown is the banner 'Cash basis - management reporting only (IT Act s.145)' in app/clients/[id]/accounting/page.tsx (~line 1770); no entity-type test exists. DECISION QUESTION: where does the default live? Recommendation: per client, overridable per report, with the company/tax-audit warning. — _Sources:_ docs/QUICKBOOKS_ACCOUNTING_ROADMAP.md 'Priority 1' India guardrails and 'Open decisions'
- **POST-B-188** · `accounting-gl-reporting` · low — **Decide whether a posted journal may take or change attachments.** JournalEntryUpdateIn accepts validated attachments for a DRAFT journal only; a POSTED entry is refused with a sentence and JournalEditor sets attachmentsReadOnly = readOnly || isPosted. The reason is that prevent_posted_journal_modification (last defined in migration 274) lets a posted header change only inside journal_edit_in_progress(), which only edit_posted_journal sets, and that RPC rewrites lines and carries no attachments. Allowing it means replacing the posting kernel's own edit RPC (derive the new body from the migration that last defined it, never from memory). DECISION QUESTION: may a CA attach a document to an already posted voucher? Options: (a) leave as is (the editor's advice today is to attach to the reversal or to the source document), (b) extend edit_posted_journal or add a sibling that changes attachments only, edit-logged. Recommendation: (a) until a pilot CA reports needing it, since (b) touches the immutability rule's own mechanism. — _Sources:_ CLAUDE.md lines 1418-1435 (ACC-25); docs/audits/findings-status.json ACC-25; docs/audits/2026-09-12-the-probe-pass/accounting-and-inventory.md ACC-25; CLAUDE.md 'A JOURNAL'S SUPPORTING DOCUMENTS ARE A DRAFT-ONLY EDIT' — _Refs:_ ACC-25
- **POST-B-189** · `accounting-gl-reporting` · low · weeks · UNSURE — **Decide whether chart of accounts needs a real multi-level hierarchy.** The roadmap asked for a hierarchical chart ('groups and sub-groups are how a CA thinks'; production had 133 accounts with zero parents). ACC-24 was closed by deriving parent_group/sub_group from account_type/subtype on the Account Groups screen; coa_seed_service.STANDARD_COA still writes no parent_group, sub_group or parent_id, parent_id is writable but used by no report, and nothing rolls balances up a multi-level group tree on the TB or ledger. Unsure whether this is still wanted as a gap or accepted as closed by ACC-24. DECISION QUESTION: accept the derived two-level groups, or build real roll-up? Recommendation: accept unless a migrating CA asks for Tally-style nested groups on the trial balance. — _Sources:_ docs/audits/2026-09-07-a-plus-roadmap.md section 1 (lines 82-84) — _Refs:_ ACC-24
- **POST-B-190** · `accounting-gl-reporting` · low · weeks — **Decide whether fx_rates should get a per-firm shape.** fx_rates stays GLOBAL (USD to INR on a date is a fact about the world); migration 470 (security_privacy-19) made 'Partner-only' true of the TABLE (write policies ask my_permission('settings','write','Partner') and admit only source='manual'). The original audit finding proposed a nullable firm_id so one firm's manual rate can never replace another's (provider rows read-only to tenants, the manual provider reading the firm's own row first); the owner-questions file says that decision 'is still yours' and calls it real work across the table, PUT /api/currencies/rates, the provider and the revaluation, changing what a document booked last year resolves to. CLAUDE.md records the global decision as 'not reopened'. DECISION QUESTION: reopen it? Recommendation: leave global unless a firm reports another firm's manual rate affecting it. — _Sources:_ docs/audits/questions-for-the-owner.md section 'fx_rates stays global' (30-09-2026 addendum) — _Refs:_ rm/security_privacy-19, migration-470
- **POST-B-191** · `accounting-gl-reporting` · low · months — **Decide whether to build multi-currency Capability B (translation, consolidation).** Multi-currency is Capability A only: INR-functional books transacting in foreign currency (Phases 1-5, migrations 146-150). Capability B (presentation-currency translation where functional is not presentation, FCTR/OCI in equity, and consolidation) was deliberately deferred as a separate track; domain/currency/policy.py (lines ~53-56) refuses a non-INR functional currency, PUT /api/currencies/policy refuses to turn such a client ON (ACC-19), and there is no reporting_currency on the client. A client keeping books in a foreign functional currency cannot use multi-currency at all. DECISION QUESTION: is that client segment in scope? Options: build, or retire the plan and keep the refusal. Recommendation: do nothing until a real client reports a non-INR functional currency; then scope it as its own track. — _Sources:_ /home/user/caflow-ai/CLAUDE.md lines 4573-4594 (ACC-19); docs/architecture/06-multi-currency-phase0.md 'Scope decision', Roadmap 'Future'; docs/architecture/06e-multi-currency-phase5-implementation.md 'Known limitations'; docs/releases/2026-07-01-multi-currency-phases-1-5.md ('Multi-Currency (Capability A) Phases 1-5'); docs/architecture/06-multi-currency-phase0.md; 06e-multi-currency-phase5-implementation.md (+1 more) — _Refs:_ ACC-19
- **POST-B-192** · `accounting-gl-reporting` · low · weeks — **Split foreign-currency advances in AR/AP ageing advances section.** domain/reporting/party_advances.py carries an advance at its BASE-currency unallocated_paise with a currency label only: there is no stored transaction-currency counterpart, so no foreign-currency split, and advances stay out of the ageing's by_currency block (the probe's PUR-24 caveat: a foreign payment relieves AP at the bill's booked rate while cash moves at the payment rate, the delta going to Realized FX). Named as deliberately not done; a fix needs a stored transaction-currency unallocated figure. Owner decides whether it is worth a migration. Recommendation: leave as is until a client holds material foreign-currency advances; the base-currency figure is correct, only the foreign split is missing. — _Sources:_ docs/audits/2026-09-12-the-probe-pass/purchases.md PUR-24 — _Refs:_ PUR-24

## Sales and purchase documents

- **POST-B-193** · `sales-purchase-docs` · high · weeks — **Choose surviving fee-billing engine; retire fee_invoices readers.** DECISION (roadmap decision 11, with the surviving deadline table and the maker-checker policy): which of two live practice-billing systems is canonical and survives: fee_engagements/fee_invoices/fee_receipts (the /billing screen, /api/invoices, the fee-receipt endpoint in routers/billing.py, analytics revenue and profitability) or billing_schedules plus client_sales_invoices on the internal practice client (/practice/billing, billing_service, collections_service, AR, portal dues, executive dashboard revenue)? Migration 172's header calls it 'a product decision'; R3.9b fixed only the fee_receipts partial-payment bug. Consequences now: fee_invoices carry one flat 18% gst_paise, post no journal and no GSTR builder reads them, so fee income raised through /billing never reaches the firm's books or its own GSTR-1; profitability reads only fee_invoices so retainer invoices never appear; lead conversion creates fee_engagements but no billing schedule; the practice's own fee invoices are not yet a first-run checklist tick; and the time-billing item waits on this (practice_management-13). The fee_* tables are still read or written by routers/invoices.py, engagements.py, analytics.py (revenue and profitability via invoice_repo), lifecycle.py (conversion inserts fee_engagements), clients.py, the fee-receipt endpoint in routers/billing.py (fee_billing_service), invoice_generation_service, invoice_pdf_service, time_rates_service (fee_engagements.billable_rate), invoice_repository and engagement_repository (which feeds compliance_obligation_service), and by the screens app/billing, app/accounting/receivables ('Fee Receivables') and the client compliance page (fee_engagements). domain/hub (tiles, worklist), domain/cash_flow/forecast.py, domain/onboarding/first_run.py, app/reports/cash-flow and app/accounting/invoices were already moved off fee_* and mention it only in comments. CLAUDE.md's RLS matrix reports billing reads of fee_engagements, fee_invoices and fee_receipts as an open deviation (billing:read is Partner-only but every member can read). Recommendation (both source items agree): the sales engine through the internal practice client as survivor (the 2026-06 REVENUE_OPS_BRIDGE decision; the executive dashboard already reads it): retire the fee_* readers one at a time starting with analytics and /accounting/receivables, repoint /billing, have conversion offer a billing schedule, and keep fee_engagements because the compliance engine's engagement_repo reads it. Sub-question to settle: REVENUE_OPS_BRIDGE made fee_* a read-only legacy compatibility layer with no migration of historical data, whereas the roadmap item recommends backfilling historic fee invoices and receipts as documents on the internal client keeping their numbers (options: retire, keep as history, bridge a read adapter); retiring fee_* (data migration plus endpoint deprecation) is out of scope until explicitly approved, needs a migration, and dropping the old table is a production migration needing the schema-drift fixture refresh. Weeks. Verify: one fee invoice raised from the UI appears in profitability revenue, /practice/ar, portal dues, the firm's ledger and the internal client's GSTR-1, and nothing writes fee_invoices any more. — _Sources:_ docs/audits/2026-07-02-executive-product-audit.md R3.9b scope discovery (lines 3937-3946) and Final audit decision item 5 (lines 4524-4532); docs/REVENUE_OPS_BRIDGE.md 'Rule', B3-3 and 'Target vs legacy'; practice_management-13 Move the practice's own fee billing onto one engine and retire fee_invoices — _Refs:_ R3.9b, rm/practice_management-13, rm/practice_management-16, rm/practice_management-18, rm/practice_management-14, decision 11
- **POST-B-194** · `sales-purchase-docs` · normal · weeks — **Bill time through the sales engine, per entry, and mark entries billed.** Batch 5 built time_entries.billed_invoice_id (FK to client_sales_invoices) with generated is_billed, and billing_service.mark_time_entries_billed (services/billing_service.py:519), which has no caller outside tests. The Unbilled Work panel (components/time/UnbilledWorkPanel.tsx, GET /api/billing/unbilled-work, SQL function unbilled_time_summary, migration 451, which also priced time at entry) totals billable un-invoiced hours but nothing turns them into an invoice or marks them billed, so the figure only grows. The only time-to-invoice code is the legacy POST /api/invoices/from-time/{engagement_id} (routers/invoices.py:203, services/invoice_generation_service.py ~160-215; no screen caller) with four defects: one rate for all minutes (the last entry's rate wins), it falls back to the engagement's fixed fee_paise as an hourly rate, it drops the last day of the month (upper bound is 00:00 of the last day), and it never sets billed_invoice_id; it also hard-codes 18% GST ((amount*18)//100) with no SAC or place of supply, so even corrected it is not a correct tax invoice, and it writes the legacy fee_invoices table. DECISION QUESTION: does the practice want time-and-materials billing at all (and does its own fee billing move onto the sales engine, which is the canonical-billing-system decision, practice_management-13)? If not, the Unbilled Work panel should say it is a to-do list, not a receivable. If yes, replace the route: pick unbilled entries, create a DRAFT on the sales engine using the SAC 998211 path billing_schedules already uses, set billed_invoice_id in the same transaction, release entries if the draft is cancelled, refuse double billing, add the screen. Not visible in a demo (no screen calls the route). Recommendation: leave time billing off until the canonical-billing-system decision is made; then build as described. Until then, relabel the Unbilled Work panel as a to-do list. — _Sources:_ docs/BATCH_5_COMPLETION_REPORT.md sections 2 and 6 ('mark_time_entries_billed exists for future time-based billing; no current workflow calls it'); verified apps/api/services/billing_service.py:519, routers/invoices.py:204-230; practice_management-12 Bill time through the sales-invoice engine, per entry, and mark entries billed — _Refs:_ Batch5-time-billing, rm/practice_management-12, rm/practice_management-13
- **POST-B-195** · `sales-purchase-docs` · normal · days — **Decide payment provider and whether Razorpay is live.** Online 'Pay Now' (migration 110, services/payments/{mock,razorpay}.py, webhook with HMAC and replay protection) is built, but PAYMENT_PROVIDER defaults to mock (apps/api/.env.example; render.yaml declares it sync: false so the dashboard value is invisible from the repo) and the RAZORPAY_* secrets are dashboard-only. The roadmap's owner questions were never answered anywhere in the repo: Razorpay vs Cashfree vs PayU, and whether the firm has a Razorpay account (KYC) at all. With mock, the hosted payment page is a demo double, not a real collection path. DECISION QUESTION: which provider? Owner then supplies the credentials in the Render dashboard and sets the webhook secret. Razorpay is the only provider built. If the demo shows Pay Now it is the mock provider unless PAYMENT_PROVIDER=razorpay is set, so do not present it as a live collection path. Recommendation: Razorpay (the only provider built) if the firm completes its merchant KYC; otherwise keep mock and label the hosted page as a demonstration. _When: If the demo shows Pay Now it uses the mock provider; present it as a demonstration, not live collection._ — _Sources:_ docs/QUICKBOOKS_ACCOUNTING_ROADMAP.md 'Needed from the product owner' and 'Open decisions'; docs/MANUAL_TESTING_ROADMAP.md Phase 16
- **POST-B-196** · `sales-purchase-docs` · normal · weeks — **Decide rules for automatic, bounded client chasing and fee reminders.** Client-facing reminders are manual only (POST /api/sales-invoices/{id}/remind, one invoice at a time) and the nightly collections step (collections_service.flag_overdue_for_internal_followup) only writes a Timeline note and its own counter ('Nothing was sent to the customer'); the old automatic bulk reminder was deleted deliberately (unbounded, could not resume, no visible switch) and reminder_service.py still says it sends nothing and that 'future integrations will call send_whatsapp()'. Batch 4 shipped reminders as in-app/Timeline only and deferred portal/WhatsApp mirroring; the Practice AR/Collections screens offer no send at all. This is about chasing the practice's OWN clients for papers and fees, which is different from D27 (the sweep may not email a client's own customers: stays 'No'). DECISION (roadmap decision 9): the rule, the channels (email, portal, WhatsApp) and the trigger thresholds N. Claude recommends rebuilding it as: a per-firm opt-in switch in Settings, a per-client opt-out, a per-run cap with a cursor so one firm's backlog cannot starve the nightly sweep, a delivery log, three triggers (an obligation still 'Awaiting Documents' N days before its due date; a document request older than N days; an overdue fee invoice) using the firm's own wording (the 'reminder' email kind exists in domain/branding/email_template.py with no live sender), all sent through practice_mail_service.deliver under the PRACTICE_MAIL_ENABLED firm-wide switch (off by default). Weeks of work. Verify: switch off sends nothing; with 3 eligible clients exactly 3 mails on day 1 and 0 on an immediate re-run; a run capped at 2 resumes the next day. — _Sources:_ docs/BATCH_4_COMPLETION_REPORT.md section 3 and section 10 ('Reminder channels'); verified apps/api/services/collections_service.py:370-430,455-470; practice_management-05 Automated, bounded client chasing by email, tied to deadlines and open requests — _Refs:_ Batch4-reminder-channels, rm/practice_management-05, D27, SALES-23, decision-9
- **POST-B-197** · `sales-purchase-docs` · low · weeks — **Add Bill of Entry line detail for duty apportionment and UQC.** bills_of_entry (migration 389) holds the customs assessment as header figures only: no HSN, quantity, UQC or per-line duty. So the non-creditable basic customs duty and social welfare surcharge enter landed cost as one charge and are not apportioned across the Bill of Entry's own lines (domain/gst/bill_of_entry.py says so), and Rule 46(h)'s UQC cannot be produced for an import. Building BoE lines (a line table with HSN, quantity, UQC and duty, a migration plus editor and landed-cost feed on the migration 396 basis) would unblock both. DECISION QUESTION: do importers matter to the first cohort? Recommendation: defer until an importer client exists. — _Sources:_ docs/audits/findings-status.json INV-05 what_is_left, PUR-18; docs/audits/2026-09-12-the-probe-pass/accounting-and-inventory.md INV-05 (apportioning customs duty across a Bill of Entry's own lines; no line detail exists on bills_of_entry) — _Refs:_ PUR-18, INV-05
- **POST-B-198** · `sales-purchase-docs` · low · weeks — **Bill of Entry: deferred duty payment and s.27 refund not modelled.** domain/gst/bill_of_entry.py (PUR-18, migration 389) names two refusals rather than guessing: deferred payment of customs duty (Customs Act s.47(2) proviso with Notification 134/2016-Cus) and a s.27 customs refund of duty. Either would change what is owed to customs and the timing of the import ITC, so neither is guessed. DECISION QUESTION: does any importer client in the first cohort need either? Options: leave both refused (today) or model them as states on the Bill of Entry. Recommendation: leave refused and reopen only when an importer client actually needs one. — _Sources:_ CLAUDE.md lines 1347-1352 (PUR-18); docs/audits/findings-status.json PUR-18 — _Refs:_ PUR-18
- **POST-B-199** · `sales-purchase-docs` · low · days — **Decide additional charges (freight, packing, insurance) on sales invoices.** The July review named discounts and additional charges; discounts shipped (CGST s.15(3), migration 364) and round-off is opt-in, but there is no freight/packing/insurance field on SalesInvoiceIn or the editor, so a CA adds a catalogue 'Freight' service line as a workaround. CGST s.15(2) makes incidental expenses part of the value of supply and the tax rate on them follows the principal supply, so the rate and allocation need a decision before any field is added. DECISION QUESTION: add a pre-tax 'other charges' block taxed at the invoice's highest/principal rate, or keep the line-item workaround and say so? Recommendation: keep the catalogue-line workaround and say so in the editor until a CA asks, since the rate rule (principal supply) needs a CA's confirmation. — _Sources:_ docs/audits/2026-07-05-sales-invoice-ux-workflow-review.md Part 2 item 7
- **POST-B-200** · `sales-purchase-docs` · low · days — **Decide firm HSN rate history screen or delete the feature.** firm_hsn_rate_history (migration 181), routers/firm_hsn_rate_history.py (list, /resolve, create, delete) and api.firmHsnRateHistory in lib/api/index.ts (~line 6486) exist, but no page or component calls the client (grep of apps/web finds only the lib/api definition) and /api/firm-hsn-rate-history is in the unreached-endpoint ratchet (4 endpoints). So a CA cannot record that a code's GST rate changed on a date (e.g. the 22-09-2025 rationalisation) and the Decision D pre-fill hint (GET /resolve, 'nothing calls it automatically') never surfaces on the invoice line. Either add the screen (Settings, Firm HSN/SAC Library row action, plus a pre-fill hint on the line, CA-confirmed, never in GST maths) or delete the feature. Recommendation: add the screen only if CAs ask for dated rate changes; otherwise delete the feature and its four endpoints to shrink the unreached budget. — _Sources:_ docs/FIRM_HSN_LIBRARY.md 'Rate history (Decision D)'
- **POST-B-201** · `sales-purchase-docs` · low · days — **Decide invoice 'Viewed' badge: build open tracking or remove it.** The roadmap's invoice lifecycle was Draft, Issued, Sent, Viewed, Paid with viewed_at from a tracking pixel or pay-link open; only the engagement letter records a view. The invoice hub drawer renders a 'Viewed -' badge titled 'Open-tracking not enabled' on every invoice (components/invoices/InvoiceViewDrawer.tsx ~line 244), which can read as a broken feature; Sent is derived from invoice_deliveries and live. Enabling Viewed means a tracking pixel or pay-link hit recorded for a third party (the client's customer), which is a privacy/DPDP choice, so it was left off. DECISION QUESTION: build open tracking, count pay-link opens only, or remove the badge? Recommendation: remove the badge until a consented tracking design exists. — _Sources:_ docs/QUICKBOOKS_ACCOUNTING_ROADMAP.md 'Priority 2'; docs/SALES_INVOICE_UX_BLUEPRINT.md 1.5 and Batch 6; docs/audits/2026-07-05-sales-invoice-ux-workflow-review.md Topic 14; apps/web/components/invoices/InvoiceViewDrawer.tsx
- **POST-B-202** · `sales-purchase-docs` · low · days — **Decide invoice-level attachments (PO, work order) on sales invoices.** Blueprint section 1.9 / Batch 9: an invoice-level attachment list of {name, url} (a PO or work order), metadata only and never part of GST or journal maths. Not built: nothing like invoice_attachments exists in migrations, routers or the editor and hub; the 2026-07-06 density audit (F7) records it as 'Intentional, documented (Batch 9)' and it has never been scheduled. The reusable rule exists: domain/attachments (http/https links or an uploaded document id, closed scheme vocabulary) is already used by manual journals (138), bank transactions (259) and payroll declaration items (410). Sources disagreed on whether this needs a decision; whether a CA wants files on an invoice is a product choice, so the owner decides. If wanted: a column plus the shared validator on both create and PATCH doors, read-only once issued. — _Sources:_ docs/SALES_INVOICE_UX_BLUEPRINT.md 1.9 and Batch 9; docs/audits/2026-07-06-sales-invoice-workspace-density-audit.md F7; docs/SALES_INVOICE_UX_BLUEPRINT.md Batch 9
- **POST-B-203** · `sales-purchase-docs` · low · weeks — **Decide maker-checker approval before a sales invoice is issued.** The competitive design's stated differentiator was a practice-review workflow: a preparer creates an invoice, a reviewer or partner approves, then it issues. Today POST /{id}/issue is rbac('accounting','write'), so any Executive and above can issue; the approvals inbox (services/approval_service.py _SPECS) covers only user_create/activation, role_change, assignment_* and coa_*, and journals have their own accounting:approve step, so nothing gates invoice issue. DECISION QUESTION: should issuing a client's sales invoice ever require a second person (per firm setting, per amount, per client)? Per-person permission grants (migration 403) exist but cannot express a two-person rule. Recommendation: not before a pilot CA asks; if built, make it a per-firm opt-in with an amount threshold, routed through the existing approvals inbox. — _Sources:_ docs/audits/2026-07-05-invoice-workspace-competitive-design.md Pillar 5 / Phase 2; docs/audits/2026-07-05-sales-invoice-ux-workflow-review.md
- **POST-B-204** · `sales-purchase-docs` · low · weeks — **Decide PDF and Send for quotation, proforma invoice and purchase order.** Only three send buttons exist (engagement letter, payment link, sales invoice). A purchase order (the client sends it to a vendor), a quotation and a proforma invoice have no PDF renderer and no send path; re-checked 02-10-2026: routers/sales_cycle.py and purchase_cycle.py have no pdf/send/email and no per-document PDF service exists (there is one services/*_pdf_service.py per document kind: invoice, statement, payslip, engagement, year_end, bank_reconciliation, report). Each would need its own PDF service, so it is a real build, not wiring. Any send must stay a manual Send click (D27: never automatic). The owner-questions file records 'Not started - say if you want it'. DECISION QUESTION: do you want PDF and Send for these three documents? Recommendation: PDF first (a CA will print a quotation), send second. — _Sources:_ docs/audits/questions-for-the-owner.md section 3 'One gap you did not ask about' — _Refs:_ SALES-21, PUR-25, D27
- **POST-B-205** · `sales-purchase-docs` · low · days — **Decide price-list extensions: customer groups, other editors, importers.** Deliberately not done for price lists (migration 459): customer GROUPS (only a default list per customer), pre-fill in the quotation, credit-note and debit-note editors, and importers setting a customer's price list. A list is only a pre-fill for an invoice line's rate and changes no tax. Owner decides whether any of these are wanted. Recommendation: add the quotation-editor pre-fill first (the commonest ask) and leave customer groups until a client has many customers on one list. — _Sources:_ CLAUDE.md line 287 (accounting-20) — _Refs:_ rm/accounting-20
- **POST-B-206** · `sales-purchase-docs` · low — **Decide the excluded bill-history suggestion extensions.** Deliberately NOT done for the supplier-bill-history coding suggestion (domain/purchases/bill_history): no learning across clients or firms (the accounts differ), no auto-apply or 'accept all', no fuzzy vendor matching on extraction (the match is exact), the extraction path does not consult the history so extracted lines still arrive with a blank account, and no GST registration table. Recorded so nobody half-builds them; each is an owner call. — _Sources:_ CLAUDE.md line 117 (Deliberately NOT done) — _Refs:_ rm/ai-08
- **POST-B-207** · `sales-purchase-docs` · low · weeks — **Decide whether clients' own invoices get their own branding.** SALES-13: invoice templates and email templates belong to the PRACTICE (migration 126) and are applied only to the practice's own documents; a client's sales invoice and its covering email cannot carry the client's logo, bank, UPI or footer because clients has no such column or template store. Needs a migration and a client-level settings screen. build_sales_invoice_pdf correctly takes no branding argument today, because printing the practice's firm_branding/invoice_settings would put the accountant's UPI and bank account on a client's outward invoice (re-creating SALES-01 as a payment-instruction error). The correct build is a CLIENT-side store (a migration or a client_branding table), _SUPPLIER_COLUMNS and build_sales_invoice_pdf changes, and a client-scoped settings screen; the layout templates stay the practice's own. Owner decides whether clients' own documents should be brandable. Recommendation: build only if a CA reports issuing clients' invoices from this product with the client's own bank and UPI on them; keep the practice's templates off client documents. — _Sources:_ docs/audits/findings-status.json SALES-13 what_is_left; docs/audits/2026-09-12-the-probe-pass/sales.md SALES-13 (+ findings-status.json SALES-13) — _Refs:_ SALES-13, SALES-01
- **POST-B-208** · `sales-purchase-docs` · low · hours — **Decide whether invoice and bill create doors may invent unit NOS.** routers/sales_invoices.py (lines ~1000, 1908, 2031) and routers/purchase_bills.py (574, 1096, 1536, 1635, 1669) write ln.get('unit') or 'NOS', so a line nobody gave a unit is stored as counted in NUMBERS, and the bulk importers rely on it (SALES_INVOICE_IMPORT.md: 'the server defaults it to NOS whenever it is blank'). For an AI-extracted bill the editor flags the unread unit and its caption says the fallback applies, but CLAUDE.md (AI-01) records the fallback as a separate invention at a separate door, left for an owner decision; GSTR-1 Table 12 no longer invents a unit (it reports the gap). DECISION QUESTION: keep the default (Rule 46(h) wants a UQC on goods lines; a service line needs none) or store NULL and let the return report an absent unit like quantity, rate and GST rate (which are refused when unread)? Recommendation: store NULL for an unread unit and keep NOS only where a person typed it. — _Sources:_ /home/user/caflow-ai/CLAUDE.md lines 5395-5396 (AI-01); docs/SALES_INVOICE_IMPORT.md 'Unit of measure (UQC) is never a column'; CLAUDE.md AI-01 / UQC paragraphs — _Refs:_ AI-01
- **POST-B-209** · `sales-purchase-docs` · low · days — **Decide whether overdue interest gets a debit-note form.** Interest a client charges its customer is prepared as a DRAFT sales invoice through create_invoice under a service item 'Interest on delayed payment' (nothing posted, issued, emailed or scheduled, no compounding; migration 461). A debit-note form is not built because invoice vs debit note and the time of supply are the CA's decisions. Owner decides whether a debit-note path is wanted; recommendation: only if CAs ask. Recommendation: keep the draft invoice; add a debit-note path only if CAs ask. — _Sources:_ CLAUDE.md line 283 — _Refs:_ rm/accounting-22
- **POST-B-210** · `sales-purchase-docs` · low · weeks — **Decide whether to accrue goods received but not invoiced (GRNI).** Purchase orders and goods receipts post nothing and move no stock (PUR-25, migration 393); the expense, credit and payable arise when the bill is received. Goods received and not invoiced is a real period-end accrual but needs a GRNI account and a reversal path, so it was recorded as an owner decision rather than half-built. No tolerance (e.g. 2%) is applied to the three-way match and none is invented; no price variance is posted (INV-05a already costs at the bill's value); the first proviso to s.16(2) ('bill to ship to') is named, not modelled. DECISION QUESTION: build the accrual? Recommendation: wait until a client with material period-end receipts asks. — _Sources:_ CLAUDE.md lines 1537-1540 (PUR-25); docs/audits/findings-status.json PUR-25; docs/audits/2026-09-12-the-probe-pass/purchases.md PUR-25 (+ CLAUDE.md PUR-25 bullet) — _Refs:_ PUR-25
- **POST-B-211** · `sales-purchase-docs` · low · weeks — **Decide whether to ship an HSN/SAC master or keep firm-built libraries.** FIRM_HSN_LIBRARY.md Decision A (liability posture): PracticeSync ships no HSN/SAC content and asserts no classification anywhere a user can see it; every firm curates its own firm_hsn_library. public.hsn_master (migrations 036/175/178, ~204 seeded rows: 2-digit chapters, 4-digit SAC groups, common 6-digit SACs; the ~12k-row 8-digit leaf catalogue was never loaded) is kept but unread (routers/hsn.py and firm_hsn_library.py both say so). GST-26 and SALES-22 are 'open by documented decision, not a defect'; the probe pass flagged that their suggested fix reverses a written owner decision, and that its premise (the codes are public domain) is the point in dispute and cites a code comment, not counsel. Cost: a cold start where a new firm's lookup is empty; mitigations are onboarding step 3 (links the GST portal's HSN search), CSV import and inline quick-add. DECISION QUESTION: keep Decision A, or introduce an opt-in suggestion layer (offer master rows as read-only 'add from master' with codes and descriptions only and no rates, the firm's own entry always winning)? Also: why was the master withheld (rate accuracy, licensing, support)? Reopening needs an owner reversal and possibly legal advice first; the code is then easy and needs no migration (merge a second source behind the firm's own in routers/hsn.py::_fetch_library_rows), but sourcing ~12k leaf codes is the hard part, and docs/HSN_SAC_MASTER_MAINTENANCE.md is the only loading procedure (a new numbered migration per change, idempotent upsert stamping source/version/updated_at, retire never delete, leaf rates NULL unless unambiguous, refresh on GST Council changes and each April). — _Sources:_ docs/FIRM_HSN_LIBRARY.md 'The decision', 'Why this is not a contradiction'; docs/HSN_SAC_MASTER_MAINTENANCE.md (whole); docs/audits/2026-09-12-the-probe-pass/gst.md GST-26; docs/audits/2026-09-12-the-probe-pass/README.md section 9 SALES-22; docs/audits/2026-09-12-the-probe-pass/sales.md SALES-22 (+1 more) — _Refs:_ GST-26, SALES-22
- **POST-B-212** · `sales-purchase-docs` · low · days — **Decide which practice-to-client mails exist: fee-invoice and client reminders.** Settings offers four email-template kinds but only 'engagement' is live (KIND_IS_LIVE in domain/branding/email_template.py). 'invoice': the practice's own fee invoice has no email path (/api/invoices/{id}/pdf only downloads). 'reminder': send_compliance_due_soon now has a caller (practice_mail_service) but mails the practice's own staff in built-in wording, and no compliance reminder goes to the CLIENT. 'document_request' now emails the portal contact but only in built-in wording (its shipped default is fixed in a separate item). The Settings screen says so via status_by_kind. DECISION QUESTION: build a fee-invoice send and/or a client-facing reminder, or remove the inert template kinds from Settings? Mail is behind PRACTICE_MAIL_ENABLED (off by default). Recommendation: remove or clearly mark inert kinds until a sender is actually wired. Needs a decision per kind and a send path before the wording can be applied. Related: the bounded client-chasing decision (which would be the first live sender of the 'reminder' kind), and the engagement merge-field item. — _Sources:_ CLAUDE.md lines 1069-1084 (SALES-13); docs/audits/findings-status.json SALES-13; apps/api/domain/branding/email_template.py KIND_NOT_LIVE_REASON; docs/audits/2026-09-12-the-probe-pass/sales.md SALES-13 (+ findings-status.json SALES-13 what_is_left) — _Refs:_ SALES-13
- **POST-B-213** · `sales-purchase-docs` · low · weeks — **Decide which remaining bulk importers to build (loans, vendor payments, fees).** BULK_IMPORT.md 'Not yet covered (deliberately)': loans/FDs, purchase payments (vendor payments), firm-level accounting/fee invoices, and payroll monthly variable inputs. Since it was written, importers were added for chart of accounts, vouchers (accounting-08), opening documents, the fixed-asset opening register and payroll attendance (app/payroll/attendance), so only these four remain; for payroll 'monthly variable inputs' there may be no per-month input entity to import into (unverified). Add on demand; each should reuse the existing create endpoint and report per-row errors. Recommendation: vendor payments first (the commonest migration need), the rest on demand. — _Sources:_ docs/BULK_IMPORT.md 'Not yet covered (deliberately)'

## Inventory and fixed assets

- **POST-B-214** · `inventory-fixed-assets` · normal · weeks — **Build Schedule II componentisation as parent/child fixed assets.** FA-11 is partial (CWIP built, migration 397). Componentisation is the next and larger FA-11 build: where a part's cost is significant and its useful life differs, Schedule II requires depreciating it separately, and fixed_assets has no parent/child. Suggested shape is a parent/child on the same table, with depreciation changes; it needs a migration. Which part is a component is a Schedule II Part C judgement about a specific asset, so the CA records it. The owner picks the scope and order (the 14-09 note: 'tell me which of the seven you want'); the recommendation on record is to build this first. — _Sources:_ docs/audits/findings-status.json FA-11 what_is_left; docs/plan/THE-PLAN.md; docs/audits/2026-09-14-what-needs-both-of-us.md 'FA-11'; docs/audits/findings-status.json FA-11 what_is_left; docs/audits/2026-09-12-the-probe-pass/fixed-assets.md FA-11; findings-status.json FA-11 what_is_left — _Refs:_ FA-11
- **POST-B-215** · `inventory-fixed-assets` · normal — **Choose the next fixed-assets build (FA-11 remainder).** FA-11 is 'partial': capital work-in-progress is built (migration 397) and six capabilities remain, each needing a migration: Schedule II componentisation (the next and larger build), AS-10 revaluation, AS-28 impairment, the Part C extra-shift (NESD) factor, a location/custodian transfer log and a physical-verification history. This is an owner build-order call, not a document fetch, except for the NESD markings. Recommendation on record: componentisation next. Hazard to preserve: a shift multiplier folded into wdv_rate_percent would make schedule_ii_departure misreport every double-shift asset, so it must be its own column. The individual builds are separate items in this ledger. — _Sources:_ docs/plan/STUCK.md section 2 row FA-11; docs/audits/questions-for-the-owner.md item D; docs/audits/2026-09-07-a-plus-roadmap.md section 9 (lines 373-378); findings-status FA-11; docs/plan/2026-09-24-overnight-run.md Batch 5.1 (line 203-205); docs/audits/findings-status.json FA-11; docs/plan/STUCK.md section 2 (+1 more) — _Refs:_ FA-11
