- **POST-B-216** · `inventory-fixed-assets` · normal · hours — **Decide whether depreciation posting may be refused by a filed-return lock.** Depreciation posting and its twelve-month range runner both call period_lock_service.assert_open (apps/api/routers/fixed_assets.py lines 909 and 1836), which includes the 'a return covering the date has been filed' branch. So June depreciation cannot be posted after June's GSTR-3B is filed on 20 July, which is the very case CLAUDE.md gives for keeping filed-return refusals out of the posting kernel (it names 'depreciation charge'). The refusal is deliberate and test-pinned (tests/test_a_year_of_depreciation_is_one_request.py::test_a_locked_period_stops_that_asset_and_says_why) and CLAUDE.md calls it 'an owner decision to re-take, not a bug to swap'; an asset's acquisition asks the same assert_open. DECISION: keep the refusal, or ask only closure_reason (firm year lock or finalised client year-end) for depreciation, as the kernel does. Claude recommends the second, because a depreciation journal feeds no GST return and the filed-return branch exists to protect return figures; the owner takes it. Code path re-confirmed against the tree on 02-10-2026. — _Sources:_ CLAUDE.md lines 208-219 (A FIXED ASSET is one of those documents); docs/audits/2026-09-12-the-probe-pass/fixed-assets.md section 2 defect 2 (no finding); CLAUDE.md 'A FIXED ASSET is one of those documents' — _Refs:_ SALES-15, FA-04, probe-pass-FA-defect-2
- **POST-B-217** · `inventory-fixed-assets` · normal · months — **Decide whether manufacturing (bill of materials, production voucher) is in scope.** There is no BOM, production run, raw-material consumption, WIP or composite-item movement (inventory_stock_ledger.movement_type has no manufacture or consume), standard cost is deliberately refused (AS-2 paragraph 17, costing.STANDARD_COST_REFUSED), and no decision record declines it; the roadmap lists BOM under inventory completeness and the Form 3CD clause 35 yield is blocked on the same missing model. Everything else on the inventory list is built (godowns and batches migration 398, alternate units and reorder levels 409, FIFO 394, stock ageing 408, count sessions INV-08). DECISION: are manufacturing clients in scope. If yes it is a multi-week to months schema build (BOM, production voucher consuming components at the client's FIFO or weighted-average cost and producing finished goods at that cost plus recorded conversion charges, posted through the kernel) touching the costing engine and the stock position and ageing functions. Claude recommends out of scope until a pilot firm actually has a manufacturing client, since most of the practice book is service clients. — _Sources:_ docs/audits/2026-09-07-a-plus-roadmap.md section 9 (lines 381-382); docs/audits/2026-09-12-the-probe-pass/accounting-and-inventory.md INV-03; docs/audits/2026-09-07-findings/fixed-assets-and-inventory.json INV-03; roadmap accounting-19: Manufacturing: bill of materials and production (stock journal) — _Refs:_ INV-03, rm/accounting-19
- **POST-B-218** · `inventory-fixed-assets` · normal · hours — **Run read-only production check for stock-ledger rows crossing firms.** engineering-28 fixed the inventory posting paths, but whether any row was ever written across firms before the fix was never measured because nothing was run against a database. Run on production, read-only: select count(*) from inventory_stock_ledger l join service_catalogue s on s.id = l.service_catalogue_id where l.firm_id <> s.firm_id. DECISION: approve a read-only production query (or run it yourself in the Supabase SQL editor and give Claude the count). A non-zero answer means a data repair plus a firm filter on the reversal paths (reverse_sale_stock, reverse_purchase_stock), which would otherwise touch the other firm's item; zero closes the question. _When: Needs approval for a read-only production query; the fix is small once the count is known._ — _Sources:_ /home/user/caflow-ai/CLAUDE.md line 3118; engineering-28 Add a structural guard that every firm-scoped query carries its firm filter (residual: unrun production check) — _Refs:_ rm/engineering-28
- **POST-B-219** · `inventory-fixed-assets` · low · days — **Allow a mid-year opening position on the fixed-asset register?.** The opening fixed-asset register (migration 456, fixed_assets.opening_position_date) takes ONE position date per file and it must be a 31 March, because a reducing balance is one annual figure from the year's OPENING written-down value and a mid-year position carries part of that year with no opening figure in the file. Mid-year positions are deliberately not supported. DECISION: keep the 31 March rule, or let a file carry a mid-year position (the file would then have to carry the year's opening WDV). Claude recommends keeping the rule until a migrating client actually needs a mid-year position. — _Sources:_ /home/user/caflow-ai/CLAUDE.md lines 3440, 3443 — _Refs:_ migration 456, rm/accounting-18
- **POST-B-220** · `inventory-fixed-assets` · low · days — **Decide an Income-tax block vocabulary for opening-register assets.** The fixed-asset opening register (migration 456) stores the Income-tax block key as free text with no vocabulary, so block-wise written-down value for the IT Act cannot be validated or summed. It was deliberately not done. Decision question: add a controlled block vocabulary (and a migration) or keep free text? Recommendation: add the vocabulary only when block-wise depreciation is wanted from the opening register. — _Sources:_ /home/user/caflow-ai/CLAUDE.md line 3443 — _Refs:_ migration 456
- **POST-B-221** · `inventory-fixed-assets` · low · days — **Decide how a cross-registration godown transfer raises its tax invoice.** domain/inventory/location.py states that a transfer between godowns under DIFFERENT GST registrations is a supply even without consideration (Schedule I para 2 with s.25(4)) and REFUSES to mint the invoice, because the value is s.15 with Rule 28 (open market value of like goods, or 90% of the recipient's onward price, at the supplier's option) and which the client elects is recorded nowhere. A same-registration transfer travels on a Rule 55(1)(c) delivery challan and posts no journal. DECISION: record the Rule 28 election per client and prepare the invoice at the elected value, or keep refusing. Claude recommends recording the election and producing only a DRAFT invoice for the CA to review, in keeping with prepare-only, once a multi-registration client needs it. — _Sources:_ /home/user/caflow-ai/CLAUDE.md lines 3845-3859 — _Refs:_ INV-03a, migration 398
- **POST-B-222** · `inventory-fixed-assets` · low · months — **Decide whether to offer fixed-asset revaluation (AS-10) and impairment (AS-28).** FA-11 remainder: there are no revalued-cost or impairment columns and no way to book either through the register; only a policy-note string in routers/year_end_notes.py mentions impairment. Revaluation is an accounting-policy MODEL (a revaluation reserve and a depreciation split, so whether to offer it is a product decision) and impairment is a subsystem of its own. The sharpest narrow defect: after an impairment or revaluation journal the register keeps depreciating the unimpaired cost, because _compute_annual_depreciation charges on purchase_cost_paise. DECISION: offer both as value-only movements posted through the one kernel, offer neither, or only guard the defect. Claude recommends sequencing them after componentisation and, until then, naming the limitation where such a journal is posted against an asset. — _Sources:_ docs/audits/findings-status.json FA-11 what_is_left; docs/audits/2026-09-14-what-needs-both-of-us.md 'FA-11' (section 4); docs/audits/2026-09-12-the-probe-pass/fixed-assets.md FA-11; docs/audits/2026-09-14-what-needs-both-of-us.md FA-11 — _Refs:_ FA-11
- **POST-B-223** · `inventory-fixed-assets` · low · weeks — **Lot-to-bill link so a stock write-off knows which ITC head to reverse.** A s.17(5)(h) stock write-off reverses credit taken on some mix of CGST/SGST and IGST bills, and the inventory ledger carries no lot-to-bill link (the migration 398 batch has no purchase-bill column). INV-06 therefore splits intra-State by default (odd paise to CGST), writes the caveat into the register row notes, and accepts itc_reversal_is_interstate on the adjustment so the CA can say otherwise. The total reversed is right and only the head split is approximated. Lot-level tracking would settle it and is not built; DECISION whether to build it. Claude recommends waiting until a client with mixed-State purchases writes off stock. — _Sources:_ docs/audits/OPEN-QUESTIONS.md B21 — _Refs:_ B21, INV-06, migration 398
- **POST-B-224** · `inventory-fixed-assets` · low · weeks — **Offer weight and volume as landed-cost allocation bases.** INV-05 (migration 396) apportions freight, insurance and customs into stock cost by value (default) or quantity (clients.landed_cost_basis, with a purchase_bills.landed_cost_basis override). Weight and volume are named and deliberately not offered: they are the most accurate basis for freight, but service_catalogue holds no weight or volume, so it needs a column and a figure typed per item first (domain/inventory/landed_cost.py refuses 'weight' and 'volume'). The owner decision of 14-09-2026 recorded that no competing product ships it (TallyPrime and Zoho do value/quantity, QuickBooks Enterprise value/quantity/percentage, Xero nothing). DECISION QUESTION: add per-item weight/volume? Recommendation: no, unless a freight-heavy trading client asks; the first step would be the per-item weight column. — _Sources:_ /home/user/caflow-ai/CLAUDE.md lines 3763-3767; docs/audits/findings-status.json INV-05 what_is_left; docs/audits/2026-09-14-what-needs-both-of-us.md 'INV-05'; docs/audits/findings-status.json INV-05 what_is_left (+1 more) — _Refs:_ INV-05
- **POST-B-225** · `inventory-fixed-assets` · low · days — **Quantity columns are NUMERIC(10,3): reopen the 9,999,999.999-unit ceiling?.** INV-09 part 2 was closed on 24-09-2026 as a STATED LIMIT, not a defect: migration 188 declares opening_qty_units, stock_qty_units, quantity_delta and running_qty_units at NUMERIC(10,3), so one movement or running total holds at most 9,999,999.999 units of one item. Widening touches 22 declarations plus every index and generated column over them, for a limit no book in this product's market reaches; the alternate unit (migration 409) relieves the nearest case, such as cement bought in tonnes and sold in bags. Reopening is a migration and an owner decision; Claude recommends leaving it unless a real client reaches the ceiling. — _Sources:_ docs/audits/findings-status.json INV-09; docs/audits/2026-09-12-the-probe-pass/accounting-and-inventory.md INV-09; findings-status.json INV-09 closure — _Refs:_ INV-09, migration 188, migration 409
- **POST-B-226** · `inventory-fixed-assets` · low · days — **Record a per-asset ITC position on assets opened through the register.** An asset that arrives through the opening fixed-asset register (migration 456) carries no ITC position (no itc_claimable), so CGST s.18(6) at its later disposal NAMES the gap instead of computing the credit limb. Deliberately not done; a fix needs a per-asset field (a migration) and a source for the figure. DECISION: add the field, or keep the named gap. Claude recommends adding it only when a migrated client disposes of an asset whose credit was taken, since the gap is stated on every answer. — _Sources:_ /home/user/caflow-ai/CLAUDE.md line 3443 — _Refs:_ migration 456, FA-08b
- **POST-B-227** · `inventory-fixed-assets` · low · days — **Tie the opening fixed-asset register out to the ledger.** The opening register (migration 456, fixed_assets.opening_position_date) posts NO journal and states the register-versus-ledger tie-out as a named gap rather than computing it; opening cost and accumulated depreciation are not compared with the ledger balances. Deliberately not done. DECISION: build the comparison or keep the named gap. Claude recommends a report-only comparison that names the difference and never posts, the shape domain/accounting/opening_documents.reconcile already takes for opening documents. — _Sources:_ /home/user/caflow-ai/CLAUDE.md line 3443 — _Refs:_ rm/accounting-18, migration 456

## Banking

- **POST-B-228** · `banking` · low · months — **AA consent flow shape is written but nothing is built.** 05 section 6 records the shape only (no table, migration, endpoint, screen or model): follow apps/api/routers/engagement_sign_public.py for the request, tokenised link, expiry and audit, but the client's approval is captured AT THE AA and never on our page. Six refusals to honour if ever built (#105, #106): never render the approval ('I agree'), never touch a credential or OTP, a consent handle is not a consent, never treat ACTIVE as durable (poll and degrade into upload), never declare purpose 102, and DataLife is a separate clock that collides with the Companies Act s.128(5) eight-year retention so raw statements and posted entries cannot share a lifecycle. The consent artefact schema, FIU plumbing, TSP API and callback contract are deliberately unspecified because no partner exists. Build only if gate 1 reopens. — _Sources:_ docs/compliance/05-bank-data-and-the-account-aggregator.md §6 (6a-6f); CLAUDE.md 'Live bank feeds through the Account Aggregator' — _Refs:_ #106, #105
- **POST-B-229** · `banking` · low · days — **Add an editable CA memo/notes field on bank transactions.** Audit Part 3 B lists a memo/notes field on a bank line as missing and no tier item built it: bank_transactions (01-10-2026 snapshot) has no memo or notes column, and the passed journal's narration is fixed as 'Bank: <statement description>' (bank_posting_service lines 509 and 641). Size S. DECISION QUESTION: add an editable per-line memo that can carry into the voucher narration (as Tally users expect), or keep narration derived. — _Sources:_ docs/audits/2026-08-02-bank-module-quickbooks-gap-audit.md Part 3 B 'Memo / notes field' — _Refs:_ Part 3B memo
- **POST-B-230** · `banking` · low · days — **Allow a GST rate together with a multi-account bank split.** services/bank_posting_service refuses the combination ('A GST split and a multi-account split cannot be combined yet', ~line 391) because both decide the non-bank legs and combining needs a GST rate per split leg; the pieces (charge_gst.split_inclusive_charge, splits.build_split_lines) already compose. The audit says do it 'when a real case turns up' (Tier 1.2 follow-up). DECISION QUESTION: build it now (e.g. a bank charge line split across expense heads with GST) or keep refusing. — _Sources:_ docs/audits/2026-08-02-bank-module-quickbooks-gap-audit.md 'Known follow-up from 1.2' (~line 385) — _Refs:_ Tier 1.2 follow-up
- **POST-B-231** · `banking` · low · weeks — **Cross-check bank payments against GSTR-2B for suppliers without eligible ITC.** India-specific item 6.7 (size M), not built: no join exists between bank payments and the GSTR-2B reconciliation (the only bank/GST code is GST on bank charges, BANK-24). The audit calls it a report, not a subsystem: list vendor payments for which the supplier's invoice is missing in 2B or ITC is unavailable. DECISION QUESTION: is the report wanted? Recommendation: build it after the demo as a read-only report over stored 2B rows and bank payments. — _Sources:_ docs/audits/2026-08-02-bank-module-quickbooks-gap-audit.md Part 6 item 7 — _Refs:_ India 6.7
- **POST-B-232** · `banking` · low · weeks — **Decide foreign-currency bank statement lines: rate and FX gain/loss leg.** BANK-03 option (a) shipped: services/banking_service.assert_bank_account_is_inr refuses both import and post of a non-INR bank_accounts.currency, because bank_posting_service.post/_plan is INR-only and _create_journal would book USD 1,000.00 as Rs 1,000 (balanced, footing and wrong by the exchange rate). match_and_settle_multi is deliberately unguarded (it already carries currency and exchange_rate into the receipt and payment engines and is the model to copy). Option (b), posting foreign-currency statement lines, needs a rate per statement line (probably a migration) and a decision on where the FX gain or loss leg lands so the kernel's balance assertion holds. DECISION QUESTION: do foreign-currency bank accounts matter to the target clients? Recommendation: keep refusing until a client with such an account exists. — _Sources:_ /home/user/caflow-ai/CLAUDE.md lines 4560-4571 (ACC-03); docs/audits/2026-09-12-the-probe-pass/banking.md BANK-03 option (b); CLAUDE.md 'bank_posting_service.post is INR-only and refuses rather than converting' — _Refs:_ ACC-03, BANK-03
- **POST-B-233** · `banking` · low — **Decide whether bank-line GST handles zero rate, Table 3.2 and s.17(5).** Bank-line GST (domain/gst/bank_charge_gst.py, migration 382) deliberately refuses three things: a recorded ZERO rate declares nothing (it posts identically to an unmarked line, so nothing says whether the receipt is nil-rated, exempt, outside the levy or not a supply), it never reaches GSTR-1 Table 3.2 (no recipient state or class; the SalesTransaction defaults must not be filled in), and it makes no s.17(5) split. What it cannot compute is named on every answer (s.16(2)(aa) wants a supplier document a bank line does not hold; an outward supply with no tax invoice will not be in GSTR-1, Rule 46). DECISION QUESTION: reopen any of the three, or keep refusing. Reopening any is the owner's call. — _Sources:_ CLAUDE.md lines 1777-1787 (BANK-24) — _Refs:_ BANK-24
- **POST-B-234** · `banking` · low · days — **Decide whether cheque dishonour and customer TDS on PDCs are modelled.** The post-dated cheque register (post_dated_cheques, migration 460) deliberately does not model a dishonour: a cheque that bounces after conversion is reversed by hand on the Receipts or Payments screen and the bank charge is booked by hand (domain/banking/brs.py mentions returned cheques only as reconciling items). Converting a received cheque also passes tds_paise = 0, so customer TDS withheld on a received cheque is not handled by the register. DECISION QUESTION: (a) add a guided 'cheque returned' action on a bank line or a receipt (reversal_service.reverse_receipt plus posting the charge; audit item India 6.5, size M), (b) let a received-cheque conversion carry tds_paise, or (c) keep the manual reversal and the zero. Claude's recommendation: keep (c) until a pilot firm shows bounced cheques are a real workload; if built, put the action on the receipt or bank line so it reuses the ordinary reversal path rather than teaching the PDC register to own a dishonour. — _Sources:_ CLAUDE.md line 285 (accounting-21); docs/audits/2026-08-02-bank-module-quickbooks-gap-audit.md Part 6 item 5; migration 460 header — _Refs:_ rm/accounting-21, India 6.5
- **POST-B-235** · `banking` · low · days — **Decide whether to retire reconciliation 'Adjustments' into a posted journal.** BANK-05 left `adjustments_paise` as a Manager+ (banking:approve) figure with a mandatory 10-character reason, an audit row and a timeline warning (migration 355 CHECKs); 9c stopped short of making it a posted journal on purpose because most of what was plugged there is a TIMING item that belongs on the book side of the BRS. Now that bank_reconciling_items (migration 356, BANK-04) carries timing items, what is left in the field is 'genuinely journal-shaped', which the phase plan names as the condition for retiring it. DECISION QUESTION: replace the plug with a journal through the posting kernel, or keep the audited plug. — _Sources:_ docs/audits/2026-09-08c-the-phase-plan.md Phase 9 ('9c stopped short of the accounting answer on purpose') — _Refs:_ BANK-05
- **POST-B-236** · `banking` · low · days — **Decide: may a trusted bank rule post percentage split legs?.** BANK-11 step 3: the party half was built (migration 404, payee_type/payee_id on the rule) and the TDS half was decided as D19 (a trusted rule posts the payment and FLAGS the line for a TDS decision, migration 413; it never decides). Still out is a general SPLIT LEG: a rule cannot know a future amount, so fixed amounts fire only on identical totals and only PERCENTAGES generalise (e.g. 60% factory / 40% office on a bill that differs every month). domain/banking/rules.RuleSuggestion deliberately has no split field and a guard asserts that, so adding one is an intentional change to a rule that posts unattended. DECISION QUESTION: allow percentage split legs on a trusted rule, or keep trusted rules single-leg (a CA can still split any line by hand). — _Sources:_ docs/audits/questions-for-the-owner.md section 'A trusted rule may propose split legs and a party' (was section 11) — _Refs:_ BANK-11, D19
- **POST-B-237** · `banking` · low · weeks — **Emailed bank-statement intake to a per-client address.** STILL OPEN: bank data enters only by manual upload (routers/banking.py); no inbound-email code exists. The password-handling prerequisite (locked PDF statements, accounting-23) is built. It needs an inbound email provider (cost unknown) and a DPDP review because narrations hold third-party data; it is not a live feed and must not be described as one. DECISION QUESTION: build it, and pick the inbound mail provider. The same inbound-address infrastructure would serve ai-22's per-client document email and the receipts-inbox item. Claude's recommendation: defer until after pilots and decide together with ai-22 so one inbound mail provider and one sender-allow-listing rule serve both. Verify when built: a forwarded sample statement creates a pending import for that account only, duplicates are ignored, and an unknown sender is held for review. _When: Choosing an inbound mail provider belongs with the owner-parked mail-provider decision (owner parked until after the demo)._ — _Sources:_ roadmap accounting-24: Emailed-statement intake (forward the bank's statement to a per-client address) — _Refs:_ rm/accounting-24
- **POST-B-238** · `banking` · low — **Hold the written AA counsel brief for reopening only.** If gate 1 ever reopens (only via #130 finding a purpose exists or can be added), money should be spent once on the nine written counsel questions in 05 section 0a: eligibility is derivative (no FIU registration to apply for); the Department of Revenue trap; the ICAI trap; whether a partner FIU can lawfully declare a purpose serving ITS CLIENT's business; whether purpose limitation is breached by a by-product (books); the shell-FIU pattern and the FIP supply-side kill switch; whether the RBI 26-10-2023 reciprocity duty (an FIU must also be an FIP) binds an FIU holding no information; the supervisory cost of an acquired registration; and confirmation that continuing statement upload creates no AA-perimeter exposure (the cheapest question, protecting the status quo). No counsel is engaged and nothing is outstanding; this is a recorded hold, not work to start. — _Sources:_ docs/compliance/05-bank-data-and-the-account-aggregator.md §0a 'The counsel brief', §5 rows 3-4 — _Refs:_ #104
- **POST-B-239** · `banking` · low · days · UNSURE — **Legacy bank statements without an account cannot post and re-import duplicates them.** bank_posting_service._NO_BANK_LEDGER (a 422 at services/bank_posting_service.py:100-103) tells the CA to 're-import the statement with the bank account selected' for statements imported before bank_account_id became required. But domain/banking/dedup.hash_rows includes bank_account_id in each line hash, so a re-import inserts NEW rows beside the old unlinked ones, which stay unposted for ever; no relink or backfill path exists (checked). How many production bank_statements rows have a null bank_account_id is unmeasured. DECISION after measuring: build a statement-to-account relink (and re-hash) or accept; recommendation: measure first, and accept if production holds only dummy statements. — _Sources:_ docs/audits/2026-09-12-the-probe-pass/banking.md BANK-22 ('needs a backfill decision'); verified in services/bank_posting_service.py:98-103,175 and domain/banking/dedup.py:91 — _Refs:_ BANK-22
- **POST-B-240** · `banking` · low · weeks — **Loan EMI principal/interest split assistant on bank lines.** The A+ roadmap lists loan EMI splitting under banking completeness. A CA can already split any bank line across accounts by hand (domain/banking/splits.py; an EMI is the second example of a repeat the exceptions engine knows), but nothing proposes the principal/interest split from a loan schedule, and trusted rules deliberately cannot carry split legs (D19; RuleSuggestion has no split field). DECISION QUESTION: is a loan master with an amortisation schedule that pre-fills the split wanted? It is linked to the percentage-split-legs decision for trusted rules. — _Sources:_ docs/audits/2026-09-07-a-plus-roadmap.md section 2 (line 132)
- **POST-B-241** · `banking` · low · days — **MT940 / SWIFT bank statement parser.** Audit Tier 3.1 (size M): MT940 is what most Indian corporate net-banking actually exports, and no parser exists. Statement import today is CSV, XLSX and PDF with bank adapters plus the interactive column mapper (migration 314). The audit's own rule applies: write the parser from a real MT940 file, not from memory, and keep balance_agreement() and statement_check as the safety net. DECISION QUESTION: is corporate MT940 demand real enough to build, and can a sample file from a client be supplied? — _Sources:_ docs/audits/2026-08-02-bank-module-quickbooks-gap-audit.md Tier 3 row 3.1 — _Refs:_ Tier 3.1
- **POST-B-242** · `banking` · low · days — **OFX / QFX statement import.** Audit Tier 3.5 (size S): 'low priority in India; include for completeness'; not built (no ofx or qfx anywhere in apps). It would use the same safety net as other imports (balance_agreement, statement_check). DECISION QUESTION: build only if a client or bank actually produces the format; recommendation is to leave it until one does. — _Sources:_ docs/audits/2026-08-02-bank-module-quickbooks-gap-audit.md Tier 3 row 3.5 — _Refs:_ Tier 3.5
- **POST-B-243** · `banking` · low · hours — **Optional: send the drafted Sahamati/ReBIT purpose-code enquiry.** #130's remaining half is whether a purpose code can be ADDED for an agent maintaining the customer's own books. It is two questions with two owners: ReBIT owns the purpose-code taxonomy (api.rebit.org.in/purpose/) and Sahamati's Fair Use Committee owns only the bounds (proposals flow from FIU User Councils, a channel PracticeSync cannot join as a non-FIU). The owner DECIDED NOT TO SEND on 2026-09-06 because two of three conditions for asking worth fail. The draft email (to info@sahamati.org.in / services@sahamati.org.in, or via sahamati.org.in/contact) is kept verbatim in 05 section 2a and should be shortened to drop its Question 1, which section 2b answers. Reversing costs one email and a yes would reopen gate 1. — _Sources:_ docs/compliance/05-bank-data-and-the-account-aggregator.md §2a (draft enquiry), §2b 'What this changes', §7 'The one thing that would reopen it' — _Refs:_ #130
- **POST-B-244** · `banking` · low · days · UNSURE — **Pre-R2.11 imported statements cannot be re-imported safely.** Statements imported before the R2.11 parser fix (early July 2026) may hold balances stored as zero or mis-signed Dr/Cr rows; a corrected re-upload hashes differently and lands as a SECOND set of bank_transactions rather than superseding the old rows, risking doubled totals or a double-posted entry. Left as a standing by-design limitation because a blanket created_at cutoff would over-flag rows that were never wrong. If any real pre-fix statements exist, a product decision is needed first (exact-hash vs date+amount matching, replace-in-place vs supersede with an audit trail, CA confirmation) before building a re-import path. Moot if production holds only dummy data; that could not be checked from the repo. _When: Production state (whether any real pre-fix statement exists) was not checked._ — _Sources:_ docs/audits/2026-07-02-executive-product-audit.md R2.11.1 (line 268) and Final audit item 9 (lines 4547-4549) — _Refs:_ R2.11.1
- **POST-B-245** · `banking` · low · hours — **Ratify Account Aggregator route 3 as the permanent position.** docs/compliance/05 section 0a chose route 3 ('do not consume via AA') 'provisionally, pending #130' with no counsel engaged and nothing spent. Routes 1 (partner with a regulated FIU) and 2 (acquire a registration, e.g. a SEBI RIA) both fail on purpose: a purpose code is derivative of the FIU's own licence and none of codes 101-105 covers an agent keeping a client's books. CLAUDE.md and docs/plan/THE-PLAN.md already treat the line as CLOSED; the only thing that reopens it is #130 finding a purpose that exists or can be added. DECISION QUESTION: confirm this as permanent (drop the word 'provisionally' and, with it, #105-#107) or leave it provisional. Statement upload remains the base case either way and net-banking screen-scraping is never an option. — _Sources:_ docs/compliance/05-bank-data-and-the-account-aggregator.md §0a, §7 'The final position, in one place'; docs/plan/THE-PLAN.md 'Explicitly not in scope' — _Refs:_ #104, #107
- **POST-B-246** · `banking` · low · weeks — **Receipts inbox: emailed or photographed receipt, OCR, auto-match to bank line.** Audit Tier 4.2 (size L), not built. The OCR engine exists (routers/document_intelligence_v1.py with the Gemini vision path, domain/banking/vision.py) and bank lines already take attachments (Tier 1.8, but see the documents.uploaded_by defect), yet nothing ingests an emailed or photographed receipt or matches it to a statement line. An inbound-email address needs a provider and DNS (a human step; the same inbound infrastructure as the emailed-statement and per-client document-email items) and the AI rules in CLAUDE.md apply: a document is data, extraction stages a proposal, and nothing posts. DECISION QUESTION: build it, and when. — _Sources:_ docs/audits/2026-08-02-bank-module-quickbooks-gap-audit.md Tier 4 row 4.2 and Part 3A — _Refs:_ Tier 4.2

## Filing, integrations and registrations

- **POST-B-247** · `filing-integrations-registrations` · high · hours — **After the CA demo: reverse 'no registrations' decision and start wave 0.** DECISION QUESTION: once the first CA demo has happened, do you reverse the standing decision 'No registrations are being pursued at this time' (docs/compliance/08 section 1, 11-09-2026; docs/audits/OPEN-QUESTIONS.md section C; roadmap decision 7) and start the registrations, and how far? Options: (a) wave 0 only, the free self-service steps (Third Party Software Utility Developer registration, NIC e-invoice sandbox, the drafted enquiry emails, GSTN batch-5 eligibility, the CPC-TDS email); (b) wave 0 plus wave 1 decisions and infrastructure (ERI entity, India static-IP hop, GSP/ASP conversations); (c) also start the months-long gates (ERI Type-2, ASP contract, NIC production credentials); (d) stay prepare-only. Claude recommends (a) straight after the demo, e-invoice/e-way sandbox first (they are the only two statutory outputs software can complete end to end, because the IRP signs and there is no taxpayer signature), and holding (b)/(c) until a pilot CA asks to file. Context: D17 (owner, answered) is stay prepare-only, keep the filing simulation, and the OWNER starts registrations after a CA demo because GSP/ERI/NIC credentials are months of lead time and the demo justifies them; nothing has been applied for, so domain/filing_posture.py forbids 'applied for', 'in progress' or 'coming soon' wording and says direct submission is planned. No route exists for MCA, EPFO, ESIC or professional tax, TDS/PF/ESI have no API to be granted, and the Account Aggregator stays closed (no purpose code covers bookkeeping); ITR_SOFTWARE_PROVIDER_ID is unset, so generate_itr_json refuses by name today. Playbook is docs/compliance/07-getting-permission-to-file.md (waves 0/1/2; doc 08 supersedes it as the circulated paper; THE-PLAN Track 5 holds the commercial track post-demo); resuming should cost days because the eligibility chains and six drafted enquiries already exist. Audit market context: Zoho Books and ClearTax file GSTR-1/3B as GSPs and RazorpayX files PF/ESI/PT/TDS, so prepare-and-upload is defensible for a CA audience but not for SMEs. Each step is its own item below. _When: D17: owner starts registrations after a CA demo (owner parked until after the demo)._ — _Sources:_ docs/compliance/07-getting-permission-to-file.md §1, §4, §7; docs/compliance/08 §1; docs/audits/OPEN-QUESTIONS.md §C; docs/plan/THE-PLAN.md Track 5; docs/compliance/08-government-api-access-the-verified-position.md sections 1, 3, 6.5; docs/plan/THE-PLAN.md Track 5; docs/audits/questions-for-the-owner.md 'Four more were answered' (D17); docs/audits/OPEN-QUESTIONS.md section C (+7 more) — _Refs:_ D17, Track 5, THE-PLAN Track 5, D18, rm/market_and_trust-23, regulatory_access-01
- **POST-B-248** · `filing-integrations-registrations` · normal · hours — **Choose GST route: ASP under which GSP.** DECISION QUESTION: which GSP to be an ASP under (or none yet). GSP empanelment is almost certainly closed (two independent sources say GSTN stopped accepting new GSPs; no batch 6 found; the turnover bar was reported Rs 10 crore, then Rs 5 crore, then an uncorroborated Rs 50 lakh for batch 5) and a startup would not qualify, so the realistic route is ASP under an existing GSP: no empanelment, no net worth, a commercial contract (per-call price quoted 10 paise to Rs 1 from one undated source). Candidates ClearTax, IRIS, Cygnet and Masters India are all GSPs that also sell competing practice-management products, so the data clauses decide as much as price. The client sees the GSP/ASP name on their own GST consent dashboard (Manage API Access, 6 hours to 30 days, OTP, revocable; every consent emails/SMSes the signatory naming the ASP since 29-09-2025), and the signature stays the client's (DSC for company/LLP, EVC otherwise); per-GSTIN consent is a first-class product surface to design, not an implementation detail. The contract also buys GSTIN verification, taxpayer-status and return filing-status APIs (no free public endpoint), useful for vendor verification and s.16(2)(aa). Code seams: domain/gst/portal_service.py (GSTPortalProvider; get_provider refuses any name but 'manual'), domain/gst/gstr1_builder.py, services/gst_filing_record_service.py. Claude recommends deciding only after the GSTN reply and at least three written quotes with data-use clauses. _When: D17: owner starts registrations after a CA demo (owner parked until after the demo)._ — _Sources:_ docs/compliance/08-government-api-access-the-verified-position.md sections 3.2, A.1, B.4; docs/compliance/02-gst.md section 10 — _Refs:_ GSP, ASP, GST-25
- **POST-B-249** · `filing-integrations-registrations` · normal · weeks — **Decide and stand up an India-hosted static-IP egress hop.** apps/api runs on Render in Singapore by design (render.yaml; Render cannot move regions, and a paid host is an owner-parked decision), but NIC e-invoice production allows at most 4 whitelisted static IPs and the ERI production path probably needs one too. DECISION: where and how to host a fixed-IP forwarder that only filing and portal calls pass through, and who pays; Claude recommends deciding it before either application completes because it is a deployment line item with a long tail that gates two registrations, and not before the owner starts the registrations. If a Cloudflare custom domain is added for the API, TRUSTED_PROXY_HOPS must become 2 in the same change (docs/operations/edge-protection.md). _When: Owner parked until after the demo (paid host / host-tier decisions); also gated on D17 registrations._ — _Sources:_ docs/compliance/07-getting-permission-to-file.md Wave 1 #7; docs/plan/THE-PLAN.md - Track 5 — _Refs:_ Track5
- **POST-B-250** · `filing-integrations-registrations` · normal · hours — **Decide ENABLE_FILING_SIMULATION before the first real filing is recorded.** DECISION QUESTION: keep the filing walk-throughs on beside real records, or switch the kill switch off? render.yaml (line ~229) pins ENABLE_FILING_SIMULATION to "true" on the owner's 2026-08-29 reasoning that 'this deployment records no real filings'. Since then the CA-records-a-real-filing path is live (mark-filed writes the real ARN, date and the public.filings row that locks the period, from both compliance trackers), and docs/DEMO_FILING.md and CLAUDE.md say to set the flag to false on any deployment that records real filings because the flag is what removes the demo control. Decide before the first pilot firm records a real return: either keep the demo on for walk-throughs, or set false and let the screens stop offering the wizard. NOTE: the sources differ on where to flip it (one says the Render dashboard, one says render.yaml); the checkout pins a literal value, not sync: false, so a dashboard value may be overwritten on a blueprint sync and the reliable flip is a one-line render.yaml change Claude can make once decided. Leave it on for the demo, where the walk-throughs are the point. _When: Trigger is the first pilot firm recording a real return; keep ON for the demo._ — _Sources:_ /home/user/caflow-ai/CLAUDE.md lines 4825-4848; docs/DEMO_FILING.md 'The kill switch'; render.yaml line ~229 — _Refs:_ D17
- **POST-B-251** · `filing-integrations-registrations` · normal · hours — **Decide ERI route and which legal entity registers (Type 2/3, Quicko).** DECISION QUESTION: which route and which legal entity for ITR filing by software, before anyone applies. ERI eligibility is a company with net worth of at least Rs 1 crore, or a firm of Chartered Accountants, Advocates or Company Secretaries with a valid PAN (the CA-firm limb has no net-worth test found). Whoever registers owns the ERI id, the client consents and the statutory obligations, every taxpayer's return goes out under that entity's ERI id, and switching later means re-consenting every client, so it is a partnership-structure decision and not paperwork; if the operating entity is a private limited company, structuring through a CA firm is worth considering early. Options: (a) product company if it clears Rs 1 crore; (b) CA-firm limb; (c) resell via Sandbox by Quicko, which sidesteps registration, UAT and IP whitelisting but puts the client's consent with the aggregator's ERI, not PracticeSync; and separately confirm whether ERI Type 3 (offline-utility developer, 'pure software providers who do not themselves act on behalf of taxpayers', raised by external research) fits better than Type 2 (own software through ITD APIs, no bank guarantee on the sources reached). Plan in quarters (at least four serial manual gates: application, ISA/CISA due-diligence certificate, ITD UAT certification, production IP whitelisting); an ERI transmits a return and never completes it (s.140 verification stays the taxpayer's). Claude recommends reading the ERI page first (the facts item) so the choice rests on current type, fee and guarantee facts; all grades are [S]/[U]. The ERI application itself is a separate item. _When: D17: owner starts registrations after a CA demo (owner parked until after the demo)._ — _Sources:_ docs/compliance/03-income-tax-and-tds.md §1 (Eligibility, Buying instead of building, Costs); docs/compliance/07-getting-permission-to-file.md §3.4 ('The structuring decision'), §4 wave 1 #6; docs/compliance/08-government-api-access-the-verified-position.md section 3.3, A.4 'The structuring decision', D.1.3 #2 (+1 more) — _Refs:_ D17, ERI, Track5, Track 5
- **POST-B-252** · `filing-integrations-registrations` · normal · months — **Decide whether to build real GSTR-1/3B filing through the ASP route.** DECISION QUESTION (roadmap gst-29, B3: registration and money, follows decision 7): approve building real GST filing through an ASP? Today D17 stands (prepare-only): real_filing is False in routers/gst_workspace.py and only the filing-demo walk-through exists; it needs the read-only fetch item first. Claude recommends not until reads work on a sandbox GSTIN and a pilot asks for it. Hard rules that stay: the taxpayer's own DSC or EVC signing on the PORTAL (never an OTP box in this app), one explicit confirmation click per return (never batch, scheduler or blind retry), idempotency (record the reference before the call, check after a timeout), and a NEW endpoint with the simulation deleted, not repointed. Design constraints from docs/compliance/02-gst.md: per-GSTIN client consent (Manage API Access, 6 hours to 30 days, revocable at any time, SMS/email naming the ASP and GSP) must be a first-class expiring model that degrades gracefully; the signature is the authorised signatory's (Rule 26) or a GST Practitioner's (Rule 83, FORM GST PCT-05), so the static-export apps/web cannot DSC-sign and the option set is EVC-only (viable for companies only if Rule 26 resolves that way), a browser extension/desktop helper, or hand-off to the portal (today); and the flow must be long-running, chunked and resumable while lib/api aborts at 45 s and never retries. Verify when built: filed once after a single confirmation, a forced timeout plus retry does not double-file, and the filings row and period lock are written. _When: D17: owner starts registrations after a CA demo (owner parked until after the demo)._ — _Sources:_ docs/compliance/02-gst.md §1-§3, §10; README 'The rule that does not move'; roadmap gst-29: File GSTR-1 and GSTR-3B through the ASP route with one explicit confirmation per return — _Refs:_ D17, GST-31, rm/gst-29
- **POST-B-253** · `filing-integrations-registrations` · normal · weeks — **Decide: extend the portal-order handoff screen beyond payroll.** DECISION QUESTION: build handoff panels (a portal-order screen saying what to type in which box, with no credential or OTP field and nothing transmitted) and finish pre-flight ('refuse here what the portal would refuse there') for GSTR-1/3B, TDS 24Q/26Q and MCA? Track F3 is built only for payroll statutory: domain/payroll/handoff.py (EPF, ESIC, professional tax), GET /api/payroll/runs/{id}/handoff and components/payroll/StatutoryHandoff.tsx. Doc 08 s.5.1 lists handoff as 'no' for GSTR-1/3B, TDS and MCA too (routers/mca_workspace.py only comments 'handoff and evidence-of-handoff, never automation'), and pre-flight is only 'partial' for GSTR-1/3B, ECR and MCA. Claude recommends GSTR-1/3B first because the product stays prepare-only and a handoff panel is how a CA files today. — _Sources:_ docs/compliance/08-government-api-access-the-verified-position.md sections 5.1, 5.2 (F3) — _Refs:_ F3, Track F
- **POST-B-254** · `filing-integrations-registrations` · normal · weeks — **Decide: WhatsApp Business API channel versus click-to-open links.** The product's own tagline says it replaces WhatsApp, but today 'WhatsApp' is wa.me deep links built one client at a time (app/notifications/whatsapp, billing, engagements); the WhatsApp screen keeps the last 20 messages in the browser's localStorage (allowlisted in scripts/a-browser-only-screen-says-so.test.ts as convenience-only: if it ever becomes the place a CA checks whether a reminder went out it needs a table), there is no messaging_threads table, portal_messages.channel is only a field, and reminder_service.py says it must not send anything. A competitor (Vyapar TaxOne, ex-Suvit) offers WhatsApp document collection. DECISION: build a real integration or keep click-to-open. A real one needs a Meta Business account / BSP registration (owner-only), template approval, opt-in handling, a DPDP notice/consent review for messaging clients' customers, and either extending or keeping the owner's D27 ruling (the CA presses send; no automated mail to a client's customers). Claude recommends keeping click-to-open until a registered BSP exists, and rewording the pitch if it overstates. — _Sources:_ docs/audits/2026-09-07-where-we-are-against-the-one-platform-goal.md section 7.4 (lines 679-683); docs/audits/2026-09-07-a-plus-roadmap.md section 10 (lines 395-397); docs/PRACTICESYNC_AUDIT_v1.md sections 0-1 (Portal: WhatsApp Partial); docs/audits/2026-07-02-executive-product-audit.md R3.4 cites the same — _Refs:_ D27
- **POST-B-255** · `filing-integrations-registrations` · normal — **Real filing through the software: preconditions and what stays not built.** Filing from the app is intended but out of scope until asked for by name (D17: stay prepare-only), and is blocked on the registrations. Nine preconditions for the first real filing endpoint: (1) idempotency, writing intent and reference BEFORE the call and checking status after a timeout, never a blind retry, and lib/api aborts at 45 s so a filing call must not live behind that abort; (2) an explicit confirmation click per return, never batch, scheduler or auto-resubmit; (3) the signature stays on the portal (no EVC OTP field or stored portal password; follow routers/engagement_sign_public.py's tokenised hand-off); (4) consent and expiry as model objects; (5) the audit trail written in the same transaction, unswallowed; (6) delete the services/filing_demo flow for that return (gstr1, gstr3b, gstr9, itr, tds_return, pf_ecr, esi, mca), never repoint it; (7) ENABLE_FILING_SIMULATION=false on any deployment that records a real filing; (8) wire get_provider()'s switch in the same commit that adds a provider; (9) a real filing writes the filings row that journal_period_lock_reason reads, via services/gst_filing_record_service.py from the portal's response, with no second table. Revocation of a licence, ERI registration, NIC client-id or client consent must always leave the manual path working. _When: D17: owner starts registrations after a CA demo (owner parked until after the demo)._ — _Sources:_ /home/user/caflow-ai/CLAUDE.md lines 4774-4848; docs/compliance/07-getting-permission-to-file.md §6, §5 (last paragraph) — _Refs:_ D17, GST-31
- **POST-B-256** · `filing-integrations-registrations` · low · weeks — **Decide whether to model XBRL applicability and taxonomy choice.** DECISION: is it worth building? AOC-4 XBRL is mandatory for listed companies and their Indian subsidiaries, paid-up capital of at least Rs 5 crore, turnover of at least Rs 100 crore, or any Ind AS company (NBFCs/HFCs/banks/insurers exempt), and once a company files in XBRL (even voluntarily) all later filings must be XBRL [P]; two AOC-4 taxonomies exist (C&I for Indian GAAP, Ind AS) and CRA-4 uses a Costing taxonomy. The product only gates on 'is a Companies Act company' on the xbrl page and generates a single MCA_2023 package; it neither tests applicability nor chooses a taxonomy. Options: build an applicability test plus taxonomy picker (depends on the MCA validator/taxonomy download), or keep the single package and say so on the screen; Claude recommends deferring until a company client needs it. — _Sources:_ docs/compliance/04-mca-epfo-esic.md §1 'XBRL' (who must file; two taxonomies)
- **POST-B-257** · `filing-integrations-registrations` · low · weeks — **Decide: build eSign, batch signing and a UDIN/certificate register?.** Only dsc_records (migration 014) and the DSC-tracker page exist. UDIN is stored for the year-end engagement only (domain/udin.py, migration 290, year_end_pdf_service) and is never generated here. Doc 5 s.21 / TRD 5.5 specify signatures, signing_batches and udin_records tables and an eSign / batch DSC service; eSign needs an ESP, and the portal-side signing posture in CLAUDE.md ('signing happens on the portal, never in our UI') constrains the design. DECISION: build a UDIN/certificate register for 3CD, 15CA/CB and other certifications now, or leave it until the ERI/GSP registrations (docs/compliance/07). — _Sources:_ docs/PRACTICESYNC_AUDIT_v1.md sections 1-2 (Signing / UDIN Partial)
- **POST-B-258** · `filing-integrations-registrations` · low · days — **Decide: record which director signs which MCA form (DSC join).** DECISION QUESTION: build a record and warning for 'which director signs which MCA form'? A DSC register exists (public.dsc_records, migrations 014 and 120; routers/dsc.py with a renew path; the /settings/dsc-tracker screen; expiry within 60 days on /risks) and mca_directors exists, but dsc_records holds only holder_name, PAN, class, purpose, expiry and token serial, with no client, director/DIN or form link. The fact of which director signs which form is a human-held decision nobody records (like vendors.msme_status), so no honest 'your signatory's DSC expires before this due date' warning can be raised, and an expired or unassociated DSC cannot be flagged against a stalled MCA filing, the commonest cause of one; the DSC belongs to the CLIENT's director (Class 3 roughly Rs 850-4,500, the client's cost). Options: (a) a migration adding client/DIN/form columns or a link table plus a deadline cross-check, (b) leave it. Claude recommends (a), small and nullable with no backfill, after the 'who signs what' reading item is settled. — _Sources:_ docs/compliance/08-government-api-access-the-verified-position.md sections 1, 3.5, 5.2 (F7); routers/mca_workspace.py comment; docs/audits/OPEN-QUESTIONS.md B12; docs/audits/WHERE-WE-STOPPED.md lines 525-532 (Track F7, 'the one thing genuinely NOT built') — _Refs:_ F7, Track F, B12

## Marketing, commercial and legal

- **POST-B-259** · `marketing-commercial-legal` · high · weeks — **Publish a real Privacy Notice and Terms of Service; footer links dead-end.** apps/marketing/components/SiteFooter.tsx points both 'Privacy' and 'Terms' at /support (checked) and neither app has a privacy or terms page (marketing routes are demo, pricing, products, resources, support, access). DPDP Rule 3 (in force 13-05-2027, medium confidence) requires a STANDALONE notice in plain language itemising the data, the specific purposes and links to withdraw consent, exercise rights and complain; the IT SPDI Rules 2011 privacy-policy duty (recalled; counsel to confirm) may apply now, and PracticeSync is a Data Fiduciary in its own right for firm staff accounts, billing contacts, telemetry, support mail and the demo form (name, firm, email). OWNER: name the contracting legal entity and a grievance/security contact and retain Indian technology-law counsel (two quotes, 2-4 weeks); the Terms cover service scope ('the software prepares, the CA files and stays responsible'), liability limit, acceptable use, data return on exit and governing law. CLAUDE THEN: drafts the notice around what the code does (data categories, purposes, retention per domain/dpdp/retention.py, sub-processors, rights, grievance), builds /privacy and /terms (about a day), points the footer, adds a marketing guard so a legal footer link can never equal /support, adds sitemap and claims-ledger entries (the ledger fails the required check otherwise), and files counsel's sign-off under docs/compliance. Site analytics were refused until the notice exists, and a funnel counter would also need a migration. — _Sources:_ /home/user/caflow-ai/CLAUDE.md line 5443 (market_and_trust-17/-29); docs/compliance/06-data-protection-dpdp.md §5 (Rule 3), §3; apps/marketing/components/SiteFooter.tsx; CLAUDE.md (frontend_ux-04/-12/-28 bullet); docs/marketing/2026-09-16-website-redesign-plan.md (marketing site scope); apps/marketing/components/SiteFooter.tsx lines 94-99; CLAUDE.md market_and_trust-17/-29 bullet (+2 more) — _Refs:_ rm/market_and_trust-17, rm/market_and_trust-29, DPDP Rule 3, market_and_trust-17/-29, rm/market_and_trust-01, rm/security_privacy-07
- **POST-B-260** · `marketing-commercial-legal` · normal · days — **Build a /security trust page, security.txt and incident process.** The site has six homepage security cards and four at /products#security but no security page, no /.well-known/security.txt (apps/marketing/public holds only _redirects, hero and og.jpg), no disclosure contact and no incident process. Ask: name a monitored security contact (ties to the legal-entity decision) and an incident owner; Claude then publishes a page in the site's honest style stating what is enforced (tenant isolation at two layers, append-only ledger, role and per-person access) and what is not (no certifications, pen-test status, MFA scope), plus security.txt and a written incident runbook naming the 6-hour step. Basis: CERT-In's 2022 directions reportedly require incident reporting within 6 hours and 180-day log retention within India for service providers and body corporates (searched, medium-high); applicability, and the fact API logs run in Singapore, are for counsel. Any new capability sentence must also enter the marketing claims ledger. — _Sources:_ market_and_trust-19 Build a trust page: what is enforced, how to report a flaw, and what happens in an incident — _Refs:_ rm/market_and_trust-19, rm/ops-29
- **POST-B-261** · `marketing-commercial-legal` · normal · weeks — **Build the data-principal rights process (DPDP Rule 14).** Rule 14 requires the means of exercising access, correction, erasure, grievance redressal and nomination to be PUBLISHED; nothing in apps/ implements an intake, a grievance contact or a request log (grep for grievance, data-principal and DSAR finds nothing relevant). Decide who answers: the CA firm is the fiduciary for client data (PracticeSync assists as processor), PracticeSync itself for staff accounts, billing contacts, telemetry and support mail. Erasure answers already exist per path as refusals with a statute and date (payroll employee, customer, vendor, bank account) via domain/dpdp/retention.py; the missing piece is intake, tracking and publishing. The grievance time limit is unconfirmed (see the DPDP primary-text reading item). — _Sources:_ docs/compliance/06-data-protection-dpdp.md §5 (Rule 14), §5b — _Refs:_ DPDP Rule 14
- **POST-B-262** · `marketing-commercial-legal` · normal · months — **Decide the design-partner pilot: recruit firms, protocol, GA decision.** The July audit's verdict was 'do not GA; close Tier 0-1, re-audit on a real database, then pilot with 3-5 supervised design-partner firms and measure filing accuracy and time saved'. The engineering tiers closed, but no document records a pilot, its protocol or a GA go/no-go; production holds seven fabricated clients (migration 337 comment) and there is no adoption evidence. DECISION (roadmap decision 10): recruit 3 to 5 small practices in one city (firms running 15-60 SME clients on Tally), set a founding price (do not publish a list price until Tally import and filing gaps close), name a practising CA adviser who runs weekly calls, and agree written success criteria with only measured figures reported (domain/onboarding/first_run.py holds the outside-CA rehearsal protocol). Gate: no real client data before the processor agreement (decision 3) and a restore drill (decision 2) exist; check ICAI rules on members endorsing vendors before naming anyone. Claude's follow-on work: relax the marketing guard (which bans customer counts and testimonials) only for evidence-backed entries and record the launch decision. Expect 3 to 9 months to a first named reference. _When: Pilot recruitment follows the demo._ — _Sources:_ docs/audits/2026-07-02-executive-product-audit.md section 1 (lines 29-34) and section 12 (lines 303-312); market_and_trust-26 Run three to five design-partner pilots and publish real, named references — _Refs:_ exec-audit-s12-pilot, rm/market_and_trust-26, rm/accounting-26, rm/payroll-12, D17
- **POST-B-263** · `marketing-commercial-legal` · normal · weeks — **Decide the lawful basis and build the consent path (superset of s.7(a)).** Whether a CA's document-request checklist fits DPDP s.7(a) 'voluntarily provided' is actively disputed ([S]): one reading requires the principal to have initiated the sharing unprompted. 06's recommendation: do not architect on s.7(a); build a consent path (s.6: free, specific, informed, unconditional, unambiguous, a clear affirmative action, withdrawal as easy as giving) since it is a superset. No consent capture or withdrawal exists in the product. DECISION: whose consent it is (the firm as fiduciary, captured through PracticeSync), what the client sees and how withdrawal works; read together with the counsel question on s.7(a). — _Sources:_ docs/compliance/06-data-protection-dpdp.md §4 — _Refs:_ DPDP s.6, DPDP s.7(a)
- **POST-B-264** · `marketing-commercial-legal` · normal · hours — **Decide the real offer and fix pricing-page promises (free trial, plan contents).** apps/marketing/app/(site)/pricing/page.tsx states Solo Rs 1,499/month (1 user, up to 25 clients), Practice Rs 3,999/month (up to 5 users, 150 clients, 'save ~2 months on annual'), Firm 'Custom' and 'Free trial, no credit card' on every plan, and calls the pricing 'indicative'. Nothing in apps/api enforces a user or client limit, a plan, a trial expiry or an annual option. The 'free trial / no credit card / Start free trial' wording is still live in the pricing metadata, hero subtitle, 'Free trial, no card' tile, FAQ, Solo/Practice buttons, the home page (page.tsx:317), the support FAQ and the access page, and 'no credit card' is not in the claims ledger; 'Audit logs' is listed as a Firm-only feature though every tenant has one; the support page promises a 'dedicated onboarding specialist' for Practice and Firm while pricing lists only 'Dedicated onboarding & migration' under Firm; phone support is 'priority on Practice & Firm' while Solo lists email only. DECISION: what the real offer is (trial terms, what each plan includes, whether tier buttons become Book a demo, which became the primary CTA on 16-09). Recommendation: replace the Start-free-trial buttons with Book a demo and drop 'no credit card' until a trial mechanism exists, and reword audit logs and onboarding to match what each plan gets; hours of work. If the owner wants the site clean before a CA reads it, this is a candidate to pull forward. — _Sources:_ docs/marketing/2026-09-16-website-redesign-plan.md (section 5.1 CTA: 'Start free trial' demoted but kept); apps/marketing/app/(site)/pricing/page.tsx; CLAUDE.md ACC-19 bullet (no billing machinery); roadmap market_and_trust-05: Remove or label the pricing promises the product cannot keep (SSO, SLA, account manager, specialist, trial) — _Refs:_ rm/market_and_trust-09, rm/market_and_trust-05
- **POST-B-265** · `marketing-commercial-legal` · normal · weeks — **Decide: build plan, trial and billing machinery, or invoice by hand.** The product has no subscription, trial-expiry, plan-limit or billing-the-firm machinery (grep for plan, seat and trial in routers and migrations finds none; multi-currency entitlement was made self-serve for that reason): no trial_ends_at, seat or client cap exists, the pricing page's 'up to 25 clients' and 'up to 5 users' are not enforced and the free trial never ends; firms.plan (migration 003: starter/professional/enterprise) is written only by seed data and read by nothing. The September audit sizes the market at about 100,000 CA firms with a whole stack costing Rs 40,000-80,000 a year and a realistic ceiling of about 200 firms at Rs 25,000 (Rs 50 lakh ARR). DECISION: build the minimum now (plan, trial_ends_at, seat and client caps on firms, a Partner-visible Plan page, checks where clients are created and staff invited) or first raise invoices to paying firms from a platform-owner firm using the existing billing_schedules and sales invoicing, with a Razorpay adapter for collection later; the owner also decides who collects payment. Recommendation: invoice by hand first (founding-price pilots before subscription machinery) and build caps only after the pricing model and the first pilots settle. Verify when built: a trial firm is prompted on day N+1, a Solo firm cannot create a 26th client, a paid firm's invoice shows in the platform-owner firm's receivables. — _Sources:_ docs/audits/2026-09-07-where-we-are-against-the-one-platform-goal.md section 7.2 (lines 627-644) and 10 (lines 790-794); apps/marketing/app/(site)/pricing/page.tsx; market_and_trust-13 Build the minimum plan, trial and billing machinery, and bill the first firms with your own invoicing — _Refs:_ rm/market_and_trust-13
- **POST-B-266** · `marketing-commercial-legal` · normal · weeks — **Draft a DPA and publish a sub-processor list and data-flow statement.** The CA firm is the Data Fiduciary and PracticeSync its Data Processor (DPDP Rule 6 requires equivalent safeguards flowed down by contract), so firms will ask for a DPA, which 06 expects to become a sales gate well before 13-05-2027; no DPA text, sub-processor file or public data-flow statement exists. Internal inventory (docs/compliance/01-what-exists-today.md s.2): Supabase (Mumbai), Render (API, Singapore), Cloudflare Pages (both sites), Groq and Google Gemini (AI, outside India), Resend, Sentry, Razorpay. DECISION: approve counsel to draft the DPA (security flow-down, breach notice, deletion on exit, sub-processor change notice, ICAI professional-confidentiality point, retention annexe from domain/dpdp/retention.position(), security measures: MFA, RLS and assignment scoping, audit log, incident procedure). Claude then publishes a versioned page at /legal/dpa and /legal/subprocessors (purpose, region, data class per processor) linked from the privacy notice, with a test that every external host or provider env key the backend uses is listed; ties to the AI sub-processor terms work. — _Sources:_ docs/compliance/06-data-protection-dpdp.md §3, §5 (Rule 6 flow-down), §7 (point 4); roadmap market_and_trust-03: Publish a Data Processing Agreement template and a subprocessor list with data locations; security_privacy-08 Provide a Data Processing Agreement, a sub-processor list and a data-flow statement — _Refs:_ DPDP Rule 6, rm/market_and_trust-03, rm/security_privacy-08
- **POST-B-267** · `marketing-commercial-legal` · normal · days — **Name the legal entity, address and accountable people on the site.** The site names no founder, CA adviser or company: the footer reads '2026 PracticeSync' with no legal entity, registered office or registration number, contact details are generic, there is no About or team route under apps/marketing/app, and the footer's Privacy and Terms links both open /support. Whether a company or LLP exists yet cannot be seen from the repo. The owner supplies (roadmap decision 3): legal entity name, registration identifier, registered address, at least one named accountable person and, with consent, named CA advisers (check ICAI rules on members endorsing vendors before naming anyone). Claude then builds the About page and footer and aligns the Terms to the same entity, in about a day. The entity also decides GST invoicing, the software-developer registration (market_and_trust-23) and any later ERI choice, and the 'a real person will help you' promise needs a named person to be believable. Git history shows 2,321 commits under the AI assistant and three human identities, which an investor may read as key-person risk (inference only). — _Sources:_ market_and_trust-22 Name the people and the legal entity behind the product — _Refs:_ rm/market_and_trust-22
- **POST-B-268** · `marketing-commercial-legal` · normal · days — **Narrow positioning; publish a 'what PracticeSync does not do yet' page.** The site metadata still says 'Replace Tally, ClearTax, Winman and WhatsApp' (apps/marketing/app/layout.tsx) and the hero shows '4 Separate tools replaced by one login' (components/home/Hero.tsx), while the product files nothing, has no IMS, no FVU or ITR JSON file, WhatsApp is deep links and the Tally import writes masters only; the products page and pricing FAQ already answer parts plainly. The team's audit says narrow (three modules at grade A beat fourteen at C) and name the wedge: one ledger behind every statutory output and a product that names what it does not know; 'runs alongside Tally' is safer than 'replaces Tally' (Tally's installed base reported at 2-3 million, low confidence; ICAI reports 98,967 registered firms, 72,696 of them sole proprietorships, medium). DECISION (B2): choose the positioning and approve a public capability-and-limits page (filing, IMS, bank feeds, Tally import depth, AI scope) with every row backed by the claims ledger. Recommendation: yes; lead with the wedge for the small firm running 15 to 60 SME clients. — _Sources:_ roadmap market_and_trust-10: Narrow the positioning and publish a 'what PracticeSync does not do yet' page — _Refs:_ rm/market_and_trust-10
- **POST-B-269** · `marketing-commercial-legal` · normal · weeks — **Provide a whole-firm data export (the 'what if you shut down' answer).** The July/September audit lists 'what happens if you shut down? (have an export answer)' among the four objections to expect. There is no firm-level export: only per-report PDF and Excel (report-exports), the year-end pack and the Tally-related routes; settings has no export page and domain/dpdp holds retention rules only. DECISION: the shape, ideally a firm-wide archive of ledgers, documents and the audit log in open formats that can also serve DPDP data-principal access requests (see the rights-process item); then build. Recommendation: scope it as the data-return-on-exit commitment the Terms will need. — _Sources:_ docs/audits/2026-09-07-where-we-are-against-the-one-platform-goal.md section 10 (lines 784-788)
- **POST-B-270** · `marketing-commercial-legal` · normal · days — **Record acceptance of the Terms at sign-up and staff-invite acceptance.** Sign-up collects firm name, full name and email only: there is no terms, consent or agree step in app/signup, app/join, app/onboarding or app/login, and no terms_version or accepted_at field exists anywhere in api or web (checked by the roadmap). Depends on the Terms text existing. DECISION (once terms exist): make acceptance mandatory at firm creation (POST /api/onboarding/firm) and at invite acceptance, stored server-side as terms_version, accepted_at and user id with a 422 refusal otherwise; needs a migration (new table or columns) that auto-applies to production on merge. Enforceability of click-through is a question for counsel. Recommendation: build immediately after the terms text is approved. — _Sources:_ roadmap market_and_trust-02: Record acceptance of the Terms at sign-up and at staff-invite acceptance — _Refs:_ rm/market_and_trust-02
- **POST-B-271** · `marketing-commercial-legal` · normal · days — **Serve and publish the retention position under Rule 14.** domain/dpdp/retention.position() emits the ten-category structure (books_of_account, gst_returns, payroll, tds_records, income_tax_records, bank_data, client_onboarding, access_logs, support_correspondence, product_telemetry) with statute, anchor, duty-holder and confidence, but nothing serves it: no endpoint, no page (grep finds position() used only in tests). Rule 14 obliges publication and a DPA annexe needs the table; 06 calls this 'the next piece of DPDP work with a real deadline behind it'. Small build: an endpoint plus a page or annexe. DECISION: where it appears, public marketing page vs a Settings screen; recommendation: Settings first, plus the DPA annexe. Also keep the TODO(compliance) marker in retention.py honest when EPF/ESI periods land. — _Sources:_ docs/compliance/06-data-protection-dpdp.md §5b ('What is wired, and what is not'), §5e ('What is still not wired') — _Refs:_ #126, #105
- **POST-B-272** · `marketing-commercial-legal` · low — **Decide Firm plan terms: SSO, SLA, account manager; cite encryption.** SSO, 'SLA & account manager' and 'and encrypted' were TAKEN OFF the pricing page on 02-10-2026 (commit 5d471555) because nothing backs them; UNPROVEN_CLAIMS in tests/_marketing_claims.py (asserted in test_every_capability_sentence_on_the_marketing_site_is_in_the_claims_ledger.py) is now empty and a promise returns only by being written there with the decision it waits on. The Firm plan keeps 'Unlimited users & clients', 'Dedicated onboarding & migration' and 'Audit logs'; the hosting FAQ no longer says the database is encrypted (Supabase documents encryption at rest; nothing in the repo cites or checks it, so say it again only with a citation). The public SLA claim is unproven (no availability instrument, ops-12). DECISION: will any of SSO, an SLA or a dedicated account manager be offered (each is a build or a commitment); recommendation: offer none until a pilot firm asks, and add the encryption claim only with the citation from the encryption-at-rest item. — _Sources:_ /home/user/caflow-ai/CLAUDE.md line 5445 (market_and_trust-09/-16); docs/marketing/2026-09-16-website-redesign-plan.md section 2 (accuracy problems) and brief section 16 'do not invent ... certifications'; CLAUDE.md market_and_trust-09 bullet; apps/api/tests/test_every_capability_sentence_on_the_marketing_site_is_in_the_claims_ledger.py line 50 — _Refs:_ rm/market_and_trust-09, rm/market_and_trust-16, rm/ops-12
- **POST-B-273** · `marketing-commercial-legal` · low · hours — **Decide the pricing model and how prices are shown (annual, GST, bands).** The pricing page labels prices 'indicative' and never says whether GST is extra: Solo Rs 1,499/month (about Rs 17,988 a year, 25 clients, 1 user), Practice Rs 3,999/month (about Rs 47,988 a year, 150 clients, 5 users), Firm custom. Comparators (searched, medium confidence): Vyapar TaxOne 'CA Unlimited' from about Rs 10,000 a year plus GST; Zoho Books Rs 899 to Rs 9,999 a month excluding GST per organisation; the audit assumed Rs 15,000 to 40,000 per firm per year. Owner choices: annual price, a GST-exclusive label, per-client bands versus flat, and whether to show a list price before a pilot proves value. Recommendation: state GST treatment and annual amounts now (hours of copy) and set the founding price with the pilots rather than publishing a list price until the Tally import and filing gaps close; record the rationale in docs/plan. — _Sources:_ market_and_trust-14 Decide the pricing model against what incumbents charge — _Refs:_ rm/market_and_trust-14
- **POST-B-274** · `marketing-commercial-legal` · low · days — **Decide the rupee-glyph font for PDFs (Rs. vs FreeSans vs DejaVu).** Every PDF prints 'Rs.' because ReportLab's Helvetica has no U+20B9. Embedding the glyph needs a font file in the repo: GNU FreeSans is metric-compatible (0.977-1.013x of Helvetica, no column moves) but GPLv3 with the font exception, which makes committing it GPLv3 redistribution; DejaVu Sans is permissive (Bitstream Vera) but 1.13x/1.26x wider and would re-break the columns T5a-4b fixed; Liberation Sans lacks the glyph on this build. Decision D13 keeps 'Rs.' and nothing is blocked. DECISION: carry a GPLv3 font, move to DejaVu with a full re-measure, or stay on 'Rs.'; recommendation: stay on 'Rs.' until told otherwise. — _Sources:_ docs/audits/2026-09-19-checkpoint.md 'What needs the owner' row M; docs/audits/questions-for-the-owner.md section M — _Refs:_ M, T5a-5, D13
- **POST-B-275** · `marketing-commercial-legal` · low — **Decide whether the demo form gets a bot challenge.** No Turnstile or other CAPTCHA is on the demo form: it would be a third-party script on the marketing site, against the owner's decision that the sites load nothing from another host, and would widen the CSP. The honeypot, the per-address caps and the (unapplied) Cloudflare edge rule stand in for it (docs/operations/edge-protection.md 3.4). Revisit only if the form is abused. — _Sources:_ docs/operations/edge-protection.md §3.4 — _Refs:_ rm/ops-30
- **POST-B-276** · `marketing-commercial-legal` · low · days — **Decide whether to redesign the logo mark (tick inside a continuous ring).** The website brief asked to 'explore' a proprietary tick-in-ring mark. Stage 5 deliberately did NOT redesign it: apps/marketing/components/Logo.tsx still draws the existing ring plus accent arc plus checkmark in currentColor (so it cross-fades with the header). A new mark is a brand decision, 'the one item of the brief deliberately left open'; apps/web's LogoIcon already spins the ring for loading states, so the concept is half-built. DECISION: commission or approve a new mark, or keep the current one; recommendation: keep it until the domain and brand are settled. _When: Brand decision; no demo impact._ — _Sources:_ docs/marketing/2026-09-16-website-redesign-plan.md STATUS table row 'Stage 5' (line 28) and Stage 5 plan (lines 306-309)
- **POST-B-277** · `marketing-commercial-legal` · low · days — **Publish a monitor-fed status page; promise no SLA before 90 days.** No status page exists and no uptime monitor is attached (docs/operations/service-levels.md: availability has no target; the smoke run is four samples a day); a marketing guard (tests/test_the_marketing_site_says_what_the_product_does.py) forbids '% uptime' claims because nothing measures it. Once the external monitor (see the uptime-monitor item; ops-07) has run 90 days, publish the monitor's own page with real incident history, probing GET /ready (a bounded database request, unlike /health), link it from the footer (apps/marketing/components/SiteFooter.tsx has no status link) and the trust page, and only then consider a written target in the Terms; relax the guard only to allow the measured figure, not a round number. Claude recommends a hosted free status page tied to the same monitor; Zoho publishes regional status pages and a 99.9% monthly SLA (searched, medium-high), so a CA comparing will look for it. The SLA half is done: the unbacked 'SLA & account manager' bullet was taken off the pricing page on 02-10-2026 (#677). — _Sources:_ ops-08 Publish a status page and a measured uptime figure, and promise no SLA until 90 days exist; market_and_trust-20 Publish a status page, and keep 'SLA' out of the copy until uptime is measured (duplicate of ops-08) — _Refs:_ rm/ops-08, rm/market_and_trust-20, rm/ops-05
- **POST-B-278** · `marketing-commercial-legal` · low — **Watch for a DPDP Rule 15 negative list or SDF designation.** Rule 15 is permissive by default with a negative list, so Render (Singapore), Groq and Gemini processing is allowed today, but the Government can restrict by order at any time. A Significant Data Fiduciary designation (by Central Government notification, on volume and sensitivity; a platform holding payroll for a large share of India's SMEs is a 'less unlikely' candidate over time) could bring specified localisation and, under Rule 13, an annual DPIA and independent audit, algorithmic due diligence (the AI copilot is in scope of the concept) and an India-based DPO answerable to the Board of Directors; none is built or planned. Standing watch item; reopen trigger = such an order or designation. Sentry's region should be read off its DSN and added to the same list. — _Sources:_ docs/compliance/06-data-protection-dpdp.md §5 (Rules 13, 15) — _Refs:_ DPDP Rules 13, 15

## Screens and ease of use

- **POST-B-279** · `frontend-ux` · normal · weeks — **Decide a server idempotency key for ambiguous write retries.** DECISION: should the ledger-posting creates accept a client-generated idempotency key, so a retry after an ambiguous failure cannot create a second document? Today a request that succeeded server-side while the screen showed an error becomes a second voucher on retry whatever the button does (singleFlight only blocks repeat clicks while the first request is pending). Facts: no general Idempotency-Key header exists in routers, middleware or core; the posting kernel dedupes only on (client_id, reference_no, entry_date) and receipt_service has a narrow _find_existing fast path; lib/api never retries. Options: (a) mint a key when the editor opens, send it as a header and store it with the document on journal, invoice issue, receipt and payment creates; (b) rely on the existing dedupe keys. Recommendation: (a), starting with journal posting and receipts, after the demo. — _Sources:_ /home/user/caflow-ai/CLAUDE.md line 5449 (frontend_ux-09) — _Refs:_ rm/frontend_ux-09
- **POST-B-280** · `frontend-ux` · normal · weeks — **Decide whether phone and tablet use is supported at all.** DECISION: layout.tsx declares a PWA manifest (display standalone) and registers /sw.js, but 92 of the 170 page.tsx files contain no sm:/md:/lg:/xl: prefix (86 of 160 on 16-09-2026), including the biggest screens (client sales, purchases, payroll attendance's 11-column leave table). D11 settled the upper bound of width, never the lower. Options: (a) declare desktop and tablet only and drop the manifest and service worker; (b) support phone for a few flows (see the phone-first flows item) with a narrow-width pass on the largest screens. Recommendation: (b), limited to those flows, decided together with the phone-first item. — _Sources:_ docs/plan/2026-09-16-the-redesign-plan-revised.md - section 4 'installable PWA with no responsive design'; counted today — _Refs:_ D11
- **POST-B-281** · `frontend-ux` · low · weeks — **Decide a shared last-selected client for firm-level tool pages.** ClientNavContext covers only /clients/[id]/*. R3.6 added lib/workspace/useClientPicker.ts and migrated only pages with 'explicit pick before acting' semantics (trial-balance-import, reports/cash-flow, tds, tds/returns, income-tax/ais); roughly 25 other pages (create-record modals that default to the first client, filters that default to all) each keep their own useState and clients query. Forcing one hook shape risked changing intended UX, so it is a per-page product call; a shared 'last selected client' would remove the constant re-selection the July audit lists as 'should fix'. DECISION: remember the last client across firm-level modules, and where? Recommendation: remember it only for read-only filters and report screens, and keep the explicit pick on write modals. — _Sources:_ docs/audits/2026-07-02-executive-product-audit.md R3.6 'Deliberately not done' (lines 4437-4447), UX should-fix (line 148) — _Refs:_ R3.6
- **POST-B-282** · `frontend-ux` · low · months — **Decide the interface-language posture: D8 extraction or regional languages.** DECISION: what to do about i18n. D8 is locked as 'extract strings, translate nothing' and lists translating anything as out of scope, yet apps/web has no i18n library, catalogue or extraction convention, layout.tsx is lang="en", and screens rebuilt in Tracks 1-3 were not extracted (the 16-09-2026 plan argued extraction from a screen being rewritten is nearly free, and from a settled one costs a second full pass). The roadmap item (frontend_ux-30) asks for a Hindi, Gujarati and Marathi interface for clerks and small-town practices, sized at months: string extraction across about 169 routes, a translation budget and a CA-reviewed statutory-terms glossary. Options: (a) extract only when a screen is next touched, with a string module location chosen; (b) one-off extraction sweep; (c) drop D8 as superseded; (d) reverse D8 and translate, which needs budget. Recommendation: keep D8, adopt (a), and start translation only when pilot firms ask, beginning with the ten most-used screens and a practising-CA-reviewed glossary. — _Sources:_ docs/plan/THE-PLAN.md - decision D8; docs/plan/2026-09-16-the-redesign-plan-revised.md - section 7 #8; roadmap frontend_ux-30: Regional-language interface for clerks and small-town practices — _Refs:_ D8, rm/frontend_ux-30
- **POST-B-283** · `frontend-ux` · low · days — **Decide the leftover indigo: fold into navy or keep as second colour.** DECISION: is the leftover indigo a second brand colour to keep, or drift to fold? G0 answered NAVY and converted the indigo-600 fills (1,193 utilities in 162 files), but tints, badges and text were left as a different role and the question was never answered for them.
