Still present on 02-10-2026: three hex sites (app/settings/firm-hsn-library/page.tsx:342 #6366F1 and #4338CA, app/clients/[id]/accounting/page.tsx:1900 #EEF2FF and #4338CA, components/invoices/InvoiceEditor.tsx:1271 #EEF2FF, #C7D2FE and #4338CA) plus roughly 35 to 52 Tailwind indigo-* utilities (engagements, pipeline, workflows, payroll statutory and reports, ownership-map, sign page, statutory-values icon). Recommendation: fold into the navy and ps.* tokens by role; the three hex sites also count toward the 44-class hex budget. — _Sources:_ docs/audits/questions-for-the-owner.md section G and G0 answer block — _Refs:_ G, G0
- **POST-B-284** · `frontend-ux` · low · days — **Decide the permission model for loans and scheduled-reports empty states.** DECISION: how should the Loans and Scheduled-reports screens be gated for an EmptyStateAction? They write straight over PostgREST under a role rule, or none, that no backend permission pair in core/permissions expresses, so a gate would be invented. Options: (a) add backend permission pairs (and move or role-guard the writes) first, then add the gated action; (b) leave the empty state without a create action. Recommendation: (a), when those screens next move onto the API. — _Sources:_ /home/user/caflow-ai/CLAUDE.md line 5457 (frontend_ux-24) — _Refs:_ rm/frontend_ux-24
- **POST-B-285** · `frontend-ux` · low · days — **Decide the text-link colour now that the primary is navy.** DECISION: G0 deliberately did NOT convert text-blue-* (about 462 utilities in app/ and components/, re-counted 02-10-2026) because #182350 on white reads as body text, not as a link, so what a link looks like is its own question. Options: keep blue for links by naming a link token and mapping the utilities by role, or move links to navy with an underline. Recommendation: a named link token in the blue family, so the palette pass can map the 462 sites by role. — _Sources:_ docs/audits/questions-for-the-owner.md section G0 'What is deliberately NOT navy' — _Refs:_ G0
- **POST-B-286** · `frontend-ux` · low · days — **Decide whether DateInput gets a calendar popup.** DECISION: should the typed date field offer a calendar popup button? None was built: a popup that opens the browser's picker needs a hidden native input, the very thing the field replaces, and would be a permanent exception in the ratchet, so this is a decision rather than an accident. Options: (a) no popup, typed entry only; (b) a button over a hidden native input, accepted as a ratchet exception; (c) a custom popover component. Recommendation: (a) for now, since typed entry matches a CA's keyboard-first workflow, and revisit if pilot firms ask. — _Sources:_ /home/user/caflow-ai/CLAUDE.md line 5465 (frontend_ux-19) — _Refs:_ rm/frontend_ux-19
- **POST-B-287** · `frontend-ux` · low · days — **Decide whether global search includes Knowledge Base articles.** DECISION: should global search include KB articles? The Batch 7 design listed it as optional ('scoped to the caller's visible set'). routers/search.py searches clients, accounts, journals, leads, engagements, tasks, documents and DSC records only; the KB is reachable via /knowledge, the client Knowledge tab and a nav entry. Any addition must reuse knowledge_service visibility (assignment scope, internal Partner-only articles) and the FTS indexes from migration 073. Recommendation: add it once the KB holds enough articles to be worth searching. — _Sources:_ docs/BATCH_7_DESIGN_REVIEW.md section 9 table row 'Global search'; docs/BATCH_6_DESIGN_REVIEW.md section 9; verified apps/api/routers/search.py — _Refs:_ Batch6-7-global-search
- **POST-B-288** · `frontend-ux` · low — **Decide whether live figures may return to the client module grid.** The client module grid carries plain names and no figures (removed the same day it shipped, D25, because figures loaded after about 10 s and re-sorted, re-banded and re-flowed every card). The decision leaves one door open: figures may return in a fixed-height slot inside a card that never re-orders; the same figures are on Overview. Reopen only on the owner's say; the rule to keep is that progressive loading must not move what is already on screen. — _Sources:_ docs/plan/THE-PLAN.md - decision D25 — _Refs:_ D25
- **POST-B-289** · `frontend-ux` · low · days — **Decide whether module landing pages should double as an index.** DECISION: should /practice, /team, /health and /relationships (259 to 1,021-line working dashboards) link their own sub-screens? After 2.7a/2.7b every guard that can say 'a screen is in the wrong place' passes and pageOnly is 0 for every module, but these four link none (practice links one) of their sub-screens; the complete module panel sits beside them. Index cards were deliberately not added ('work with no reader'). Recommendation: leave as is unless demo audiences get lost. — _Sources:_ docs/plan/2026-09-24-overnight-run.md '2.7c' (lines 602-605) — _Refs:_ 2.7c
- **POST-B-290** · `frontend-ux` · low · days — **Decide whether the sales invoice editor keeps an unsent draft.** DECISION: extend lib/drafts/unsentDraft and useUnsentDraft (today a frozen list of two: the journal and the purchase bill) to the sales invoice editor? The editor keeps no draft or autosave; the discard guard (useUnsavedChanges) warns, but a reload or crash loses typed GST lines, and drafts are per tab (sessionStorage) and never shared. The sales-invoice blueprint (SALES_INVOICE_UX_BLUEPRINT 1.3 and Part 6) asked for an autosave-draft on blur of the whole editor. Extending needs the same field-by-field validation as the bill draft: keep raw typed fields only, never totals or server-handed ids, offer the restore on a click and clear it after the server's success check. Recommendation: yes, after the demo, following the bill-draft pattern. — _Sources:_ /home/user/caflow-ai/CLAUDE.md line 5449 (frontend_ux-23); docs/SALES_INVOICE_UX_BLUEPRINT.md 1.3 header hint and Part 6; CLAUDE.md frontend_ux-23 — _Refs:_ rm/frontend_ux-23
- **POST-B-291** · `frontend-ux` · low · days — **Decide whether to build customer and vendor server-side search.** DECISION: build debounced /api/customers/search and /api/vendors/search now? The combobox audit's Phase 1 proposed them for books with thousands of parties, but the migration shipped client-side lookups over the already-loaded list and left only the fetchOptions seam (on CustomerLookup, AccountLookup, ClientLookup and EntityLookup) with no endpoint behind it. That is fine at a CA client's realistic size. Recommendation: do not build until a client with thousands of customers or vendors appears. — _Sources:_ docs/audits/2026-07-01-combobox-smart-lookup-audit.md sections 5 and 8; docs/audits/2026-07-01-combobox-smart-lookup-migration-summary.md section 2
- **POST-B-292** · `frontend-ux` · low · days — **Decide whether to build the per-device density toggle (D4).** DECISION: build the comfortable/compact toggle D4 called for, or record that the fluid layout alone answers D4? D4 decided 'density persistence: per device' (localStorage, no migration) and the 12-09-2026 plan said the layout should offer the toggle, but no density provider, toggle or storage key exists in apps/web (only a combobox panelDensity prop). If built it is a per-device preference to be allowlisted in the browser-storage guard as a per-viewer convenience. Recommendation: record that the fluid layout answers D4 and drop the toggle unless a pilot firm asks. _When: Unsure whether the owner still wants the toggle now that the layout is fluid._ — _Sources:_ docs/plan/THE-PLAN.md - decision D4; docs/plan/2026-09-12-compliance-and-craft.md - 'Density' section — _Refs:_ D4
- **POST-B-293** · `frontend-ux` · low · hours — **Decide: add a ps.surface-alt token for the band lighter than ps.bg?.** DECISION (question I, never answered): #FCFDFE and #FCFCFD were left alone in the colour sweep; they are a zebra band LIGHTER than ps.bg, and on GSTR-3B one sits on a row whose own hover is bg-ps-bg, so collapsing it would make the row identical to its own hover. Still present at app/gst/gstr3b/page.tsx lines 617, 734 and 890, and tailwind.config.ts has no surface-alt token (re-checked 02-10-2026). Options: add ps.surface-alt between white and ps.bg, or let those rows take ps.bg and lose the band. Recommendation: add the token, since collapsing breaks the GSTR-3B row's hover cue. — _Sources:_ docs/audits/questions-for-the-owner.md section I — _Refs:_ I
- **POST-B-294** · `frontend-ux` · low · hours — **Decide: stronger hover step for navy primary buttons?.** DECISION: G0 recorded a regression the owner may want fixed. The navy primary's brand to brand-dark hover step is 1.18:1 (about 6.7 L* units: above just-noticeable for a button-sized area, but subtler than blue-600 to blue-700's 1.30:1); the existing pair was reused rather than inventing a third navy. Options: keep it, or add one stronger hover token ('if you want a stronger step, say so and it is one token'). Recommendation: keep unless the hover reads weak when the owner uses the screens. — _Sources:_ docs/audits/questions-for-the-owner.md section G0 (answer block) — _Refs:_ G0

## Schema, data and migrations

- **POST-B-295** · `schema-data-migrations` · high · hours — **Decide the Tally import rules (decision 8) before building ledger import.** The Tally importer still writes customers and vendors only (domain/tally/migration_service.py WRITTEN_ITEM_TYPES = {customer, vendor}); spreadsheet bulk imports now exist for opening documents, vouchers, trial balance and the fixed-asset register (accounting-05/-08/-17/-18) but no Tally XML/Excel path carries a practice's chart of accounts, opening balances or bill-wise dues across. Owner decisions needed before engineering starts (roadmap decision 8): (a) make account-name uniqueness per client, because migration 015's UNIQUE (firm_id, account_name) and (firm_id, account_code) collide when each client's Tally ledgers are imported (a constraint-change migration with a real-Postgres test, applied to production on merge); (b) may imported invoices exist without a ledger posting; (c) how prior-year comparatives are held; (d) whether mid-year cutover is supported or only 1 April and quarter starts. Claude recommends deciding these now so a 1 April 2027 cutover kit is possible. The builds accounting-01, -02, -03, -04 and gst-26 (separate items) all wait on this and on a real Tally export from a pilot CA. Verify when built: a real 12-month client export imports in one session and trial balance and open bills tie to Tally to the paisa. — _Sources:_ market_and_trust-24 Import a Tally practice's ledgers, opening balances and bill-wise dues (duplicate of accounting-01) — _Refs:_ rm/market_and_trust-24, rm/accounting-01, rm/accounting-02, rm/accounting-03, rm/gst-26, decision-8
- **POST-B-296** · `schema-data-migrations` · normal · weeks — **Decide what erasure means once statutory retention has lapsed.** A lapsed statutory duty does NOT permit the delete (06 5d, 5b): posted journals, filed GST returns and ageing schedules still reference the row (customer FKs cascade; two vendor tables carry vendor_id with no FK), so the refusal stands either way and only its wording changes; DELETE /clients/{id} is a soft delete, not an erasure path. Net effect: no path in the product ever removes a data principal's personal data even after every duty lapses, while DPDP s.8(7) obliges erasure when the purpose is served unless another law requires retention. DECISION, ideally with counsel: anonymise in place, scrub identifiers while keeping the ledger row, or accept refusal with a written position. Recommendation: the written position now, scrub-in-place considered only after counsel reads s.8(7). — _Sources:_ docs/compliance/06-data-protection-dpdp.md §5d ('A lapsed duty does NOT permit the delete'), §5b — _Refs:_ #126, #129, DPDP s.8(7)
- **POST-B-297** · `schema-data-migrations` · normal · hours — **Decide whether to drop the three migration-247 scratch tables in production.** public._backup_247_invoices, public._backup_247_journal_lines and public._mig247_targets were made by hand during migrations 247/248 and exist only in production (still in the 01-10-2026 snapshot; sizes about 736 kB, 4 MB and 904 kB, roughly 34,000 rows in all per the August audit). RLS is on with no policy, so nothing can read them: the cost is drift (every comparison must account for them), not exposure; xbrl_tag_mappings also exists in production with no migration and no code. Migration 337 section 5 holds the three DROP statements un-run because once a real client's books exist a pre-correction ledger backup is evidence under Companies Act s.128(5) with Rule 3(1), so the answer becomes 'no' the day a real client is onboarded; production holds dummy data today (seven fabricated clients). DECISION: drop now or keep for ever. Recommendation: drop while only dummy data exists, in the same change and fixture refresh as the retired-tables drop, after a quick look that the rows are only the old 247/248 snapshots; if any real client is already live, keep them. — _Sources:_ docs/audits/2026-09-03-guard-drift-first-run.md 'Tables in the live database that no migration declares'; D2 'What remains'; migration 337 section 5; docs/audits/2026-08-02-migration-ledger-drift-audit.md 'Secondary findings' (lines 129-144); apps/api/migrations/337 comment (lines 252-272); docs/audits/2026-09-07-where-we-are-against-the-one-platform-goal.md section 4.7 (lines 502-503) and 12.1 (lines 854-857); apps/api/migrations/337 section 5; docs/schema-drift.md (+1 more) — _Refs:_ scratch tables 247, ledger-drift-secondary
- **POST-B-298** · `schema-data-migrations` · normal · weeks — **Extend the Tally importer beyond customer and vendor masters.** domain/tally/migration_service.WRITTEN_ITEM_TYPES = {customer, vendor} (checked): ledgers, journals, opening balances and masters from a Tally export are validated and counted 'not written' and the screen says so (apps/web/lib/migration/writtenTypes.ts). It parses only LEDGER and VOUCHER elements: no BILLALLOCATIONS (bill-wise AR/AP outstanding), INVENTORYENTRIES/STOCKITEM, COSTCENTRE or GODOWN, and there is no Busy or Zoho import (July audit Tier 4 'reliable two-way Tally/Busy/Zoho import'). The spreadsheet bulk importers (opening documents, vouchers, fixed-asset register, trial balance) are the practical route today. DECISION: invest in an XML ledger, voucher, bill-allocation and stock import (and which other formats), or keep the spreadsheet route as the migration story. Recommendation: decide with the first pilot firms' real export in hand. — _Sources:_ docs/audits/2026-07-02-executive-product-audit.md Tier 4 (line 284), Innovation Must Have (line 162), demo-ware list (line 139); docs/audits/2026-09-07-where-we-are-against-the-one-platform-goal.md section 7.5 (lines 685-694) and 10 (lines 766-773) — _Refs:_ Tier4-import

The guards fixture (production_guards_2026-09-03.json, meta applied_through_migration 381) was captured 13-09-2026 21:05 IST and is about 97 migrations behind head 478, so policies and constraints from 382-478 are excused rather than compared and only the 8-table shield cap holds the line; the schema fixture was refreshed to 470 on 01-10-2026 23:33 IST (474 and 477 sit in ADDED_AFTER_THE_SNAPSHOT). The firm-scope guard also reads the snapshot's applied_through_migration, and the dead-table drops need a refresh. A person with read access to production must capture: either scripts/db/schema_snapshot.py and guard_snapshot.py against a production DSN, or the console route in tests/fixtures/README.md (Supabase execute_sql pages, reassembled and checked against guards_md5). Replace both files in a commit of their own and set applied_through_migration to production's max(filename). — _Sources:_ /home/user/caflow-ai/CLAUDE.md lines 3091-3097, 3117; docs/audits/2026-09-03-converging-the-last-guard-differences.md 'Refreshing that fixture is the natural next step'; tests/fixtures/README.md; docs/schema-drift.md 'The other half'; apps/api/tests/fixtures/README.md — _Refs:_ guards fixture refresh
- **POST-B-300** · `schema-data-migrations` · low · days — **Add a server-preview (dry_run) hook to the document and voucher import dialog.** POST /api/opening-documents/bulk and POST /api/accounting/vouchers/import accept dry_run, but the shared import dialog has no server-preview hook, so no dry-run SCREEN exists for those two imports and a CA cannot see per-row verdicts before committing. - **POST-B-301** · `schema-data-migrations` · low · hours · UNSURE · live session — **Check bank_transactions index coverage; migration 055's two indexes are dead.** migrations/055_v131_hardening.sql:78 (uq_bank_transactions_import) and :99 (idx_bank_txns_unreconciled) index bank_transactions on txn_date and bank_account_id, which the table does not have (it has transaction_date), so neither statement worked (probe pass confirmed against production on 12-09-2026: 15 live indexes, none matching). The harm is smaller than the probe pass said: migration 094's header documents that it superseded them with indexes on transaction_date, match_status, statement_id and (firm_id, client_id), migration 173's header concludes 'no action needed', and 224 added uq_bank_txns_client_import_hash. What is unmeasured is whether the reconciliation reads (_session_txns and the unreconciled queue) need a composite (client_id, transaction_date) or a partial unreconciled index. Read production pg_indexes and the Supabase performance advisors first and add a corrective migration only if a read is slow; do not quote index coverage from 055. — _Sources:_ docs/audits/2026-09-12-the-probe-pass/README.md section 7 (defect with no finding); banking.md BANK-22 — _Refs:_ probe-pass-migration-055, BANK-22
- **POST-B-302** · `schema-data-migrations` · low · days · live session — **Compare production table GRANTs with the migrations.** The 1 Sep walkthrough found service_role lacked INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE on five money tables in production (fixed by migration 306) and recommended adding GRANTs to the drift check. What shipped is a shape invariant over the migration-built database (test_r269_service_role_grants_pg.py: if authenticated can INSERT, service_role must), but neither production snapshot records table privileges, so a production-only missing grant would still first show as a runtime 42501. Recommended: capture table and function privileges from production into a snapshot and compare, narrowing to the directions that break writes (needs a production read, so best done with the fixture refresh). — _Sources:_ docs/audits/2026-09-01-can-a-ca-run-a-client-for-a-full-year.md blocker 1 'Systemic fix'

Migration 469's note confirms /api/documents is the 'documents' table, a different one, and that an API delete door for client_documents is still missing. DECISION: consolidate onto client_documents (needs an API door, a retention position and a Storage-delete rule) or keep both and fix the team_members FK. Recommendation: consolidate onto client_documents, since the browser already uses it and team_members has no rows for the other table's FK to point at. — _Sources:_ docs/audits/2026-09-03-three-writes-production-refuses.md 'What is still open' bullet 2 — _Refs:_ documents vs client_documents

The owner has already decided 'delete both dead tables' (docs/audits/questions-for-the-owner.md); what remains is one change: the DROP migration (merging applies it to production), the production snapshot refresh it forces because a DROP moves both sides of the fixture comparison (docs/schema-drift.md, tests/fixtures/README.md), and removal of the tables from three test allowlists (test_direct_write_tables_are_role_guarded, test_sql_role_tiers_match_python, test_role_write_policies_pg). — _Sources:_ CLAUDE.md lines 1307-1317; CLAUDE.md lines 1486-1506 (PUR-16) and 1554-1581 (PUR-15); docs/audits/findings-status.json PUR-16, TDS-27, PUR-15; docs/schema-drift.md (+2 more) — _Refs:_ migration-371, PUR-16, PUR-15, TDS-27, migration-378
- **POST-B-307** · `schema-data-migrations` · low · weeks — **Make migrations build from zero: fix ten failures or take a baseline.** Ten early migrations (008, 045, 053, 054, 055, 068, 070, 071, 095, 144) cannot apply to a fresh database and are frozen in tests/test_migrations_apply.py EXPECTED_MIGRATION_FAILURES (equal to BASELINE_FAILURES, may only shrink); duplicate numbers 45, 46 and 94-97 are grandfathered. Each runs without the single-transaction wrapper and commits what precedes its failure, and every later migration and *_pg.py test was written against that partial schema. Side effect: a new Supabase project cannot be built from migrations alone, which blocks a clean staging environment for the table-top exercises and the restore rehearsal. DECISION: take a schema baseline at the current production migration and archive old files (without editing any applied file, because the runner's checksum tracking would re-run an edited one in production), fix each file, or do nothing. Recommendation: defer; a staging copy restored from production needs no baseline, so revisit only if onboarding a second engineer hurts. Verify when built: a fresh build equals the production snapshot (md5) with an empty expected-failures set. — _Sources:_ docs/deploy-migrations.md 'A file is NOT wrapped' table; apps/api/tests/test_migrations_apply.py; roadmap engineering-13: Create a baseline schema so a new database builds cleanly from zero — _Refs:_ rm/ops-16, rm/engineering-13

Run schema_snapshot.py against a scratch build and production, then update the doc; best done in the same session as the fixture refresh. — _Sources:_ docs/schema-drift.md 'What is still drifted'

Dropping or reconciling it needs a migration and the production-fixture refresh in docs/schema-drift.md. - **POST-B-316** · `schema-data-migrations` · low · weeks — **Tag records with consent, purpose and retention expiry (DPDP Rules 8, 14).** 06 names this one of the two items worth starting now: if every record knows which consent and purpose it belongs to, expiry or erasure on withdrawal or retention lapse becomes a query, not an archaeology project. Not built: no migration adds consent, purpose or retention-expiry columns (grep of apps/api/migrations finds none); today erasure_decision() derives the lapse date from each record's own date at request time. DECISION: stored columns vs a derived view, and which tables first (payroll, customers, vendors, bank data). Recommendation: a derived view first, because no consent is captured today and the lapse date is already derivable, adding stored consent columns only once the consent path decision is taken. It is the same data an Account Aggregator DataLife would have demanded (moot under route 3). — _Sources:_ docs/compliance/06-data-protection-dpdp.md §5 ('The two items worth starting now', item 2) — _Refs:_ DPDP Rules 8, 14

No re-grade has been recorded since the 269 closures; findings-status.md counts findings, not grades. DECISION QUESTION: keep the A+ frame as the north star? ## Other

- **POST-B-321** · `other` · high · days — **Make practice profitability true: cost definition, status filter, unbilled clients.** Decision question: what is 'cost' on /practice/profitability (recommended: users.cost_rate_paise x hours, with a Partner screen to set the rates) and fix the fee_invoices half now (recommended, days, no migration) or wait for decision 11's surviving fee-billing path and re-point afterwards? Verified at HEAD: revenue sums every non-deleted fee invoice including Draft and Cancelled (invoice_repository.get_revenue_by_client, no status filter, unpaged); it lists only clients that HAVE revenue (routers/analytics.py ~380) so a heavy-effort client with no invoice never appears; cost is minutes x time_entries.hourly_rate_paise, which is NULL on every timer-started entry (counted as zero, so profit equals revenue), while users.cost_rate_paise (Partner-only GET/PUT /api/billing/staff-cost-rates, api.billing.listCostRates/setCostRate in apps/web/lib/api/index.ts) has no screen and nothing reads it (billing_service.py:413 says so), and the stored time_entries.billable_rate_paise is also unread; time_entries is read unpaged past the ~1,000-row cap. Done when an endpoint-level TestClient test shows Draft/Cancelled add 0, a client with 10 hours and no invoice shows a negative margin, 1,500 entries are all counted and cost uses users.cost_rate_paise when an entry has no rate. _When: Visible wrong numbers if the demo opens /practice/profitability on seeded data: confirm the seeded time entries carry a rate and no Draft/Cancelled fee bill inflates revenue. May slip to post-demo if the demo avoids that screen. | Demoted from pre-demo: A days-long build with an owner decision on what 'cost' means, and the demo can simply avoid /practice/profitability (revenue includes Draft and Cancelled fee invoices and cost is zero for timer-started entries). That is on the avoid-or-explain list in the 'known gaps' decision item; fix after the demo, or pull it back if the demo script includes the screen._ — _Sources:_ docs/BATCH_5_COMPLETION_REPORT.md sections 1, 3 and 6; docs/BATCH_1_COMPLETION_REPORT.md section 8 (billable_rate_paise vs hourly_rate_paise coexist); verified apps/api/repositories/time_tracking_analytics_repository.py:236-295, apps/web/app/practice/profitability/page.tsx:362-372; practice_management-14 Make the profitability figures true: status filter, effort-only clients, paging, real cost rate — _Refs:_ Batch5-cost-rate, rm/practice_management-14
- **POST-B-322** · `other` · normal · weeks — **Automated document collection, client deadline reminders and WhatsApp Business API.** R3.4 (large): the July audit calls manual document chasing the biggest daily admin sink. Today /notifications/whatsapp is a click-to-chat composer (templates, wa.me, last 20 messages kept only in localStorage, allow-listed as a convenience), services/reminder_service.py says 'future integrations will call send_whatsapp()', no scheduler job reads document_requests, and email_service.send_compliance_due_soon now feeds the staff digest, not clients. Not built: an automatic follow-up cadence on open document requests, client-facing deadline reminders, and WhatsApp Business API delivery (needs a Meta BSP account, an owner step). Any mail goes through practice_mail_service.deliver and the PRACTICE_MAIL_ENABLED switch. DECISION: whether and when to build; the mail-provider choice is owner-parked until after the demo. — _Sources:_ docs/audits/2026-07-02-executive-product-audit.md R3.4 (line 263), CA productivity #4 (line 155), Innovation Should Have (line 163) — _Refs:_ R3.4
- **POST-B-323** · `other` · normal · days — **Decide and enforce maker-checker on compliance obligations.** Decision question (roadmap decision 11, maker-checker policy): should reviewer and approver be enforced on obligations? The 8-state status ladder is real but nothing compares the caller with the stored reviewer_id/approver_id (they appear only in the assign endpoint, routers/compliance_ops.py:48-50,143-144, and as notification recipients), and mark_filed (domain/compliance_record_service.py ~264-284) fast-forwards In Progress -> Ready For Review -> Ready To File -> Filed in one call, so a preparer can review and file their own work in one click. Recommendation: an optional FIRM SETTING, default OFF because a solo CA is preparer, reviewer and approver; when ON, Ready For Review -> Ready To File only by the assigned reviewer or a Partner, Filed only by the approver or a Partner, preparer and reviewer must differ where the firm has two or more people, and mark_filed stops at review unless the caller may approve. Done when a preparer's transition to Ready To File returns 403 naming the reviewer, the reviewer's returns 200, and with the setting off behaviour is unchanged. Days. The payroll two-person release item is the same policy for payroll. — _Sources:_ practice_management-18 Enforce reviewer and approver on obligations (segregation of duties) — _Refs:_ rm/practice_management-18, decision 11
- **POST-B-324** · `other` · normal · weeks — **Decide how a due-date extension is represented in the compliance engine.** CBDT/CBIC extend due dates by notification several times a year (GSTR-1/3B for named states, TDS, ITR). services/compliance_engine.py derives every date by rule and CLAUDE.md says it 'needs touching only when a date is changed', i.e. a code edit and deploy; grep finds no extension/override concept in the engine, migrations or routers, and obligations already stored in compliance_calendar / compliance_records keep their original date. The 2026-06 audit called this the missing versioned, dated rule model (compliance_rulesets / compliance_rules / client_obligations, Doc 5 section 26). Decision question: how is an extension represented (per notification, per period, per state or class of taxpayer), how are stored obligations re-dated, and who records it? Claude implements after the decision. — _Sources:_ docs/PRACTICESYNC_AUDIT_v1.md sections 1-2 ('Compliance Rule Engine (versioned)' Partial; FR-F-09 / TRD 5.6)
- **POST-B-325** · `other` · normal · weeks — **Fee and hour budgets per engagement, timesheet approval and write-offs.** routers/analytics.py ~557 /revenue-vs-effort gives realisation only; nothing holds budget_hours or fee budget, burn alerts, a timesheet submit-and-approve step or a write-down at invoicing. Add budget hours and fee to the engagement, an in-app alert at a configurable burn (e.g. 80%), weekly timesheet approval that locks approved entries, and a write-down line on the invoice. Depends on practice_management-12 (bill time) and on the owner decision in practice_management-13 (single fee-billing engine, decision 11); practice_management-11 (price time at entry) is done. Verify: 10 budget hours with 9 logged raises the alert; an approved week is locked from edit. _When: Blocked behind practice_management-12 and the fee-billing engine decision (decision 11)._ — _Sources:_ practice_management-25 Fee and hour budgets per engagement, timesheet approval, and realisation with write-offs — _Refs:_ rm/practice_management-25
- **POST-B-326** · `other` · normal · weeks — **Retire legacy compliance_calendar so all screens show the same deadlines.** Decision question (part of roadmap decision 11): which deadline table survives, and is retiring compliance_calendar still wanted or is the two-tracker design accepted? Recommendation: compliance_records. R3.13b-e retired compliance_tasks (migration 170) and moved /deadlines, /practice/compliance and the client Compliance tab onto compliance_records, but apps/web/app/gst/page.tsx (lines ~209, 602) and app/income-tax/page.tsx (~238, 469, 622, 666) still read and INSERT compliance_calendar straight over PostgREST (rbac never runs), capacity_risk_service.py (~123), risk_register_service.py (~88), routers/compliance.py (legacy mark-filed and /seed) still use it, so a partner can be told different things by different screens; CLAUDE.md records that both trackers lock the period (gst-27). Order: re-point the two backend readers, make the two tab screens call the API, backfill unfinished and filed calendar rows into records (filed date and ARN included), freeze writes to the legacy table, then RLS-harden or drop it (a production migration needing the schema-drift fixture refresh). Done when no compliance_calendar read/write remains outside a frozen legacy module and a parity test shows /deadlines, the GST tab, the capacity forecast and the risk register give the same open/overdue counts. _When: Cheap pre-demo check worth doing separately: scripts/seed_demo_firm.py never mentions compliance (grep finds nothing), so confirm the GST and Income-tax trackers, which read compliance_calendar, are not empty or contradicting /deadlines on the demo firm._ — _Sources:_ docs/audits/2026-07-02-executive-product-audit.md R3.13 (lines 273-277), R3.13e 'Scope correction' (lines 3639-3680), Final audit item 1 (lines 4498-4504); practice_management-16 Retire the second compliance store so every screen shows the same deadlines — _Refs:_ R3.13, R3.13e, rm/practice_management-16, decision 11
- **POST-B-327** · `other` · normal · weeks — **Set portal upload policy for client document uploads.** Decision question (roadmap decision 9): what policy governs a portal client uploading against a document request: file-type allowlist, size cap, per-client quota, whether to scan (scanning service cost unsure) and what lands in a CA's audit file? Today a portal client can see a request but cannot upload: routers/portal_self.py says 'Uploading here is not available yet', portal_data.py says it is deliberately not built, no file input exists under apps/web/app/portal, and migration 005's storage policies admit no portal principal (staff upload on the client's behalf exists). Recommendation: an API-mediated endpoint (no direct Storage write for a portal principal) reusing core/uploads.py's bounded read, magic-byte check and our own content type, a per-client quota and no antivirus at first (named, as in the existing upload rules), creating the client_documents row and flipping the request to fulfilled with fulfilled_at; the installed PWA can capture from the camera. Weeks of work, and the public claim 'a client cannot upload' (marketing claims ledger) must change with it. Verify: a portal token uploads a PDF against a request, a second client's token gets 404, an oversize or disallowed type is refused in plain words. — _Sources:_ practice_management-01 Let portal clients upload documents against a request — _Refs:_ rm/practice_management-01, rm/security_privacy-20, decision 9
- **POST-B-328** · `other` · low · months — **Cross-client batch compliance cockpit (generate, validate, mark filed, ARN).** The July audit's top CA-scale unlock (R3.2, size XL). What exists: bulk Mark Filed with ONE filed date on /deadlines, firm-wide obligation generation (compliance_obligation_service.generate_due) and a bulk GSTR-2B upload that routes files by GSTIN. What does not: batch generation of real GSTR-1/GSTR-3B/TDS/MCA records across N clients (gst_workspace, tds_workspace and mca_workspace each need one client_id), ARN capture per batch, and a firm-wide cockpit screen. R3.2 was explicitly deferred as a separate product initiative. DECISION QUESTION: start it? Recommendation: not before the single-client workspaces are verified by a practising CA; any batch filing must keep the per-return explicit confirmation click (never auto-submit). — _Sources:_ docs/audits/2026-07-02-executive-product-audit.md R3.2 (line 261), 'R3.13 scoping' (lines 2610-2621), CA productivity (lines 150-156) — _Refs:_ R3.2
- **POST-B-329** · `other` · low · days — **Decide on document vault storage tiers, content-hash dedupe and staleness.** The 2026-06 audit marked Documents Partial against Doc 5: no Tier-1/Tier-2 tiering, no SHA-256 dedupe of uploaded files (grep finds no hashing in routers/documents.py; file hashing exists only in ais_service and banking_service) and no generated-document staleness marker; versioning by label exists. Low priority. Decision question: is duplicate-upload detection (a per-client content hash that WARNS, never blocks) wanted before anything is built? Recommendation: build only the warning-style hash check if wanted; tiering and staleness wait for a real need. — _Sources:_ docs/PRACTICESYNC_AUDIT_v1.md section 1 (Documents Partial)
- **POST-B-330** · `other` · low · days — **Decide what 'Total' means on the /practice/compliance dashboard.** Decision question: should the dashboard 'Total' mean lifetime or a window? compliance_obligation_service.dashboard() reads _records_for(firm_id) = compliance_record_service.list_records(firm_id) with no due-date window (paged by fetch_all, but proportional to the firm's whole history), and aggregate_dashboard()'s 'Total' is lifetime including Filed/Completed, pinned by its own test. calendar() and escalate() were bounded in R3.5b; dashboard() was left because bounding changes a displayed business number. Recommendation: current financial year plus all open items regardless of date, then bound the read the same way. — _Sources:_ docs/audits/2026-07-02-executive-product-audit.md R3.5b (lines 4223-4237) and Final audit decision item 4 (lines 4514-4523) — _Refs:_ R3.5b
- **POST-B-331** · `other` · low · weeks — **Decide whether department-scoped knowledge articles become a real boundary.** Decision question: does a practice want department-private SOPs (Payroll or Audit only)? Batch 6 shipped scope=department as an organisational tag visible to all firm staff ('not a hard access boundary in v1'); knowledge_articles.department is free text and the firm list simply filters scope IN (firm, department). A real boundary needs a department model (the product only has payroll_employees.department) plus RLS and API enforcement. Deliberately not built. Recommendation: leave as a tag until a practice asks for private SOPs, and say so in the screen copy. — _Sources:_ docs/BATCH_6_COMPLETION_REPORT.md section 8; docs/BATCH_6_DESIGN_REVIEW.md section 1 'Scope semantics' — _Refs:_ Batch6-department-scope
- **POST-B-332** · `other` · low · days — **Link signed engagement letter to its fee engagement.** `engagements` (the signed letter, migration 115) and `fee_engagements` (billing, migrations 014/108) are correlated only by client_id and timing, with no FK and no divergence check; convert_lead (routers/lifecycle.py ~1066) copies the letter's fee once, after which an edit to fee_engagements.fee_paise silently diverges from the legal record engagements.fee_amount_paise. docs/ENGAGEMENT_ARCHITECTURE_AUDIT.md recommends four additive changes, none of which exist (grep finds no engagement_letter_id): (1) fee_engagements.engagement_letter_id UUID REFERENCES engagements(id) replacing the notes string 'Auto-created from signed engagement EL-YYYY-NNNN'; (2) clearer labels, since the /engagements page shows letters while engagement_repo points at fee_engagements; (3) a reconciliation check flagging fee divergence; (4) a v_client_engagements view. Decision question for the owner: is fee divergence something to flag, with what tolerance, and are the label names right? Recommendation: do (1) and (2) first (cheap, additive) and flag any difference as a notice, never a block, rather than inventing a tolerance. — _Sources:_ docs/ENGAGEMENT_ARCHITECTURE_AUDIT.md, 'Recommended future consolidation plan' items 1-4 and 'Risks'
- **POST-B-333** · `other` · low · days — **Reminders module is an unreached stub: wire it or delete it.** Decision question: build dispatch for reminders or delete the module? /api/reminders (routers/reminders.py: list, create, mark-sent), services/reminder_service.py ('infrastructure only - Do NOT send actual WhatsApp or email') and repositories/reminders_repository.py have no UI caller (lib/api/index.ts defines `reminders` with no screen calling it) and the three endpoints sit in the unreached-endpoint ratchet ('/api/reminders': 3 in tests/test_every_mounted_endpoint_has_a_way_in.py). A reminder row can be created and manually marked sent but nothing is ever dispatched. Options: (a) build dispatch through practice_mail_service.deliver (the one switch-gated door for practice mail) plus a screen, or (b) delete the three routes, repo and table so the module stops looking actionable. Recommendation: (b) unless chasing reminders will be designed soon, because a stub that looks actionable and sends nothing is the worse state; dropping the table is a production migration needing the schema-drift fixture refresh. — _Sources:_ docs/MANUAL_TESTING_ROADMAP.md sections 7 and 8 ('Reminders module is a stub')
- **POST-B-334** · `other` · low · days — **Wire a screen for the 9 /api/task-recurring endpoints or retire them.** Decision question: build a recurring-task screen (compliance reminders, monthly close tasks) or delete the unreachable routes? Nine working endpoints, the assignment-rule engine (assignment_rules) and recurring_task_service have no frontend caller (nothing under apps/web references /api/task-recurring, re-checked at HEAD) and only the nightly job (jobs/recurring_task_job.py) generates tasks, so a CA cannot create or edit a recurring task config without the database; tests/test_every_mounted_endpoint_has_a_way_in.py still budgets '/api/task-recurring': 9 unreachable. Recommendation: build a thin list/create/edit screen, because the engine and nightly job already work and deleting them discards the assignment-rule engine; delete only if recurring tasks are not meant to be a product feature. — _Sources:_ docs/audits/2026-08-02-bank-module-quickbooks-gap-audit.md 'Recorded - this router has no frontend caller at all' (~line 767) — _Refs:_ task-recurring unreachable
